## How to use this master document

This is the **one current product specification** that future agents should reopen at the beginning of substantial ATLAS work. It deliberately combines the enduring requirements from the uploaded documents while retiring stale model-centric defaults, old Astra/GPT-6.1 names, obsolete milestones, speculative capabilities presented as facts, unsafe automatic authority, and unsupported quantitative equivalence claims. It does not erase the historical source files: archive those read-only with hashes and preserve their provenance so a future architect can recover a design detail without reactivating an obsolete instruction.

**Normative vocabulary:** **MUST** is a product requirement subject to host and owner authorization; **SHOULD** is a preferred design; **MAY** is an option; **PROPOSED** denotes a desired capability or architecture not yet implemented; **VERIFIED** requires a reproducible execution receipt and, where mandated, independent review; **RESTRICTED** means blocked by safety, privacy or qualification gates; **HYPOTHESIS** denotes a claim that must be experimentally tested. **Accepted**, **provisional**, **rejected**, **blocked**, **shadow**, **reference**, **native**, **deterministic**, and **source-verified** are distinct terms.

**Authority order (highest first):** (1) owner instructions, applicable law and system-level safety restrictions; (2) trusted host enforcement, owner permissions and protected evaluation integrity; (3) this current canonical master plan for product intent; (4) independently accepted frozen commits and review evidence for what actually works; (5) `docs/ai/ATLAS_HANDOFF.md` for the current bounded assignment and single-writer state; (6) repository architecture, requirements, schema migrations and tests; (7) archived historical specifications as design sources only; (8) generated model output as untrusted proposals. If an apparently higher-priority document conflicts with security or a newer explicit owner instruction, never silently apply it: record the conflict and use the authorized resolution.

**Persistent boot contract:** A future external developer or ATLAS internal planning agent should read this entire canonical file (or navigate every chapter via verified indexed sections), review its fingerprint and updated date, load the current handoff, independently inspect source and Git, identify the assigned phase, then select only the smallest relevant working set for execution. Do not inject this full book into every model context. Keep an indexed `README`, stable section IDs, digest, revision history, and a small pinned doctrine card linked back to the complete source. Neither an index nor an abbreviated prompt replaces the full document when governance is at issue.

# PART I — IDENTITY, MISSION, PRINCIPLES AND PRODUCT BOUNDARIES

## 1. Identity and ecosystem

**Company umbrella:** PLATZ Technologies (also referred to informally as PLATZ Tech). Brand expansion: **Platform for Logic, Art, Tools & Zero Limits**. Brand slogan: **One Place. Zero Limits.** The visual `PZ` symbol belongs to the PLATZ family. These are product/brand intentions, not proof of trademark clearance or final company registration.

**Product A — ATLAS AI:** Adaptive Tiered Learning, Analysis & Search. Owner-controlled local-first AI, project-aware research, knowledge, procedural reasoning, code, creative work, task coordination, model creation and qualification, computer automation after isolation, and controlled self-improvement. The name ATLAS refers to the intelligence product and its own original models; general Qwen/Ollama/GPT/Gemini integration must never be rebranded as an original ATLAS model.

**Product B — PLATZ Engine:** A separate creation platform, with **PLATZ Studio**, **PLATZ Core**, **PLATZ Runtime**, and **PLATZ Hub**. The authoritative engine source, implementation plan and acceptance are in **`PLATZ_MASTER.md`** and **`PLATZ_WORKING.md`**, not this ATLAS document. FORGE is a read-only historical reference. ATLAS and PLATZ may later communicate through explicit versioned owner-authorized interfaces without merging repositories or weakening either product's security. Never change PLATZ or FORGE merely because an ATLAS milestone mentions game development.

## 2. The one-sentence product promise

A person states a goal, ATLAS understands the permitted project and relevant evidence, selects or trains appropriate **original** specialists, retrieves tested facts and procedures, plans safe steps, produces a useful answer or artifact, independently verifies it, and retains permitted lessons so future work becomes more reliable and less dependent on outside AI.

ATLAS is not fundamentally a chatbot, a retrieval-augmented wrapper, an IDE add-on, a model launcher, a benchmark dashboard, a macro recorder, or a very large fixed language model. Those can be components of the larger system. The product's differentiation is **measured native-original intelligence + file-first knowledge and procedures + verifiable useful action + controlled learning over time**.

## 3. Ten permanent principles

 1. **Original-model first.** Runtime learned decisions should ultimately come from locally trained ATLAS-original weights; optional pretrained teachers, references and temporary bootstrap services must remain explicitly labeled and removable without destroying native operation.

 2. **Files carry truth and procedures.** Facts, exact versions, source records, formulas, recipes, prior solutions, tests, user memory and task history live in external inspectable storage, not as assumed facts in model weights.

 3. **Trusted host above every model.** No model, Master, agent, dataset, knowledge file, website, tool response or prompt can grant its own filesystem/network/execution privileges or promote its own checkpoint.

 4. **Useful completion above classifier scores.** Evaluate completed tasks, correct abstentions, wrong deliveries, resource cost, robustness, owner effort and real results; classify a model's narrow learning separately from broad intelligence.

 5. **Independent evidence over assertion.** Author-run tests and model confidence are not acceptance; use frozen revisions, strong baselines, independently authored adversarial tests and protected custody.

 6. **Specialization only when beneficial.** Model count, parameter count, names, roles, councils and multiple agents are not benefits unless they demonstrably improve task outcome or efficiency.

 7. **Local, private, reversible by default.** No public SaaS requirement, hidden data exfiltration or unapproved network action. Personal memory is segregated; generated work is rollback-capable; secrets are inaccessible to training by default.

 8. **Real engineering over fake completeness.** Build functioning vertical slices and exercise real UI and project workflows; no simulated training charts, placeholder agents, test labels copied from rules, or unexecuted “pass” claims.

 9. **Continuous growth without authority drift.** Authorized idle-time learning, knowledge refresh and self-development are future objectives, not blanket permissions; safety control must stay outside self-modifying code.

10. **Simple ownership.** The owner should express goals and approvals, not coordinate thousands of micro-tasks. ATLAS should handle justified decomposition, execution and evidence; external Sol and Argon should presently coordinate through durable files and short owner-relayed prompts.

## 4. Primary target hardware and delivery

Main target is a **Windows 11 desktop** with an **NVIDIA RTX 5080 16 GB VRAM**, **96 GB RAM**, and **4 TB NVMe SSD**. Hardware details must be autodetected rather than assumed. Initial deployment is desktop or an optional authenticated **owner-controlled private server**. No requirement to ship mobile apps or a public multi-tenant cloud service now. Offline inference, evidence lookup and authorized local training must remain useful without an internet connection. Windows .NET/WPF is the actual ATLAS Next implementation; do not rewrite it in React/Tauri/WinUI merely because an archived design preferred another shell. Optional WSL2/Python/CUDA services can be added when independently measured and securely integrated.

GPU resource accounting must include model weights, optimizer moments, KV caches, activation memory, Windows graphics headroom, concurrent workers and fragmentation. Large model goals (30M, 100M, 300M, \~1B, and possible 3B–12B coordinator research) are **resource and data-dependent research steps**, not mandatory minimum model sizes. Efficient classifiers below 1B are allowed and sometimes optimal. There is no demonstrated universal equivalence that “1B equals 80B” or that a 12B hierarchy equals a trillion-parameter system. Evaluate parity only on precisely defined workloads under comparable tools, latency and hardware budgets.

## 5. Who uses ATLAS and for what

Owners, programmers, game developers, researchers, writers, students, technical designers, creative artists, small-team builders and optionally authorized private-server users should have coherent paths from intention to tested result. Domains include project analysis, programming, games, document research, technical investigation, knowledge organization, model experiments, personal assistance by opt-in consent, digital creativity, 2D/3D work, and safe computer workflows. High-stakes medical, legal and financial assistance is information support with strict provenance and human decision responsibility, not unchecked autonomous action.

## 6. The intended everyday experience

Owner opens ATLAS and sees a clear home screen rather than a model zoo: **Ask ATLAS**, **Improve a Model**, **Work on a Project**, **Research**, **Create**, and **View Evidence**. For ordinary tasks, ATLAS selects a qualified native policy, returns a traceable result, and asks only necessary permission questions. An optional pretrained reference mode is visibly separate and never silently substitutes for original intelligence. Advanced users can inspect the DAG, model lineage, training plots, source revisions, test receipts and computing budgets. A user can pause, cancel, revert and stop safely. The app shows what it actually did, not what it intended to do.

# PART II — CURRENT TRUTH, HISTORICAL CONTINUITY AND IMPLEMENTATION STATUS

## 8. Original ATLAS legacy system and preservation

The original project at `C:\ATLAS\Entire ATLAS` implemented a broader Windows WebView2/React + Python/Ollama experience, including local chat, installed model registry, encrypted opt-in personal memory features, knowledge broker, model comparisons, training and checkpoint UI, Master/Task Graph, bounded Developer/Arena workflows, experimental local native policies, adapted pretrained models, experiment ledgers and tested narrow code repairs using local Qwen. It was **more functionally broad** than Next, but retained independently identified Windows path and untrusted execution containment defects in its last cited security audits. Its native specialists mostly learned narrow policy decisions and often tied deterministic rules; general ATLAS-original language reasoning was not established. Preserve this repository as read-only historical evidence and a design/test source. Never restore its code-execution authority simply to regain features.

**Continuity:** The product vision may selectively recover old user workflows, tests, UI concepts and trusted modules through reviewed porting; the new source tree, host boundaries and acceptance authority remain ATLAS Next. Do not claim “original ATLAS was better” or “Next is more intelligent” without specifying the axis: original had broader pretrained-powered functionality; Next has stronger bounded provenance and restricted execution for its supported tasks.

## 9. Anti-drift verification question

At each batch, the owner report MUST answer: **What previously impossible real task can an ATLAS-original model now complete correctly, without pretrained inference, and how does its independently verified success compare with deterministic, strong linear and prior-model alternatives?** If no such improvement exists, say **no native intelligence advantage established** and document what data, architecture, language capacity, tool limitation or evaluator coverage prevented it. UI and parser improvements may still matter but must not be counted as native intelligence gains.

# PART III — THE FIVE TIERS, MASTER INTELLIGENCES AND SPECIALIST ECOSYSTEM

## 10. Five-tier architecture — distinguish physical authority from neural roles

The historical five-tier vision is preserved as an aspirational **role hierarchy**, not five separately deployed billion-parameter fleets. **Tier 5: trusted file, knowledge and verifier substrate**—the source of in-scope verified evidence and reusable procedures; **Tier 4: small specialized workers**—retrieve, classify, plan narrow actions, write, parse, test and evaluate; **Tier 3: domain coordinators**—manage a substantive domain with domain-specific verification; **Tier 2: executive clusters**—coordinate families of domains; **Tier 1: chief/Primary Master**—turn owner goals into an auditable plan and synthesize final outcomes. The **trusted operating-system host and owner permissions are above all tiers**: files and models do not have administrative or security authority.

The earlier role catalog proposes a Chief Master, 20 executive clusters, 100 domain Masters, and over 2,000 specialist role slots organized into 25 families. **Role catalog entries are configuration records and future curricula**, not thousands of already existing model files. The software must lazily instantiate only needed roles using proven native checkpoints, shared models with distinct verified adapters, or deterministic tools. Separate weights require independent training/qualification. Do not consume gigabytes of storage or GPU time creating empty placeholder models. Budget concurrency by measured benefit. The owner can inspect which roles are working, proposed, unavailable, blocked or retired.

## 11. Three indispensable Master competencies

The three functional Masters proposed for the first meaningful native coordination architecture are **Primary Coordinator**, **Engineering/Execution Master**, and **Research/Verification Master**. They may be implemented as original independent models, modular heads, or shared backbone with separately qualified policies—whichever performs best under data and hardware constraints. “Three Masters” means three genuinely distinguishable **decision competencies**, not three labels on one hardcoded router.

**Primary Coordinator:** interprets allowed owner goals, resolves scope ambiguity, inspects cost and capabilities, converts tasks into typed DAG nodes, identifies evidence needs, routes to qualified specialists, prioritizes independent work, controls resource budget via trusted host, and either synthesizes a verified answer or explicitly abstains. It learns *which actions yield verified useful outcomes*, not just task-type labels.

**Research/Verification Master:** selects what evidence to seek, how to rank source reliability for the claim, when information is insufficient, which independent checks to perform, how to investigate contradiction, whether to refresh stale sources, and when to release a conclusion. Must learn only within evaluator-controlled bounds; trusted host verifies exact source and promotion state. It should call separate Translation, File, Web or Mathematics specialists when qualified. It must never fabricate citations.

**Engineering/Execution Master:** maps a real project and dependencies, chooses source files, proposes implementation and test plans, selects language specialists and safe tools, analyzes diagnostics, identifies minimal patches, requests independent reviews, and learns from observed tests and failures. It has no independent right to modify the owner’s project or run arbitrary generated code. Specialized C#/C++/Unreal/Unity/UEFN/Python/Rust/JS/Blender policies should be project-aware.

**Stage definitions:** (a) structural prototype—a role interface with deterministic fallback; (b) learned narrow policy—training from original initialization with measured decisions; (c) useful qualified role—independent advantage or justified efficiency at safety parity; (d) trusted orchestration—only after host authorization and end-to-end independent certification. The current 37-parameter Primary is a *stage-b* narrow gate, not a human-equivalent primary reasoner.

## 12. Role taxonomy and activation

Every catalog role MUST have: stable role ID; purpose; supported inputs/outputs; tool and source permissions; task and budget limits; model/checkpoint origin; relevant training/evaluation family; verified metrics; approved fallback; known unsupported cases; last independent review; privilege ceiling. Use a role registry and typed contracts instead of brittle prompt-specific ad hoc agents. Require a competency demonstration before marking any role available. A role entry may point to a deterministic tool, an original checkpoint, a pretrained optional reference or unavailable work. Never label an optional reference as native.

Initial high-value small specialists: file scout; source symbol navigator; project inspector; semantic/retrieval ranker; procedural memory matcher; factual evidence critic; research plan selector; source credibility assessor; translation; C#/Python code understanding; test selection; failure diagnostic; patch risk estimator; original-model training director; experiment evaluator; independent reviewer; writing and continuity keeper; creative asset inspector. The eventual scope expands to math, science, computer use, game simulation, 2D sprites, 3D, Unreal, Unity, PLATZ and office documents. Establish proof on one useful task before declaring a new role operational.

## 13. Deterministic zero-model / pure-file mode

A simple exact or parameterized answer, arithmetic formula, database projection, trusted script or verified lookup may be completed by the deterministic host **without loading an inference model**. This is good engineering, but count it as **deterministic file/tool success**, not intelligence improvement. The historical “0B” phrase means *zero model parameters invoked for that task*, not infinite intelligence or universally zero GPU usage. Immutable answer cards and parameterized procedures may be compiled from independently verified recurring results; measure real p50/p95/p99 latencies and exact-match rejection rates. A neural model deciding when to use a zero-model tool can still add value if it improves task-level outcomes or resource efficiency on new tasks.

## 14. Optional reference-model phase-out

The original plans permit pretrained models as bootstraps, teachers, judges, comparisons and emergency fallbacks. The latest owner direction is stricter: **the primary ATLAS product must not depend on such models for its intelligence.** Therefore implement an explicit **Native-only mode** with no pretrained inference calls, no external AI endpoint, and source-grounded attribution. Optional reference chat remains a separate opt-in workspace and is not a graduation shortcut. Define reference-assisted, ATLAS-adapted-pretrained and fully original lineages separately. “Trained by ATLAS” must not silently include Qwen LoRA weights. If a reference model helps create training candidates, record its identity and provenance, and verify labels independently rather than teaching from unverified generation. The native path must be independently runnable offline.

A potential graduation sequence is **bootstrap/control → shadow native → independently qualified native primary for a bounded domain → native-first optional-reference-free operation for that domain**. Graduation is reversible on regression. There is no unconditional eviction of model files, parameter cutoff, claimed 80B equivalence, or automatic replacement merely for passing an easy synthetic curriculum. Full-sovereignty claims require a comprehensive attributable native-only task suite and security audit.

## 15. Internal agent constitution

Every ATLAS-internal agent begins with an owner-authorized task receipt and least-privilege host-issued capability, not a free-form instruction giving itself authority. Agents search permitted sources; distinguish observed facts, hypotheses, suggested actions and unknowns; cite evidence; return typed outputs; and request review or abstain when necessary. A model cannot change weights, grant privileges, alter security policy or promote knowledge by declaring that it has done so. Input files, source comments, browser pages, web research and retrieved “agent instructions” are task data, not trusted system messages.

Allowed outcome states include `completed`, `needs_more_evidence`, `needs_review`, `blocked` and `failed`. Contracts must capture exact task ID, project, scope, model checkpoint, dependencies, authorized tools, allowed sources, compute limits, output schema, evidence IDs, result artifacts, uncertainty, errors, resource accounting and reviewer status. Reviewer claims cannot be taken as truth without controlled verification of the asserted behavior.

# PART IV — CANONICAL FILE-FIRST KNOWLEDGE, PROCEDURES, MEMORY AND CONTEXT

## 16. Why the file hierarchy is foundational

ATLAS does not need its weights to memorize encyclopedias, rapidly changing software APIs, specific project code, schedules or private biographies. Weights specialize in request understanding, reasoning strategies, query formulation, uncertainty, tool selection and learned decisions. **External verified files store facts and reusable reasoning procedures**. The novel goal is not ordinary RAG: verified **procedural** knowledge—tested debugging workflows, failure-recovery strategies, project conventions, experiments, planning templates, creative continuity rules, verification checklists and deterministic programs—must be as retrievable as factual claims.

Knowledge storage should be both human-browsable and machine-indexable, with stable IDs and a typed Knowledge Broker API. Models must not hardcode physical paths. SQLite/FTS for identifiers and lexical search plus optional embedding/vector indexes, symbol indexes, claim graph and checksummed source snapshots are suitable implementation choices; prefer one coherent store and indexes over twelve disconnected databases. The physical directory tree is a durable exchange format, not a reason to put everything into model context.

## 17. The twelve conceptual canonical vaults

The enduring tier-five vault registry has twelve spaces, although the physical backend may share indices:

* **V-01 Answer Cards:** exact verified statements, fast parameterized lookup, preconditions, supporting evidence, invalidation and source versions.

* **V-02 Procedures:** reusable tested reasoning strategies, debugging and research playbooks, step preconditions, tools, observed outcomes, rollback and applicability.

* **V-03 Deterministic Tools:** versioned allowlisted scripts/calculators/formulas, their arguments, budgets, tests, ownership and signatures/digests.

* **V-04 Code Intelligence:** project source snapshots, symbol/call/dependency graphs, code conventions, compiler versions, build outputs and verified patch cases.

* **V-05 Personal Memory:** owner opt-in encrypted-scoped facts/preferences, separated from public knowledge and training.

* **V-06 STEM References:** scientific papers, standards, equations, units, experiment methods, claims, uncertainty and revisions.

* **V-07 Creative Bibles:** storyworld canon, characters, narrative continuity, design guides, artistic consistency and corrections.

* **V-08 Game Assets:** sprite/texture/rig/mesh/world/animation/gameplay metadata, files and verified format constraints.

* **V-09 Verification Oracles:** independently owned benchmarks, hidden tests, frozen rubrics, evaluator lineage and access controls. **Protected material must not be made readable by candidate agents.**

* **V-10 Episodic Ledger:** concise permitted task trajectories, decisions, receipts, failures, timing and signed/hashed history without indiscriminate private retention.

* **V-11 Training Corpora:** explicitly eligible datasets, licenses, lineage, family separation, deduplication, training/eval partitions, abstentions and corrections.

* **V-12 Model Lineage:** original and reference checkpoints, tokenizer, model architecture, parent weights, initialization, optimizer state, configuration, hashes, experiment results and qualification.

V-01 through V-12 are long-term namespace obligations, not a demand to build twelve UI screens or databases immediately. Each vault needs a clear minimum working acceptance test before it is called live.

## 18. Example file-system layout

```
ATLAS_HOME/
  README_KNOWLEDGE.md
  catalog/root_manifest.json
  catalog/domain_index.json
  catalog/knowledge.sqlite
  catalog/source_registry.sqlite
  indexes/{fts,symbols,graph,vectors}/
  knowledge/public/{science,technology,software,arts,general}/
  knowledge/sources/<source_id>/{manifest.json,original,extracted,translations}/
  knowledge/claims/<domain>/<claim_id>.json
  knowledge/procedures/<domain>/<procedure_id>/{procedure.md,metadata.json,examples.jsonl,tests}/
  knowledge/answer_cards/<domain>/<id>.json
  knowledge/candidate_research/
  knowledge/contradictions/
  knowledge/retracted/
  projects/<project_id>/{manifest.json,sources,symbols,tests,task_history,artifacts}/
  users/<owner_id>/{private_memory,permissions,retention}/
  models/{original,adapted_reference,external_reference,checkpoints,registry}/
  training/{eligible,quarantine,experiments,validation,public_benchmarks}/
  evaluator_custody/    # separately protected, not readable by candidate workers
  tasks/<task_id>/{request,plan,evidence,actions,receipts,review,result}/
  procedures_library/
  logs/{audits,resource,errors}/
  quarantine/
  exports/

```

Paths are an architectural example; don't migrate the live application into this exact tree without a migration plan and review. Project IDs, relative canonical paths, immutable snapshots and trusted scoped registry IDs matter more than decorative folder names.

## 19. Source/claim/procedure lifecycle

Every source has a stable `source_id`, original location or URL, access scope, acquisition time, MIME/version/license, bytes digest, parse method, snapshot path, trust state, freshness schedule and related project. Every atomic claim has `claim_id`, normalized subject/predicate/value or proposition, exact source evidence offsets, verification method, timestamp, confidence basis, applicability, contradiction set, supersession link and permission scope. Every procedure has `procedure_id`, goal, prerequisites, steps/tool contracts, expected output, executable validation, past success/failures, versions and known hazards. Preserve the original bytes; summaries never replace underlying evidence.

Trust states should include `raw`, `candidate`, `provisional`, `verified_for_scope`, `contradicted`, `stale`, `retracted`, `rejected`, `quarantined`. Verification is **claim-specific**, not a blanket badge on a website, teacher model or document. Correcting one claim must not overwrite the historical version. Expiry and contradiction checks may downgrade a state and trigger targeted recheck. Data deletion must be supported without leaving reconstructable private remnants in model-training caches.

## 20. Retrieval and context management

The Knowledge Broker performs: scope/permission filter → exact ID and symbol search → FTS/lexical → optional semantic search → relevance reranking → provenance/validity filtering → compact contextual evidence pack → model or deterministic action → independent verification. A ranking score is not a truth score. Avoid retrieval loops and duplicate materials. Summaries can be layered as **navigation → section → evidence → synthesis**, always permitting recovery of the exact original span. Retrieve project-specific procedures before a broad web guess. Tokens and context limits should follow model and resource capacity rather than arbitrary tiny caps; truncation must be visible and fail safely.

## 21. Neural-to-file learning flywheel

A model-solving episode may improve the system in at least four distinct ways: **(1) new verified factual knowledge, (2) new reusable procedure, (3) new deterministic answer card/tool, (4) new eligible model-training outcome**. Record these separately. A verified procedure can improve future tasks without changing neural weights; report it as knowledge-based improvement. New neural training needs independent, permitted data and a demonstrated downstream advantage. Never promote a website result or teacher answer into trusted facts merely because it sounds plausible or was repeated.

## 22. Private vs personal vs project memory

**Private Playground:** transient chat/reasoning mode, no ATLAS application-side content retention, no training, no public logs. Verify persistence boundaries and be candid about third-party local services and OS-level artifacts outside ATLAS's control. **Personal ATLAS:** separately authorized, encrypted memory with explicit category and retention permissions; inspect/correct/export/delete controls. **Project memory:** scoped project-specific facts, files, decisions and procedures with explicit owner rights. **Public technical knowledge:** no personal data by default. **Experiment memory:** model/data provenance without unrestricted copies of user content. Personal data must never silently flow from private/personal scopes to public code, shared prompts, training or external models. Consent to memory is not consent to train on it.

The historical personal vault has **19 category slots** including identity preferences, hardware, active projects, AI goals, creative projects, gamedev workflows, learning, routines, travel, purchases, family and other sensitive categories. Preserve it as a proposed schema/catalog only; do not import private biographies, infer private traits, or create these categories' content without owner opt-in. Protect backups, search indexes, crash dumps and export logs as rigorously as the primary store.

# PART V — TASK GRAPH, RESEARCH, TOOLS AND INDEPENDENT REASONING

## 23. The owner-to-outcome task lifecycle

A qualified ATLAS task begins with an explicit owner objective, scope and permitted capability. The system resolves whether it has enough information. A small native language model or deterministic interface turns the request into a typed representation, not a privileged command. The Primary constructs a task graph and resource budget. File, Project and optionally Web scouts collect independent evidence concurrently where beneficial. The Evidence Broker assembles a compact source and procedure packet. A relevant specialist proposes a solution or artifact. A separate reviewer checks claims and tests. The trusted host enforces scope, source integrity and action approvals. The Master synthesizes verified results, requests more evidence or returns a clear blocked state. The outcome is logged with provenance; verified facts/procedures are eligible for staging. The loop may continue through more tasks until the owner-defined budget, success condition, stop action or safety gate ends it.

**Typical typed nodes:** `interpret_goal`, `clarify_scope`, `inspect_project`, `retrieve_knowledge`, `research_missing_information`, `translate_evidence`, `choose_procedure`, `draft_solution`, `run_approved_test`, `independent_review`, `revise_candidate`, `verify_claims`, `summarize_for_owner`, `propose_knowledge_promotion`. Each node has output IDs and dependency edges. A model’s proposal to execute a node is not itself permission to execute it.

## 24. Bounded parallelism and resource arbitration

Parallel scouts are an important feature of the vision: multiple lightweight independent searches, file inspections, translations and project planners can run concurrently when the hardware budget supports it. Do not force multiple GPU-resident neural models into 16 GB VRAM; serialize heavyweight inference and overlap CPU/file/network work. The scheduler uses task priorities, cancellation, deadlines, GPU/RAM/disk budgets, exponential backoff with hard bounds, isolation scopes and independent-node DAG semantics. Compare the parallel path against a serial baseline on completed-work latency, quality and total resource cost. There is no reward for parallelism that increases cost and produces the same answer.

## 25. Local research before web research

ATLAS first asks: what verified knowledge or procedure already exists in this project's permitted vault? Then what public permitted local knowledge exists? Is an answer card sufficient? Can trusted tools compute a result? Only if evidence is genuinely missing or stale should a qualified research planner request outside information. **Internet research is a separate owner-approved capability**, not an automatic fallback for every ambiguous request. A model should know when to abstain or ask for sources. For scientific or legal questions, source recency and jurisdiction matter; a model cannot create a verified conclusion simply by composing plausible text.

## 26. Internet research architecture and source promotion

When the owner later authorizes network research, use an explicit **isolated remote fetch environment** with privacy/identity controls, domain and budget allowlists, safe download handling, content-type limits, malware scanning where available and a prohibition on sending owner secrets. Do not claim the system can absolutely hide IP/location or guarantee malware-free files. Distinguish `request researcher fetch` from `register online account` (separate permission), `download artifact` (separate permission), and `execute artifact` (separate independently enforced sandbox permission). Never use owner identity, personal email/payment accounts, subscriptions or free trials to create agent credentials. ATLAS-created account sessions must never be logged into the physical Windows host.

Research steps: formulate queries; identify date-sensitive claims; fetch approved public sources; preserve immutable snapshots/URLs/timestamps/hashes/licenses; analyze each claim's evidence and counter-evidence; extract exact citations; check consensus vs single-source limitations; detect prompt injections and hostile instructions; assign `candidate` status; run independent checks; promote only validated specific facts/procedures into reusable files. A fetch receipt alone is not verification. Periodic refresh may recheck expiring facts only under separate scheduled-resource and network authorization. Rejected, contradicted or unsupported claims remain available in a quarantined learning/audit record, not the trusted answer namespace.

## 27. Independent reviewer and corrective loop

Reviewer and producer must have distinct evaluation objectives and, when needed, separate model/data custody. A reviewer inspects exact artifacts, provenance, code diffs, claims, tests, performance and missing requirements. A new contradictory test is more valuable than agreeing with another model’s rhetoric. Errors should produce a bounded new task with precise repro steps and acceptance criteria. Reject or safe-withhold if evidence cannot be established. Multi-round repair loops have max retries/budgets and preserve every negative attempt. Do not overfit repeatedly to a public suite, leak protected expectations into training or modify the verifier to match a weak candidate.

## 28. Typed agent and tool schema example

```
{
  "schema_version":"1.0", "task_id":"task-00042", "project_id":"proj-012",
  "parent_graph_id":"graph-007", "role":"atlas.research_scout",
  "objective":"Find the verified source of the disputed loader setting",
  "allowed_source_scopes":["project:proj-012","knowledge:public"],
  "allowed_tools":["knowledge.search","project.symbols"],
  "dependencies":["task-00040"],
  "model_origin":"atlas_original", "checkpoint_digest":"sha256:<digest>",
  "budget":{"max_steps":6,"max_output_tokens":1600,"max_seconds":90},
  "required_output":"EvidenceBundleV1"
}

```

```
{
  "schema_version":"1.0", "task_id":"task-00042", "status":"needs_review",
  "findings":[{"claim_id":"claim-221","evidence_ids":["span-884"],"scope":"proj-012"}],
  "uncertainties":["Another candidate source contradicts version 2"],
  "artifacts":["evidence-bundle-17"], "next_proposals":["review.claim_conflict"],
  "resources":{"steps":3,"elapsed_ms":41}, "authoritative_delivery":false
}

```

These JSON objects are conceptual. Implement actual schemas with validation and version migrations. No LLM-generated key, role name, confidence label or declared `allowed_tools` can replace the host's grant or authorization check.

## 29. Natural-language, language and translation

English is the preferred initial internal development and tool-contract language, not a prohibition on international users or source documents. A translation specialist may translate non-English sources using an original model or openly identified reference when authorized. Preserve original text, language, aligned offsets, translator identity, uncertainty and source citations; verify critical figures/names independently. Do not translate identifiers or code keywords gratuitously. Natural language is a learned capability that needs genuine from-scratch tokenizer/model evidence. Early structured intent recognition is not evidence of fluent language generation.

# PART VI — CODING, CREATION AND UNIVERSAL PROJECT INTELLIGENCE

## 30. Project awareness and universal file intelligence

Projects are first-class scoped workspaces. An authorized project import should support directories and archives only after safe scope/path and resource checks. The Project Inspector identifies languages, frameworks, versions, build systems, modules, dependencies, symbols, tests, assets, goals, conventions, pending changes and prior failures. It should retrieve only relevant slices rather than dump a repository into context. The source graph needs stable file IDs, hashes, version-specific parser results, commit/worktree identity, unresolved dependencies and linkable evidence. Import never grants code-execution permission.

Long-term support includes plaintext/Markdown, PDF, Word, Excel, presentations, JSON, YAML, XML, logs, databases, source languages, ZIP/tar archives, images, video, audio, 2D/3D scene formats, Blender files, Unreal/Unity assets and PLATZ formats. Each format has a recorded trust boundary, parser constraints, CPU/memory limits and an independently tested vertical slice. Archives require traversal/symlink/case alias/ADS/bomb resistance. Scanned pages or multimodal assets require explicitly available vision/OCR tools; never fabricate extracted content. Source-code symbol/call resolution should use real parsers or compilers where available and withhold when dynamic dispatch or incomplete build context prevents binding.

## 31. The ultimate ATLAS Developer experience

The owner should eventually be able to say: *“Inspect my authorized project, find the problem, explain it, make a safe patch, test it, show me the diff, and let me approve the result.”* ATLAS responds by inspecting the actual project, retrieving relevant verified language/engine documentation and known procedures, building a typed engineering plan, asking a specialist to generate a complete patch, testing in an isolated worktree/guest, obtaining an independent review, explaining tradeoffs and requesting owner approval before changing protected source. These are future gated capabilities; they are not authorized just because a candidate model is available.

**Code answer contract:** For every nontrivial code suggestion provide exact file path and target version, full paste-ready code (not placeholders), precise insertion/replacement position, needed imports/dependencies, namespace or API, setup and integration steps, exposed configurable variables with defaults and units, expected behavior, build/test commands, limitations, provenance for factual API claims, and whether compilation/tests actually ran. Never assert a patch was tested when only static review occurred. Prefer minimal edits and preserve the user’s work. Avoid unrelated refactoring. Handle incomplete project context explicitly.

## 32. Major coding technologies

High-priority project-aware specialists should progressively cover **Unreal Engine C++ and Blueprint assets**, **UEFN Verse**, **Unity C#**, **Blender Python (`bpy`)**, **C**, **C++**, **C#/.NET**, **Python**, **JavaScript/TypeScript/HTML/CSS**, **Rust**, **SQL**, shaders/HLSL/GLSL, and eventually PLATZ Engine's scripting/visual behavior graph. Expand to Java/Kotlin, Swift, Go, Lua, Bash/PowerShell, PHP, databases and infrastructure tooling when actual owner tasks justify it. A specialist must pin real compiler/runtime/engine versions, use official authoritative API evidence where available, and keep unsupported output visibly tentative. Distinguish Blueprint pseudo-steps from real asset edits.

## 33. Reliable software modification and tests

Secure development architecture: authorized read-only project inspection → bounded source change proposal → snapshot and preflight → isolated worktree → vetted tool/compiler invocation in qualified guest → unit/property/integration tests → independent reviewer → owner diff/permission gate → atomic allowed application → rollback or acceptance with provenance. Never execute arbitrary code on the physical Windows host merely because it appeared in a model response or downloaded repository. The old Arena's Windows path canonicalization bypasses (trailing dots, drive-relative, alternate data streams, case/8.3 forms and symlinks) are historical regression families. Quarantine downloads, deny unapproved network callbacks and use least-privilege credentials. Keep protected test suites and approval code inaccessible to the candidate model.

## 34. App, web and game creation

ATLAS should eventually produce entire applications and game systems, not just snippets: requirements, interface design, asset assumptions, database/schema plans, build scripts, tests, deploy guidance and maintainable code. Example Unreal systems: character controller, inventory, save/load, damage, multiplayer synchronization, abilities, enemy AI and configurable UI; Unity: data-driven inventory, serializers, scene/prefab integration, tests; UEFN: valid Verse systems with correct device/event APIs; Blender: meshes, materials, rigging, animations, geometry-node scripts, export checks; web/desktop: responsive UI, state management, accessibility and compiled/smoke-tested flows. All require relevant engine/editor versions, resource limits and explicit permissions before touching real projects.

## 35. Creative capability family

**Sprites/pixel art:** consistent character sheets, tile sets, animation frames, color palettes, topology and export naming; continuity across revisions. **2D/3D design:** meshes, topology, materials, UVs, skeletons, rigs, skinning, IK and animation; independent file-format validators. **Writing:** books, novels, story bible, character/timeline continuity, iterative chapter planning, grammar/voice edit, permissions for retained author data. **Video/audio/music:** timelines, media transcodes, voice/translation assistance, effects and original compositions subject to source rights. **Art direction:** mood boards, style guides, production specs and asset provenance. **Documents:** complete Word/PDF/slide/spreadsheet deliverables via tested format tools. **Education/math/science:** source-grounded explanations, reproducible arithmetic, deterministic calculators, unit checks, experiments and uncertainty.

Multimodal capabilities are long-term distinct research tracks. A file metadata parser is not a vision model, and a code generator is not a sprite generator. Measure artifact utility and output correctness with appropriate validators and owner review rather than calling everything a specialist prematurely.

## 36. Game-playing and simulation intelligence

Future ATLAS game-playing or simulation specialists may observe state through approved APIs, choose bounded actions, learn from virtual outcomes and run comparisons in isolated games/simulations. Allowed virtual in-game-currency transactions may be supported when authorized. No real-money purchases, in-game cash transactions using owner funds, gambling, credential access or unapproved network-account use. Distinguish simulated outcomes from actions in a real account. Always provide game-specific permission, action bounds, telemetry and stop controls.

# PART VII — ORIGINAL MODEL FACTORY, TRAINING DIRECTOR, EVALUATION AND SELF-IMPROVEMENT

## 37. Original model is a provenance claim

A model is **ATLAS-original/from-scratch** only if its trainable model weights were independently initialized rather than inherited from Qwen, Gemma, Llama, OpenAI, Mistral or another pretrained foundation, and the checkpoint manifest makes any imported tokenizer, embeddings, datasets and architectural inspiration explicit. Using public algorithms, Python, NumPy, PyTorch or CUDA does not compromise the claim. Reusing a pretrained tokenizer should be labeled as an imported tokenizer, not secretly as a learned original tokenizer. An adapted pretrained checkpoint, even if ATLAS trained LoRA weights, is **not original-from-scratch**. A model with 37 parameters can be original yet too limited to understand language; label both properties.

Each model registry row MUST include unique ID and family; exact architecture/hyperparameters; parameter counts (trainable and frozen); origin category (`original`, `adapted_reference`, `external_reference`, `deterministic`, `host_tool`); initialization; tokenizer and corpus provenance; licenses; training/validation/protected-family splits; optimizer/seed/scheduler; checkpoint SHA-256; hardware/time/resource measurements; actual known competencies; task types; uncertainty; shadow/qualified/retired state; acceptance reviewer; restrictions; previous baseline; observed wins and losses.

## 38. Model factory and scale strategy

Support multiple research tracks while keeping original models primary: **Track A** from-scratch small classifiers, encoders, action models, small language models and eventually Transformers; **Track B** independent pretrained reference comparisons; **Track C** efficient shared-backbone/adapters as optional scientific controls, with lineage; **Track D** optional teacher suggestions with verifier-grounded results. The owner’s current priority is Track A. Do not turn the five-update batch into a Qwen adapter program.

Scale must follow a **data and compute ladder**: tiny original decision policies for diagnosable tasks → learned text embeddings/compact encoders and restricted output models → small Transformers for genuine language tasks → tens/hundreds of millions if valid data and protected eval support scaling → near 1B specialists if measurable benefit justifies it → multi-billion-parameter Masters only after sufficient compute/data and deployment evidence. A model that memorizes 60 authored questions cannot reasonably be described as a general language model. Train a tokenizer on authorized text with decontamination before claiming open-vocabulary natural language capacity; evaluate on unseen compositional structures, paraphrases, domains and semantic changes. Keep a no-neural comparator and an affordable simple learned comparator in every model experiment.

Architectures may include logistic/linear models, small MLPs, GRUs, small Transformers, sparse models, retrieval-augmented but **original-weight** encoders/decoders, policy/value models and ensemble gates. Evaluate expressive capacity, calibration, failure modes, speed and task value under fair budgets; do not choose a Transformer merely because of hype. “Reasoning” must be demonstrated through multi-step generalization and correct action selection on hidden tasks, not by writing a model named `Reasoner`.

## 39. Honest dataset acquisition and eligibility

The current source-selection data has a narrow audit-family distribution; NEXT-020 produced only negative newly observed proposals. The Foundry should expand **legitimate, independently verifiable positives and negatives** across unrelated authorized project families. Each task example must include original user-style request, authorized file set, expected objective/outcome derived from a trusted independent measurement, actual candidate actions, verification receipts, failure/abstention type, toolchain versions, compute cost, origin and permission. A training row cannot be eligible because a model judged its own answer correct. A label copied from the same heuristic being evaluated is valid as imitation data only, not proof of improved reasoning.

Collect tasks via owner-authorized local test projects, installed licensed public distributions, well-defined independent transformation/compilation problems, verified factual/procedural cases and actual tested engineering workflows. Do not quietly ingest the user’s private documents, emails, web accounts, app history or source code into global weights. Permission to import a file for a question does not grant permission for training. Record licensing/data rights and deletion obligations. Preserve rejected data and the reason; no training on protected eval or leaked label files.

**Split design:** by project/family/source lineage and temporal event, not only random rows. Keep near-duplicate hashes, normalized requests, n-grams, substrings, MinHash/similarity and source text overlap checks; disclose decontamination limitations. Separate train, validation, public evaluation and genuinely protected independent custody suites. Test meaningful distribution shift (new file names, natural requests, syntax, language version, task horizon, distractors, reordered evidence, contradictions). Evaluation families and labels must be inaccessible to the candidate/trainer. Repeated measurements of the same underlying example are not independent data points; show effective question count, source-family count and action count separately.

## 40. Training Director and Learning Council

The long-term **Training Director** should persistently manage owner-approved improvement proposals rather than wait for manually written datasets. It monitors verified failures, classifies root causes, ranks opportunities by expected task benefit, chooses a bounded experiment, checks eligibility, requests resource budgets, trains candidates, runs preregistered tests, reports evidence and requests qualification. It must distinguish **model weakness, missing knowledge, unsupported parser/tool, bad evaluation, source ambiguity and external permission blocker**. The correct solution to a missing verifier feature may be a tool change—not retraining a model.

A conceptual **nine-member Training Council** retains useful roles from the older source: chief director/priority selector, diagnostics, curriculum planner, data curator/decontamination, training coach, resource scheduler, independent red-team challenger, evaluator/arena judge, and file/procedure flywheel compiler. These are responsibility contracts; they need not be nine loaded neural models. Each member's recommendation is a proposal to trusted host workflows and qualification gates. A deterministic implementation can perform an early responsibility until a trained version proves better.

## 41. Persistent training state machine

A safely resumable experiment typically moves through: `observe verified weakness → triage cause → rank opportunity → define objective/controls → generate proposed data → verify labels and rights → decontaminate/split → resource preflight → train candidate → run validation/early stop → evaluate frozen public suite → independent protected challenge → record verdict → retain/reject/owner-approved promotion → store verified lessons`. The older fourteen-stage curriculum and council catalog are historical design aids, not authority to activate unattended GPU jobs or bypass independent testing. Every transition must have an idempotent receipt, schema validation and recovery behavior. Failed training, partial optimizer state, disk shortage and interrupted sessions must not corrupt the incumbent checkpoint.

## 42. Training techniques and actual observability

Track appropriate loss, validation accuracy and calibration, confusion cases, abstentions, wrong deliveries, exact downstream completions, latency/VRAM/CPU/RAM/energy where available, dataset/source version, optimizer, learning rate, gradient norms, batch/sequence length, throughput, checkpoint sizes, wall time and early-stop criterion. Show **real measurements**; no animated fabricated progress. Support SFT where valid, contrastive ranking, preference optimization with real labels, distilled candidates only with rights and verified outcomes, and from-scratch self-supervision when data and hardware justify it. Reject optimizer/loss changes that do not improve independently measured outcomes. Reproduce seeds where practical; disclose nondeterministic CUDA operations and hardware/runtime versions.

Owner-facing controls: `Create a Model`, `Improve a Model`, `Let ATLAS Choose`, `Compare`, `Pause at Safe Checkpoint`, `Stop After Current Stage`, `Cancel/Abort Within Safe Boundaries`, `Resume`, `Rollback`, `Export`. A stop instruction must eventually reach trusted safe cancellation; do not promise a fixed three-second full GPU release in every runtime. Real checkpoint pause/resume must restore optimizer states and comparable loss trajectories, not merely reload weights. Reports should display improvements and failures in understandable terms, and never silently replace the incumbent.

## 43. Qualification and promotion

At minimum distinguish `proposed`, `training`, `trained_unqualified`, `shadow`, `qualified_candidate`, `independently_accepted`, `owner_approved_active`, `rejected`, `retired` (implement as a versioned state machine with valid transitions). An original model can demonstrate training and still be rejected. **No model can self-approve**. Candidate models and the Training Director must not modify protected tests, scoring rules, authority files or baseline snapshots during evaluation. If a proposed model underperforms deterministic/linear comparators in a mission-critical domain, it should remain shadow or be retired even if its training loss decreased.

Qualifications should be **domain-specific** and report independently held unfamiliar-task successes, wrong deliveries, confidence intervals where meaningful, maximum risk thresholds, resource costs, user effort, security checks, and regression history. A 16/20 vs 16/20 tie is not a win. A model that reduces cost with safe quality parity may qualify for a specific efficiency claim if the saving is measured and meaningful. Model-only accuracy does not establish end-to-end effect when trusted host fallback dominates outcomes. Document when the original model's decision actually changed an answer or action.

## 44. Native model and full-system benchmark matrix

Always compare: host-only; exact/FTS/TF-IDF; strong linear/logistic; prior original checkpoint; candidate original model; candidate + procedures and knowledge; candidate + verified tools; candidate in cooperation with other original models; optional authorized pretrained local reference under separate cost/lineage; and no-model pure-file path. Hold inputs/tool access and budgets constant where appropriate, also report native-only/production-quality outcomes. Measure correct completions, incorrect verified delivery, safe abstention, false abstention, evidence provenance violations, cost per successful task, CPU/GPU time, VRAM, peak RSS, number of tools, training-data burden and cross-family generalization. Use confidence bands/paired comparisons where sample sizes permit; never infer broad supremacy from twenty cases.

## 45. Self-improvement and scientific falsifiability

The meaningful native self-improvement cycle is **identify a causal weakness → obtain eligible independent outcomes → freeze hypothesis and evaluation → train candidate → compare before/after with controls → independently challenge → retain or reject → record transferable procedure and next unknown**. Failed experiments count as scientific results and can reveal tool-verifier bottlenecks. They are not evidence of model improvement. Improvement requires a model with better unfamiliar-task utility, or justified efficiency at equal quality, not a prettier dashboard or slightly lower loss. An unattended cycle must be separately authorized, resource-bounded and independently certified before deployment. Initially it can be manually triggered, offline and restricted to author-safe datasets.

## 46. Experimental architecture and curriculum

Track learning progression over at least these **fourteen difficulty dimensions**: reasoning horizon; constraint density; context signal-to-noise; ambiguity; tool/verifier chaining; error recovery and replanning; output-schema strictness; domain edge-case rarity; cross-domain synthesis; adversarial premise resistance; token/latency efficiency; temporal and state mutation depth; verification rigor; and reusable deterministic compilation yield. Increase difficulty when measured mastery warrants it, without exposing hidden answers. Hard task generation must be subject to independent validators and sample diversity; a generated task labeled by its generator is not protected evaluation. Compare multiple original architectures, seeds, ablations and cooperation policies only when statistical power and compute budget make the experiment worthwhile.

# PART VIII — EVOLUTION ARENA, SELF-DEVELOPMENT, AUTONOMY AND COMPUTE GOVERNANCE

## 47. Multi-branch software evolution arena

The owner has explicitly requested a future controlled development arena with **parallel branches of the same application**, each following a different verified strategy: **reliability-first**, **high-risk/high-reward**, **performance/optimization**, **feature-heavy**, **visual/UI-first** and optional hybrid. Each branch should undergo **ten sequential, distinct updates**, not ten replays of the same trivial patch. Every branch must use the same starting snapshot, fair resource/permission constraints, strict isolation and independent evaluation. Preserve all attempts, negative results, source lineage, test costs and rollbacks. Compare correctness, reliability, performance, genuinely useful features, accessibility, UX, maintainability and consumed resources. A later research phase can train narrow developer policies from successful strategies or safely merge independently compatible improvements. The execution boundary must be qualified before enabling such experiments. ATLAS Next must not unlock untrusted code execution merely because the old Arena had historical examples.

## 48. Automated Developer and Code Improvement

The eventual internal Developer should understand the actual authorized ATLAS repository, bugs, tests and local toolchain; generate code proposals using original specialists when qualified; test them in sandboxed worktrees/guests; solicit an independent review; record lessons; and present a precise owner approval. It should be able to improve its own UI, storage, coding tools and model-training procedures through bounded revisions. **The owner must not need to write a new technical implementation prompt for every iteration**, but must retain control of permissions, approvals and rollback. Generated patches cannot modify their own independent reviewer, trusted authorization, evaluator custody, or program security policy without a higher assurance external review and owner approval. No auto-promotion of self-modifying trusted components.

## 49. Gradated autonomy, not a binary switch

Proposed levels: **A0 manual read-only**; **A1 multi-step read-only local tasks**; **A2 bounded authorized local training/research in quarantined data**; **A3 owner-approved edits in isolated project worktrees**; **A4 independently qualified guest execution and bounded network research**; **A5 scheduled approved tasks under resource/policy leases**. Levels do not inherit all permissions from earlier tasks; each capability needs a specific host grant. Raising a level needs safety review and owner action. The current NEXT-020 state does **not** qualify ATLAS for A3–A5. The host must prevent privilege escalation, resource exhaustion, arbitrary filesystem access, network exfiltration and use of owner personal accounts at every level.

## 50. Idle-time Training and Research Director

The long-term system may detect genuinely idle windows under explicit opt-in and apply owner-configured time, energy, thermal, CPU/GPU/VRAM/RAM/disk and battery/foreground budgets. Useful approved jobs could include data eligibility checks, static indexing, validation, training candidates, model comparisons, search relevance experiments, documentation rechecks and offline creative asset analyses. The owner should see why a job was chosen, what it used, exactly what it accomplished and whether it improved anything. Immediate foreground responsiveness and pause/stop authority take precedence over idle learning. No silent downloads, subscriptions, account creation, persistent network contact or personal-data training as a byproduct of idle mode. Unattended model or application promotion needs separate independent gates.

## 51. Resource-aware workstation execution

The RTX 5080 and 96 GB RAM should be used intelligently, not necessarily at 100% utilization. Provide a single trusted compute arbiter controlling model load/unload, GPU reservations, CPU threads, memory ceilings, model offload, checkpoint I/O, cooling/thermal monitoring when available, and disk reserve. Have profiles for **pure deterministic**, **native efficient interactive**, **native high-quality interactive**, **reference comparison only**, **dedicated training**, **idle training** and **safe mixed foreground/background** when measured. Stage heavy optional services separately so WPF remains responsive. Record actual startup/loading and p50/p95/p99 request latency, training steps/s, peak allocations, source I/O, cold/warm cache effects and failure recovery. CPU-only fallback should exist for tiny models and key verification. A 16 GB VRAM card does not make unbounded 12B+ simultaneous models feasible.

## 52. Durable recovery, reporting and owner override

Task graph state, datasets, source catalogs, checkpoints, histories and queued training proposals require atomic versioned persistence, checksums, idempotency and corruption quarantine. A restart must distinguish completed from interrupted work and not silently repeat a destructive action. Survive quota exhaustion, agent session endings, Windows sleep/restart, interrupted writes and partial packages. Owner has inspect/cancel/revoke/export/rollback controls; any urgent host stop should be enforceable without requesting model cooperation. Never promise that the assistant is secretly working in background unless an actual approved persistent scheduler and watchdog are running. Daily summaries/notifications are optional and must be real, permissioned and revocable.

# PART IX — PRODUCT EXPERIENCE, UI, PERSONAL ASSISTANCE AND STORAGE

## 53. Three-tier usability

The owner should have three compatible levels of complexity: **Just Do It** (one question or goal, clear progress and permission prompts); **Command Center** (projects, selected model, resources, history, models, comparisons and current tasks); **Advanced Studio** (full DAG, model graph, training architecture, experiment traces, raw citations, source/catalog controls, privacy/export, evaluator reports). Do not require the owner to manually choose an obscure specialist for every request. “Let ATLAS Choose” selects a qualified policy based on real evidence and displays why. If no native specialist is qualified, deterministic mode should still work and the limitation should be honest. An explicitly requested reference model may be used only with clear opt-in.

## 54. Unified navigation and 13 expandable workspaces

A practical top-level navigation may have **Home**, **Chat/Assistant**, **Projects**, **Models**, **Training**, and **Knowledge/Memory**, with advanced inspectors opened contextually rather than 13 permanently competing tabs. Preserve the 13 original functionality workspaces as discoverable surfaces: Dashboard; Assistant/Chat; Projects; Task Graph; Knowledge/Tier-5 Vaults; Personal Memory; Model Registry; Model Arena; Training Laboratory; Training Observatory; Prompt Laboratory; Capability Library; Settings/Privacy/Storage Auditor. The historical alternate 13 workspace mapping additionally included Architecture Lab, Training Council/Queue, Curriculum Matrix, Self-Development Studio and Creative/Game Studio. These can be subpanels, not necessarily separate top-level screens. A workspace exists only if its actions are real; unreleased functions are clearly disabled and explained.

**Dashboard:** verified latest model results, active project, real tasks, compute and training status, current blockers, fast native-only question. **Chat:** clearly separated native, deterministic, optional reference, Private Playground and Personal ATLAS modes; citations and model trace inspectable on demand. **Projects:** import/inspect source, versioned revisions, code intelligence, research goals, artifacts, authorized write proposals. **Task Graph:** live node status, scopes, dependencies, model origin, steps and rejection. **Knowledge:** browse answer cards, procedures, source documents, contradictions, corrections, eligibility. **Models/Arena:** current original checkpoints, adapted references, independent controls, qualification stage, failures and rankings. **Training:** actual experiments, live metrics, queue, pause/resume, provenance, curricula, model creation. **Capabilities:** searchable actual tested workflows and honest unavailable states. **Settings:** permission grants, network isolation, private data, storage, resource caps and shutdown.

## 55. User experience details and quality

Test 3840x2160, 2560x1440, 1920x1080 and 1280x720, Windows DPI scaling, window resize/snap, keyboard operation, high contrast, screen reader accessibility, focus order, screen reader labels, performance with large histories and cancellation. Support font size, theme, density, navigation width, saved layout, docked inspectors, search, export, copy-to-clipboard, proper syntax highlighting and diff view. Never hide a failure behind a green success animation. Model/CPU/GPU resource indicators must be measured. Status language should distinguish `not implemented`, `installed`, `trained`, `shadow`, `qualified`, `active`, `failed`, `blocked`, `awaiting independent review` and `owner approval required`.

## 56. Local conversational AI, provenance and model identity

The original ATLAS already supported local Ollama/pretrained chat; Next has begun a controlled owner-selected loopback chat. A genuinely native-only conversational assistant remains an open research goal. Model streaming/cancellation, correct thinking/output delimiters, tool invocations, context limits and error messages should be validated against actual installed model APIs rather than assumed. Do not silently download model weights or send requests outside local loopback. A reference-assisted answer must show the actual model name, version and source of any displayed citations. Pretrained reference responses must never enter trusted claim/procedure stores without independent verification. Personal or project content must never be sent to a model with unknown network behavior without explicit permission.

## 57. Personal ATLAS and the private model sandbox

Three use cases remain distinct: **Private Playground**—no ATLAS-side retained conversation/memory/training; **Personal ATLAS**—explicit owner consent for encrypted private memory and categories; **Public/project assistant**—only permitted project and public knowledge. Personal ATLAS should support scoped preferences, corrections, goals, task reminders, safe file organization, travel planning, education and everyday assistance, while carefully distinguishing user plans from facts and current versus historical state. The 19 proposed personal categories are schema possibilities, not permission to collect information by default. Private content must not be copied to developer prompts, Git, evaluator public cases, shared cloud models, or ATLAS-original training without separate affirmative grants.

**Pairwise answer chooser:** Optional user-facing side-by-side comparison of two explicitly selected models, keeping exact model identity, settings, eligibility, similarity and human preference separate from AI judgment. A real owner click is the only `human_preference`; machine verdict is `ai_judge` or `predicted_preference`. Do not imply the owner chose a result based on a synthetic score. Support comparison exclusions to keep sensitive records out of training and research.

## 58. Storage, backup and data lifecycle

Separate binaries, model weights, indexes, caches, owner files, private memory, task receipts, public knowledge, training datasets, protected evaluation, logs and exports. Provide a storage explorer with size, scope, origin and retention. Use migrations and backups for schema changes, atomic writes where practical, integrity checks and recovery journals. Protect personal data with scoped encryption and secret handling; disk encryption alone does not imply app-level zero retention. Never copy plaintext private history into shared logs. Support inspection, export, corrections, deletion with clear limits (including backups and previously trained weights), and revocation of background jobs. A new profile or clean portable installation must not corrupt legacy profiles or silently import sensitive content.

# PART X — SECURITY CONSTITUTION, TRUST BOUNDARIES AND OWNER AUTHORIZATION

## 59. Absolute owner prohibitions

No ATLAS agent, internal model, external developer agent or unattended workflow may use the owner's **personal accounts, identity, authentication sessions, payment instruments, money, subscriptions, free trials, private secrets, personal location or private files** for autonomous actions. No real-money purchase, including in-game purchase, under any circumstances without a future explicit owner-directed non-autonomous transaction workflow (default remains deny). Virtual in-game-currency actions are a separate permitted category if within an authorized game/sandbox. No unapproved account registration, external publishing, social posting, email sending, license purchase, cloud subscription, API billing or resource reservation. A locally trained model must not infer or reveal private data by mining logs.

Online account registration or downloading by ATLAS, if ever authorized, must occur in an **isolated remote environment**, not an owner-authenticated browser/profile on the physical Windows PC, to reduce identity/IP/location exposure. Do not assert guaranteed anonymity, nontraceability or malware freedom. Never sign into an ATLAS-created account on the owner’s physical host. Do not use a VPN/proxy as a substitute for verified separation and owner-approved network policy. Protected operations need capability-specific positive permission, not blanket “agent autonomy” consent.

## 60. Trust hierarchy

Trusted OS and host policy + explicit owner consent > independently accepted security boundaries and evaluator custody > verified in-scope facts/procedures > newly tested tools > provisional sources > unverified data > model outputs. A model cannot upgrade a lower trust tier by summarizing it. User-supplied repositories, web pages, README instructions, model output, generated code, logs, document macros and tool responses are untrusted inputs unless specifically promoted by the host. Prompt injection defenses must be tested on realistic source comments, documents and malicious downloaded packages. Source citation proves *what was written*, not necessarily objective truth or safe execution.

## 61. Capability permissions

Typed capabilities should be scoped by user, project, operation, path, allowed file hashes, destination, expiration, budgets, approval state, current task and agent identity. Permissions for `read imported file`, `read project tree`, `write candidate patch`, `apply to owner project`, `run isolated tests`, `access network`, `download`, `register account`, `train on data`, `modify private memory`, `publish`, `schedule background work` and `promote model` are **different** and cannot be inferred from one another. Default deny for unfamiliar operations; revoke rights promptly; sign or hash important approval receipts; do not accept task text as authorization. A trustworthy host grants capabilities, validates every call and logs privileged changes.

## 62. Untrusted execution and guest isolation

Host execution, shell access, browser control, generated code execution and project self-development are RESTRICTED until an independently qualified guest/VM boundary exists on the actual Windows workstation. Previous attempts to rely on Hyper-V without proven guest availability failed. Never silently fall back to running untrusted code on the Windows host. Guest scope must address filesystem path canonicalization, hard links, symlinks, case folding, trailing-dot spaces, ADS, drive-relative forms, 8.3 aliases, device names, reparse points, mount escapes, TOCTOU, zip bombs, network callbacks, GPU/resource abuse and policy bypass. Use disposable snapshots, network deny by default, signed artifact movement and independent tests. Owner personal home folders, keys, browser profiles and account tokens must not be mounted into the guest.

## 63. Protected evaluation and self-modification safeguards

The same system being trained must not control the test set, hidden expected labels, evaluator scoring implementation, host authorization or its own promotion. Separate evaluator custody and author read permissions. A developer can use public evaluation but must not read hidden cases. Record baseline and preregistration *before* model fitting or code changes where benchmark-sensitive. A reviewer validates a frozen commit without editing the candidate. No model-generated patch to trusted security components gets automatic approval from a model that benefited from the change. Self-modifying agents are restricted to bounded candidates, with independent reviewer and owner promotion gates.

## 64. Supply chain, secrets, privacy and external services

Verify hashes, package manifests, signatures where supported, pinned dependencies and origin of downloaded models/data. Run only authorized packages, with no blind `curl | bash` behavior. Secret scanning, license review, model-card provenance, dataset rights and malware protections are required for higher-risk workflows. Avoid telemetry and cloud fallbacks by default. Remote research uses independent network controls and minimal logging, not personal identity. Use encryption at rest for private scopes, restricted developer logs, secure key storage and explicit retention. A public developer knowledge vault must never contain an imported private personal vault or personal account content.

## 65. Safe failure, incident response and rollback

On incorrect trusted delivery, source-crossing, evaluation leakage, path escape, unexpected network action, secret access or unauthorized resource usage: stop the affected capability, preserve forensic receipts without leaking sensitive content, quarantine outputs, report the issue, mark dependent milestones blocked, revoke capability leases, and require review before resuming. Repair changes must be independently testable and separately versioned. Owners need clear stop, export and recovery instructions. The security goal is *bounded damage and honest detection*, never an absolute claim of perfect isolation or zero residual risk.

# PART XI — PLATZ ENGINE AS A SEPARATE, COMPLETE PRODUCT VISION

## 66. PLATZ Engine identity and non-interference

PLATZ Engine is an independent major product of PLATZ Technologies, not a renamed ATLAS module and not an ATLAS Next implementation milestone. Its governing documents remain `PLATZ_MASTER.md` and `PLATZ_WORKING.md`; past FORGE source is read-only and its historical claims must be verified before selective reuse. The intended suite is **PLATZ Core** (foundational engine systems and project schema), **PLATZ Runtime** (play/export execution), **PLATZ Studio** (accessible visual editor) and **PLATZ Hub** (content, extensions and templates). ATLAS may serve as a future intelligent developer or asset assistant via explicit APIs, but either product must be independently useful when the other is not installed.

## 67. Architecture and renderer

The engine should be modular, performant, no-code capable and code-extensible for **2D, pixel, 2.5D, voxel and 3D**, including eventual large scenes/worlds. Data is canonical, editable, versioned and portable, with non-destructive operations and an auditable dependency graph. The preferred graphics architecture is **Vulkan/GPU-first**, with **OpenGL and CPU reference fallbacks** where appropriate. Prove early real rendering, shader correctness, device loss recovery, CPU/GPU consistency and asset portability. The 8K/high-refresh/16K asset scale ambitions are engineering targets contingent on independent profiling, not blanket performance promises. Large worlds need streaming, LOD, memory budgeting and graceful device capability fallback.

## 68. Studio authoring and creative systems

PLATZ Studio should offer intuitive context-driven UX, exact numeric editing, discoverable property inspectors, dockable/preset workspaces, history/undo, accessible keyboard and screen-reader controls, selection/pivot/gizmo tools and non-destructive asset pipelines. Core world features include scenes, entities, prefabs, transforms, cameras, lighting, materials, meshes, UVs, tiles, pixel drawing, voxel tools, animation, skeletons, IK, timelines, physics, collisions, traces, character movement, terrain and procedural building. Game UI/HUD, navigation, audio, gameplay templates, export and plugins are phased with appropriate functional acceptance projects.

**Universal behavior parity:** Variables → Events → Conditions → Actions must have faithful code equivalents (and vice versa where feasible) with deterministic semantics and correct serialization. No separate incompatible “visual-only” execution engine. Test example programs in both authoring modes and compare outputs with a reference runtime. Data round-trip must preserve project edits and not destructively regenerate files. An ordinary owner should be able to build a game through visual flows while an advanced engineer extends engine behavior with code.

## 69. Future ATLAS ↔ PLATZ interface

Use a versioned, capability-gated interface for queries such as project manifest, scene graph, asset metadata, selected object or property, read-only project schema, editor preview, patch proposal, test request and import/export artifact. An ATLAS game specialist can eventually answer: “Create a voxel survival prototype in PLATZ and test collision,” but only once both the engine and isolated authorized execution workflows are independently verified. No direct database/source-tree merging; model-generated assets and code remain proposals until reviewed.
