ATLAS must not block PLATZ milestones, alter PLATZ's own independent renderer roadmap, or treat PLATZ integration as a shortcut for native intelligence qualification.

# PART XII — IMMEDIATE NEXT-021 THROUGH NEXT-025 NATIVE INTELLIGENCE BATCH

## 70. Prerequisite: finish independent review of NEXT-015R–020

**Do not confuse author completion with accepted baseline.** The latest provided Sol report states that NEXT-015R and NEXT-016–NEXT-020 are implemented and author-tested, but pending **Gemini Argon Orchestrator's independent per-update and integrated verdict**. NEXT-014 remains accepted as of the latest uploaded source snapshot. The first obligation is to read the live `docs/ai/ATLAS_HANDOFF.md`, verify whether Argon has since reviewed the batch, and reconcile any consolidated defects. If review is pending, the agent can develop a proposed curriculum and independent preregistration on a separate provisional branch, but it must not silently promote unaccepted checkpoints or claim NEXT-021's training-data foundation is safe. The owner wants five substantive updates per batch; limited source approval steps can be grouped without sacrificing frozen review boundaries.

**BATCH SUCCESS DEFINITION:** By NEXT-025, ATLAS should be able to run a **bounded native-only task** that takes unfamiliar natural-language input, uses a newly trained original language/intent model to propose structured steps, retrieves verified facts and procedures, executes safe read-only tools through host grants, and records a complete verified outcome; separately it should conduct a reproducible attempt to improve an original model and accept or reject based on evaluator-controlled results. No requirement to claim a breakthrough if evidence does not support it. Every capability must show the original model's *causal contribution* versus strong baselines.

## 71. NEXT-021 — Native Learning & Knowledge Foundry

**Problem:** The current dataset has 360 action observations but only 60 authored questions across 15 project families and many measured negative proposals. The existing host often withholds because its grammar or source support cannot verify the task. Data quantity and broad model utility are insufficient. A new, trustworthy and **useful** data-acquisition/knowledge system is more important than training a larger neural network on the same narrow corpus.

**Implementable vertical slice:** Build a Foundry for owner-authorized local public projects/documents. It imports a scoped task family and normalized source manifests, runs trusted deterministic tools to measure candidate actions, binds every requested answer to the actual task and file digests, writes eligible data to immutable records, and stores verified fact/procedure cards. Collect both positive and negative trajectories; have an evidence auditor detect inability to verify vs wrong policy. Produce diverse natural-language paraphrases only when their expected result is independently verifiable and genuinely distinct. Support source family splitting, duplicate/near-duplicate detection, data rights, evaluation custody and automatic quarantine of ineligible observations. Include failure taxonomy: unsupported host grammar, wrong source selection, ambiguous evidence, contradiction, missed procedure, bad model action, timeout, security denial and evaluator mismatch.

**Required artifacts:** versioned task/dataset schema; eligibility and dedup reports; source+procedure hierarchy; provenance graph or indexed tables; reproducible acquisition CLI; tested per-case run receipts; candidate-training row builder with rights and family grouping; dataset viewer in model evidence when practical. Extend from actual local audit families and at least several distinct task types (e.g., verified source navigation, factual retrieval, plan-step selection, code test choice) rather than creating a huge synthetic count.

**Controls:** Do not use private owner data or hidden evaluator cases. No internet access is automatically enabled. An imported project is not blanket training consent. New tasks must be genuinely grounded by tests or independently verifiable source facts; teacher generations may suggest candidate cases but cannot label success alone. Preserve a fixed legacy dataset and evaluate proposed new data separately. Tool and retrieval defects should be categorized for host repair rather than relabeled as model weaknesses.

**Demonstration:** Two unrelated authorized projects with task families that do not share source templates; collect verified positive and negative actions, retrieve a stored failed-procedure record, and show that a protected holdout is inaccessible to the trainer. A deliberately switched request, changed source digest or mislabeled outcome is rejected. Report counts of unique questions/projects/families versus action measurements.

**Acceptance:** Dataset import is reproducible; every eligible positive outcome cites real independently checked evidence; at least one new task class has usable positive and negative examples; no train/eval family leakage; no output from protected custody enters training; all records survive restart and can be invalidated on changed source. A large record count is not sufficient. If true positives are still insufficient, NEXT-021 must report that limitation instead of fabricating them.

## 72. NEXT-022 — First original ATLAS language understanding / grounded generation experiment

**Problem:** The 37-parameter Primary Master receives numerical features and cannot understand natural instructions. The 65-parameter specialist ranks sources but cannot generate grounded explanations. The owner wants the first genuine from-scratch model that learns from language rather than a pretrained LLM wrapper.

**Task choice:** Begin with a bounded, **semantically varied natural-language intent and structured-response model**: understand requests for source inspection, research evidence, procedure selection and comparison; output a verified constrained action schema or short evidence-grounded answer. Use a tokenizer and text representation trained from authorized data; record any imported public tokenization algorithms and components. Establish vocabulary/length and generalization requirements before choosing architecture. A small Transformer encoder-decoder or original autoregressive decoder is a candidate, not a guaranteed choice; compare to a much simpler linear/character-n-gram baseline and an original small MLP. Choose parameter scale according to independently eligible corpus, GPU budget and actual learning curves. Scaling to millions of parameters is an experiment, not a milestone requirement.

**Training contract:** Randomly initialize all neural weights; preserve seed, optimizer and architecture hashes; reproduce training on CPU for a tiny fixture and optionally GPU for the real candidate; demonstrate that it sees raw natural-language tokens rather than only hand-engineered scoring vectors; train on task-relevant text and verified structured outcomes; monitor overfit/contamination; hold out unfamiliar projects, paraphrase patterns, combinations and task families; avoid memorized answer cards as proof of understanding. No Qwen/Gemini/GPT inference in native-only task runs. External models may be used as labeled controls or candidate-data generators only where owner-permitted and independent validators establish ground truth.

**Demonstration:** Give an unfamiliar authorized project a natural-language request not appearing in training, such as “Find the source for loading a workbook; summarize the accepted input names with citations and explain which evidence is missing.” The original model proposes a typed plan or constrained response; the deterministic host fetches/cites exact evidence and rejects unsupported claims. Run paraphrases, distractor file names, adversarially ambiguous tasks and changed source revisions. Compare task completion and cost against lexical/TF-IDF, linear, previous original and host-only policies. Show a useful learned contribution or clearly report parity/underperformance.

**Acceptance:** A real new original text-using checkpoint exists and loads offline; no pretrained foundation weights are present; at least one held-out task group demonstrates nontrivial generalization beyond exact template memorization; delivery remains evidence-verified; metrics include false abstention and incorrect trusted delivery; source/model attribution is explicit. **Do not call this a general-purpose conversational LLM** unless rigorous broad-language testing actually proves that. If data is inadequate, implement a smaller honest language policy, record why, and propose the next scaling dataset.

## 73. NEXT-023 — Learned multi-step reasoning and procedural retrieval

**Problem:** The current cooperative prototype relies on deterministic host fallback and one-step focused lookup. It does not demonstrate a model planning, selecting a procedure and adapting after evidence changes.

**Implementable vertical slice:** Create a typed **two-to-eight-step** read-only task arena with explicit state, budget and safe actions: inspect project, choose source, retrieve known procedure, find supporting text, check contradiction, request missing evidence, select a deterministic tool, evaluate and stop. A new original policy learns action selection or a short plan from verified trajectories; policies may use procedure embeddings or rule-constrained structured outputs. The host executes only allowlisted read-only steps and records transitions. Let the learned policy choose something *material* such as which evidence to inspect first or when to reroute after a failed lookup. Distinguish successful verified procedure application from simply retrieving text with the same name.

**Data/evaluation:** Use NEXT-021's eligible outcomes, including failure recoveries and procedures. Design independent task families not present in training, including distractors, changed evidence, errors and multiple valid strategies. Compare a fixed scripted plan, shortest-path/greedy rules, linear action selector, single native specialist and new policy with the same tool budget. Track successful verified tasks, wrong outcomes, safe withholds, average steps, latency, resource cost and helpful intermediate decisions. Report whether the learned actions caused improvements; do not claim multi-step reasoning if the host secretly executes the successful fixed plan regardless of the model's choice.

**Demonstration:** On a local source/document research task, the original policy selects a procedure, encounters missing or contradictory evidence, revises its source choice within the allowed graph, and produces an evidenced result or justified abstention. Record the complete action trace and show exactly what would happen under the deterministic baseline.

**Acceptance:** The multi-step runner is usable offline; intermediate model decisions affect the executed read-only path; every node is authorized, budgeted and fully logged; results are source-provenance checked; a held-out comparison establishes useful effect, or honestly fails to do so. No automatic project edits, shell or network actions. Preserve the evaluator's independent custody.

## 74. NEXT-024 — Three original functional Master competencies

**Problem:** The three-master dream remains mostly conceptual. Calling 37-parameter gating a Primary Master is not evidence of full independent reasoning, and simply copying it under Research/Engineering labels will not help.

**Implementable vertical slice:** Give each role a **distinct trained decision contract** using original initialization or provably independent trained heads with transparent lineage: Primary selects work graph and appropriate agent; Research chooses evidence sufficiency/recheck/procedure; Engineering selects project inspection/test-planning steps. The first team must complete a restricted source-and-research task where these role decisions genuinely matter. Use an explicit message bus/receipt DAG and host authorization, not prompts that bypass constraints. Roles may share embeddings or a tokenizer if this improves performance, but record each role’s own data and evaluation. Provide deterministic fallback for unqualified decisions.

**Comparisons:** Primary alone; Research alone; Engineering alone; deterministic workflow; best two-role combination; all three original roles. Measure performance, mistakes, abstention, latency, VRAM, cross-project transfer and external calls. Multi-model cooperation is not a gain unless it improves at least one useful end-to-end objective without unacceptable tradeoffs. Learned roles must be assigned by actual capability, not preselected so they appear necessary.

**Demonstration:** A user states a bounded goal involving a source-file question and research evidence. Primary selects a structured investigation; Research chooses a justified evidence action; Engineering inspects applicable code context or test plan; a separate verifier checks the answer. Explain each model's actual decision and what was different from the single-agent/control run.

**Acceptance:** Three real operational decision contracts; trained original weights where data allows, with no invented model quality; complete provenance, action budget and safe scope; independent tests for misleading evidence, wrong project, stale revision, duplicate task and task cancellation; a fair ablation measuring the cooperation effect. No general-purpose “three AI brains” claim without general competence.

## 75. NEXT-025 — Bounded native-only self-improvement cycle

**Problem:** NEXT-020's negative-only refit produced no useful improvement. The next cycle needs real eligible new outcomes, a causal training target, frozen tests and evidence of whether an original model can become better on unseen work.

**Implementable vertical slice:** Build a locally running **goal-to-evaluation loop** with at least one chosen original model: detect a measured failure family using NEXT-021 data; create a training proposal and preregister controls; acquire/validate positive and negative examples; train one or more candidate checkpoints from an appropriate original incumbent; run publicly frozen family-separated evaluation and let an independent custodian execute protected tests; compare with incumbent/linear/host; reject or retain candidate based on stated safety+utility gates; preserve complete data, seeds, weights, optimizer, costs, diagnostic traces and rejected failures. The **trainer cannot access or edit hidden expectations**, and the candidate cannot grant itself authority.

**Autonomy objective:** The native Training Director should carry out the bounded experiment from a single owner-approved task without Sol/Gemini selecting every training step. However, internal host-controlled policies and workflow scaffolding remain visible, and the experiment can be manually invoked until idle permissions are separately certified. ATLAS should explain *what it tried and what actually improved*. It can be a successful experiment if it correctly rejects a worse model; it cannot be described as a successful **model improvement** unless independent new-task performance improves.

**Demonstration:** Run an approved offline native experiment on one measured weakness; show the previous checkpoint, selected data, new candidate(s), before/after task outcomes, negative controls and reasons for selection/rejection. Simulate a contaminated dataset and verify quarantine; simulate evaluator tampering and verify a hard stop. Re-run checkpoint loading and at least one experiment on the actual Windows hardware; optionally test a tiny deterministic CPU recipe for reproducibility. Report GPU training only if the GPU really ran it.

**Acceptance:** A reproducible model-creation and evaluation experiment with honest result, original-model provenance and model-independent verified outcomes; evaluation/host boundaries intact; clean rollback; bounded resources; detailed owner-facing record; no automatic production promotion or unauthorized background operation. A *true learned-model advantage* is the scientific success goal but not a result that can be fabricated.

## 76. Batch integration and evidence required for NEXT-021–025

Separate preregistration/product/evidence commits for each milestone, plus an integrated package/UI test and canonical handoff. A single Sol activation may implement all five only as practical and safe; keep partial frozen products resumable if usage or technical blockers intervene. New language, planner and Masters must share a common versioned `TaskReceipt`, `KnowledgeEvidence`, `ModelProvenance`, `ExperimentRecord` and `QualificationState` format. Exercise complete end-to-end native-only task flow with no pretrained calls, plus explicit optional reference-mode comparator without project privacy leakage. Preserve oldest protected replays, new evaluator-owned unfamiliar suites and all failures. Record user-visible workflows, model-only and system-level metrics, resource use, point-in-time accepted baseline and which features are still shadow. Publish no packages or code without owner permission. **Argon independently reviews the entire frozen batch, produces separate per-update and integrated verdicts, and sends consolidated repairs to Sol.**

# PART XIII — LONG-RANGE PROGRAM AFTER NEXT-025

## 77. Capability release sequence beyond the immediate batch

Beyond the first native learning vertical slices, advance in **measured capability tracks**, not arbitrary stage counts. A task may belong to several tracks, but the product report must show which exact outcome improved.

**Native language:** larger and broader licensed/authorized text corpora, tokenizer quality, short constrained responses, language-model perplexity *and* task utility, longer context and coherent prose, multi-turn grounded conversation, original native code/text generation, multilingual translation and eventually vision/audio with independently suitable datasets. Any leap from 65 parameters to millions or a billion requires data and compute preflight, not just ambition.

**Native research:** original query planning, lexical/semantic retrieval, source credibility, contradiction analysis, cross-document synthesis, source freshness, multi-step evidence recheck, verified procedure promotion, authorized isolated internet research, translation and reusable knowledge. Measure factual support, source reliability, verification cost and real new knowledge usefulness on future tasks.

**Native engineering:** original source navigation/policy decisions → project understanding and dependency graph → natural-language code explanation → source-grounded code proposals → compile/test in qualified guest → minimal repair loops → multi-file integration → safe automated project engineering → controlled self-development. Keep version-specific C++, Unreal, Verse, Unity, Blender, Rust, JS/TS, Python and PLATZ specialists as qualification targets. Evaluate actual successful application modifications on unseen codebases, not guessed lines of code.

**Model factory:** reproducible train-from-scratch architecture, data eligibility, data generation with independent validators, CPU and GPU training, checkpoint/optimizer lineage, protected evaluation, model competition, collaborative/mixture policies, long-horizon curricula, idle training, automatic experiment choice, qualified role replacement and owner-controlled rollback. Measure full task outcome and reduce dependency on external developers and pretrained runtime.

**Task autonomy:** personal owner-request reasoning, typed DAG execution, parallel scouts, reviewer, recovery, permissioned local tools, isolated network/guest actions, safe scheduling, time budget, stop/revoke controls and resource tracking. First solve one read-only bounded task; then one owner-approved isolated software patch; only later consider unattended code evolution.

**Creativity and multimodal:** project-specific original writing assistants, book bibles, sprite/animation consistency, 3D geometry/rigging, audio/video workflows, game playing and game development; separately train/qualify image/vision/speech policies when evidence and compute warrant. Never present a prompt that merely calls another model as proof of original creative intelligence.

**Personal assistance:** optional private long-term memory, explicit consent categories and user-directed local tasks, separate from general public knowledge/training. Do not grant email/calendar/payment/account tools by default; privacy and owner controls precede any personal agent autonomy.

## 78. Research hypotheses to test, not presume

The older plans described a “file supremacy,” a zero-model flywheel, 1B specialists matching vastly larger models, and a large hierarchy acting like trillions of parameters. Preserve them as **testable hypotheses only**:

* Externalized verified factual/procedural memory may improve accuracy per parameter and reduce model training burden on narrow tasks.

* Distinct small specialist models may outperform a single larger model at the same compute budget on certain workflows.

* Compiling frequently verified workflows into deterministic answer cards may reduce repeated inference costs.

* Original language policies may learn useful action representations from far less data than broad generative models, depending on domain and output constraints.

* Routing and cooperation may improve completed-task efficiency when per-agent overhead is accounted for.

* Correctly curated negative examples may improve calibration and abstention but may not improve previously unsolved tasks.

* Automated improvement may discover useful candidates, but only a controlled evaluation can distinguish learning from overfit or search luck.

* No mathematical “effective parameter count” should be claimed from multiplication by a retrieval/procedure factor; compare actual task outcomes with uncertainty bands.

Register hypothesis, dataset, strong baseline, test conditions, resource assumptions, expected failure modes, minimum useful effect and independence plan before spending major GPU resources.

## 79. A practical crossover with the legacy ATLAS

Define **feature crossover** rather than a single misleading version comparison: (a) local chat and model selection, (b) personal opt-in memory, (c) project-aware coding and tested code artifacts, (d) knowledge/procedure retrieval, (e) training and model history, (f) bounded multi-agent Task Graph, (g) independent Arena/dev workflows, (h) safe desktop packaging and usability. At each category, report old verified behavior, Next accepted behavior, reviewer scope and remaining gaps. Only declare ATLAS Next functionally superior when the practical everyday tasks meet or exceed old capability **with no regression to the old security flaws**. Then, separately, report **native-original intelligence crossover** when an original learned policy has proven useful unfamiliar-task gains against strong baselines and reference-independent inference.

## 80. Roadmap flexibility and failure handling

Five features per Sol batch is the owner's throughput target. If a model is demonstrably too weak, a private dataset is ineligible, a full native language model is compute-infeasible, or an isolation boundary is unverified, the corresponding milestone must produce a **useful smaller predecessor or honest blocker**, with evidence and a revised experiment—not five superficial checkmarks. Security-critical failures stop affected dependent work. A reviewer can accept a sound early milestone and block later dependent features. Subsequent batches repair the root cause then continue. Never silently lower the metric after a failure, refit on the protected suite, or relabel reference-assisted code as native work.

# PART XIV — ACCEPTANCE TESTS, SCIENTIFIC SCORECARDS AND RELEASE CONTRACTS

## 81. Feature status and Definition of Done

Use explicit states such as `NOT_STARTED`, `IN_PROGRESS`, `IMPLEMENTED_UNTESTED`, `AUTHOR_TESTS_PASSING`, `PENDING_INDEPENDENT_REVIEW`, `REPAIRS_REQUIRED`, `INDEPENDENTLY_ACCEPTED_WITH_LIMITATIONS`, `INDEPENDENTLY_ACCEPTED`, `QUALIFIED_FOR_SPECIFIC_TASK`, `OWNER_APPROVED_ACTIVE`, `BLOCKED` and `RETIRED`. Do not confuse author tests with independent acceptance or independent acceptance of a host feature with model qualification. Skipped, unavailable, mocked, timed-out or never-executed checks are not passes.

A feature is done only when: real implementation and entry point exist; authorized user task works on representative real data; security boundaries and permissions are enforced; success and error paths have tests; saved state and restart behavior match claims; metrics are measured; docs match source; latest frozen candidate and tests are recoverable; required independent review passes; any deployment/new authority is separately owner-approved. A proposed model with untested weights is not an operational AI feature. A screen that shows an inactive button is not a completed workflow.

## 82. Per-milestone evidence contract

Each implementation record should have `milestone_id`, `requirement_ids`, `author`, `baseline_sha`, `protocol_sha`, `product_sha`, `evidence_sha`, `changed_paths`, `source_tests`, `test_command`, `environment`, `seed`, `dataset_snapshot`, `trained_weights_sha`, `model_provenance`, `resource_usage`, `result_counts`, `wrong_deliveries`, `false_abstentions`, `known_limitations`, `security_controls`, `independent_review_sha`, `reviewer`, `verdict`, `blocked_dependencies`, `next_assignment`. Use real exact hashes, not placeholder SHA strings. Store enough detailed receipts to reproduce tests without embedding personal or hidden evaluator cases in Git. The owner report should explain the main result in ordinary language and separate what the native model did from host fallback.

## 83. Metrics that matter

**Task quality:** useful completed end-to-end tasks, exact supported answers, incorrect trusted deliveries, correct and false abstentions, evidence errors, source staleness, cross-project leakage, test pass/fail, accepted code artifacts and owner interventions. **Model utility:** model-only and full-system outcomes, learned action counterfactuals, generalization by unfamiliar family, calibration/Brier/ECE, meaningful baseline margin, training-data burden, checkpoint stability. **Efficiency:** p50/p95/p99 latency, warm vs cold, CPU/GPU memory, total energy where measurable, tool calls, tokens, resident model load time, number of external inference calls, owner time, compute per correct task. **Autonomy:** number of permitted steps achieved without intervention, successful recovery, correct stop/revoke, unsafe-action attempts denied, number of tasks relying on reference models. **Knowledge:** provenance completeness, retrievability after restart, procedure-transfer success, contradiction detection, stale-retraction correctness and independent source credibility. **UX:** can the owner discover and complete the workflow, undo errors, interpret model qualification and recover from failures.

## 84. Mandatory adversarial classes

Challenge malformed UTF-8/Unicode, BOM/CRLF/UTF-16 offsets, long files, duplicate functions, overload binding, Python parser grammar, missing imports, stale citations, switched project/task/receipt, path canonicalization, symlink/ADS/trailing-dot variants, response injection, source prompt injection, malformed model checkpoint and optimizer state, dataset contamination, train/test family overlap, unauthorized training, exfiltration attempt, cancellation during write, corrupt journal, out-of-memory, GPU device loss, Windows restart, stale reference model labels, same prompt with different expected context, and relative imports. Protected tests must include cases not disclosed to the candidate author. Test real compiled source or runtime behavior when the claim is about code correctness.

## 85. Owner-level demonstration suite

**Demo A:** native-only simple fact/procedure returns correct sourced answer without loading external inference. **B:** a new natural-language query is mapped to an original model’s structured action with evidence verification. **C:** an unfamiliar project is inspected with exact symbols and dependencies. **D:** a bounded multi-step research task detects contradiction and preserves provenance. **E:** a learned policy selects a better plan or a cheaper safe path than a baseline, or reports a tie honestly. **F:** model lab trains original weights from random initialization and reloads a byte-identical checkpoint. **G:** a deliberately weaker refit is rejected, and incumbent remains unchanged. **H:** cross-family protected test evaluates a trained role independently. **I:** a user requests a complete copy-ready code artifact with tested or explicitly untested status; editing requires separate approval. **J:** protected action is denied absent owner authorization. **K:** private memory is not included in public knowledge or training. **L:** a corrupted task or checkpoint is safely quarantined and recoverable. **M:** the Windows app exposes real task/model/experiment status. **N:** a model/procedure lesson can be retrieved after restart. **O:** an integrated native-only workflow demonstrates causal model contributions. **P:** future isolated software evolution and approved background training pass separate safety qualifications. These are staged goals; do not claim all are implemented today.

## 86. Packaging and reproducible environment

Windows package builds must include complete needed runtimes/assemblies/resources, manifest digest validation, real launch and UI automation checks under the supported PowerShell 5.1 and 7 paths where feasible. Test a writable unprivileged profile, install/unpack, open project, import file, run a relevant task, export evidence, close/relaunch, recover history, and error/cancel. Freeze dependencies/lockfiles, provide exact launch/build commands and capture configuration. Keep a clean source checkout or disposable clone. Avoid shipping training-only private/protected datasets and unqualified shadow models as if they were active. Verify installer signing/updates only when such systems exist; no claim of code signature if the package is unsigned.

## 87. Release notes and owner report obligations

Every batch report must provide: exact accepted baseline; provisional and accepted revisions; what new user-visible tasks work; what original weights were trained; what tests and evidence support that; what external models were used and why; task outcomes vs strong baselines; wrong deliveries and false abstentions; regressions; unresolved blockers; privacy and permission impact; resource costs; incomplete UI/model features; replay commands; where permanent docs/knowledge changed; and the next concise user-relayed agent prompt. A useful negative result should be reported prominently, not buried under hundreds of passing tests.

# PART XV — EXTERNAL SOL/ARGON DEVELOPMENT, REVIEW INDEPENDENCE AND PERMANENT HANDOFF

## 88. Why external developers are temporary

For now, GPT-6.2 **Sol Extra High** is the primary major developer and **Gemini Argon Orchestrator** is the independent adversarial reviewer. They are **development tools**, not ATLAS's own runtime intelligence. Their externally generated code and documents may build ATLAS-native models; this does not mean native inference borrowed pretrained weights. As ATLAS grows, its own models should perform an increasing portion of planning, source inspection, candidate training and tests. Sol/Argon should become optional teachers/auditors, but no independent safety gate should disappear merely because ATLAS is self-improving.

## 89. Five-substantial-update batch protocol

Normal cycle: (1) assigned Sol reads the master plan and canonical handoff and verifies Git state; (2) when the previous Argon-authored small candidate needs review, Sol independently reviews and accepts or repairs it; (3) Sol may locally repair a known defective dependency and proceed after objective author tests without waiting for another intermediate Argon session; (4) Sol plans about **five substantial owner-visible updates** around accepted goals; (5) each has a separate preregistration when relevant, tests, product commit, evidence commit, dependency map and rollback boundary; (6) Sol freezes an integrated batch and assigns it to Argon; (7) Argon independently reviews **every update and the integrated system in one activation**, with per-update verdicts, fresh private challenges and consolidated repairs; (8) Sol repairs any issues as a grouped task and can proceed to a further provisional development batch if local gates pass; (9) nothing unaccepted is described as independently accepted or promoted. If safety-critical defects occur, stop affected dependent work and report.

Argon's priority is review; a small useful enhancement is optional only when owner-approved by current handoff and does not dilute independent review. Do not fabricate extra “NEXT” labels to reach five. Do not stop after the first small milestone just to request another developer prompt. A batch should represent real new user workflows, learned-model abilities, trustworthy data, or qualified tool effects, not merely extra parser cases or decorative UI.

## 90. Single-writer and exact repo authority

Live repository: `C:\ATLAS\ATLAS-Next`. Authoritative working handoff: `docs/ai/ATLAS_HANDOFF.md`. This canonical long-term plan: `docs/ai/ATLAS_MASTER_PLAN.md`. Review process: `docs/ai/ATLAS_REVIEW_PROTOCOL.md`. Owner report: `docs/ai/ATLAS_OWNER_REPORT.md`. Evidence logs: `docs/TEST_EVIDENCE.md` and `docs/evidence/`. The master plan is long-lived intent; the handoff is a short-lived **operational work order**. They serve different purposes. Avoid concurrent writes and conflicting custody. Only the assigned agent edits a shared worktree and handoff. Each review is of a **frozen exact product commit**, and the reviewer cannot alter that candidate while issuing its verdict. Reproducible analysis uses clean checkouts and keeps private evaluator inputs outside Git. Protect old baseline commits and backups, preserve original ATLAS, and never force-push or rewrite acceptance history without owner authorization.

## 91. No cross-agent screen control, waiting or monitoring

Owner has explicitly prohibited Sol and Argon from using Computer Use, browser/UI screen automation, chat navigation or any direct cross-agent session interaction to send messages. Neither agent may wait, poll or monitor the counterpart's work, nor claim to have delivered a message it did not deliver. Instead, each completes its own assigned phase, records full technical detail and exact hashes in handoff/owner report, and gives the owner **one short copy-ready prompt for the other agent**. The owner manually relays it. The next agent must read the canonical file and perform real work. No claim of unattended coordination unless a separate actual authorized scheduler exists.

## 92. Permanent agent startup checklist (read every activation)

1. Read the entire `docs/ai/ATLAS_MASTER_PLAN.md` through its verified sections, or confirm its digest and read every section not previously resolved in this activation; do not rely on a stale chat summary.

2. Read `docs/ai/ATLAS_HANDOFF.md`, `ATLAS_OWNER_REPORT.md`, `ATLAS_REVIEW_PROTOCOL.md`, product vision/requirements/roadmap, active review evidence and relevant source.

3. Verify branch, `git status`, HEAD, worktree, accepted baseline, provisional candidates, private/evaluator access boundary and current assigned writer.

4. Detect new owner instructions or conflicting old documents; record the resolution without erasing source provenance.

5. Identify precisely what phase is authorized: review, repair, preregistration, implementation, test, acceptance reporting or documentation.

6. Work against the requested frozen product and bounded scope. Write real code and run real tests. Record all failures and skipped tests.

7. Keep master plan up to date **only after explicit owner intent changes**, with a changelog. Never silently weaken doctrine or overwrite it with generated model goals.

8. Before ending, update owner report and canonical handoff, commit artifacts separately, specify next writer and short prompt. Stop; do not watch the other agent.

## 93. Master document installation and revision-control policy

During the next Sol activation, place the delivered Markdown master file at **`docs/ai/ATLAS_MASTER_PLAN.md`** within the actual ATLAS Next repository. Do not discard the supplied original document; keep an archived dated snapshot and SHA-256, or place an external source manifest when the source file itself is already owner-provided. Record a dedicated doc-only commit. Update `docs/PRODUCT_VISION.md`, `docs/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/REQUIREMENTS.md` (if present), and the handoff to **link to** this canonical plan, rather than duplicate dozens of conflicting pages. Place a short `ATLAS_MASTER_PLAN_README.md` with quick rules and section links; store a hash-pinned developer bootstrap prompt. Existing master/legacy docs become cited historical inputs, not live operational prompts. Do not delete historical records without approval.

When changing owner doctrine: version the master; add change note stating exact request and affected sections; preserve old version; update source crosswalk; have a second reviewer check for accidental loss of safety requirements; ensure new agents re-read the changed chapters. **“Never forget” is implemented through permanent versioned files, citations, hashes, startup checks and tests—not a claim that a language model's conversation memory is infallible.**

# PART XVI — END-TO-END OWNER SCENARIOS AND EXPECTED EVIDENCE

## 94. Scenario catalog, goals and gates

These scenarios are long-term acceptance journeys. Unless an independently accepted milestone explicitly implements the workflow, label it **PLANNED** or **RESTRICTED**; a future example is not proof that ATLAS currently performs it.

**S-01 — Build Unreal character controller:** Owner selects a real Unreal Engine project and versions. Project Inspector reads authorized source and configuration. Engineering Master maps input, movement and replication; a C++/Blueprint specialist proposes complete code and editor settings. A qualified guest compiles/tests using UnrealHeaderTool where available. Reviewer checks movement/network behavior and exact dependencies. Owner approves the final diff. Evidence: correct files, API citations, compile/test logs, reversible patch.

**S-02 — UEFN Verse gameplay device:** Identify project Verse version, devices, events, game-mode constraints and expected behavior. Produce paste-ready Verse with precise device bindings and no invented APIs. Run appropriate compile/static checks in an authorized environment. Evidence: compile output, supported device names, tested event behavior or disclosed limitation.

**S-03 — Unity save/inventory system:** Inspect Unity version, assembly definitions, scripts, prefabs and serializers; generate complete C# components, configurable values, scene integration and tests. Review save corruption, nulls, migration and domain reload. Evidence: test fixtures, integration plan, source citations and rollback.

**S-04 — Rust service repair without breaking the API:** Inspect Cargo workspace, interfaces and test suite; use native project-plan specialist to identify likely area; propose minimal patch; build/test in isolated worktree; measure runtime and public API. Independent review challenges regressions. Evidence: specific commit, cargo results and before/after API check.

**S-05 — Research a graphics subsystem:** Research Master determines evidence requirements, inspects approved local source/docs and optionally approved public official pages through isolated fetch; compares versions and constraints; records claim-level citations; returns an architecture decision with tradeoffs and unknowns. Evidence: current sources, contradictions, tested benchmark or hypothesis tags.

**S-06 — Create a PLATZ voxel survival prototype:** Future PLATZ Engine and ATLAS interface are installed and independently accepted. ATLAS reads the PLATZ project schema and proposes versioned scenes, voxel assets, inventory, collision and logic. PLATZ Studio/Runtime validates visual-code parity and resource usage. Owner approves. Evidence: separate repo boundaries, valid project data, actual runtime replay.

**S-07 — Consistent sprite pack:** Owner specifies palette/style/character; creative specialist proposes sprites and animation frames. Check dimensions, alpha, color constraints, pose consistency, names and project rights; preserve editable source and variants. Evidence: visual owner review plus technical export tests; model origin disclosed.

**S-08 — Long fantasy novel:** Create consented story bible and narrative plan; track characters, locations, magic rules, events, timeline and chapter revisions; original language model or explicitly labeled reference assists drafting; continuity reviewer catches contradictions and preserves writer ownership. Evidence: scene/character source references, edits and continuity report.

**S-09 — Authorized knowledge refresh:** ATLAS notices an expiry policy, proposes network query under an approved scheduled lease, fetches isolated external sources, cross-checks specific claims, downgrades stale knowledge and promotes only verified updates. Evidence: old/new snapshots, source timestamps, exact support, reviewer verdict and reversible change.

**S-10 — Original native-model improvement:** Model Foundry identifies a tested weakness, gathers eligible independent outcomes, freezes holdout, trains an original checkpoint, compares with incumbent/rules/linear, rejects weaker work and retains only justified candidates. Evidence: data rights, model weights, seeded training and independent protected results.

**S-11 — Bounded self-development:** ATLAS inspects its own allowed application source, proposes a patch to improve a measured issue, creates isolated candidate branch, tests, solicits independent review and requests owner approval. No self-modification of security/hidden evaluator. Evidence: signed/hashed diff, tests, rollback.

**S-12 — Cooperative software evolution arena:** Multiple bounded development branches apply distinct strategies over ten updates each; independent testing measures useful quality and cost; successful changes are merged only after compatibility review. Evidence: 10-step branch provenance, failures, scorecards and independent verdicts.

**S-13 — Private memory without training leakage:** Owner explicitly grants a category and saves a preference, then asks Personal ATLAS to use it in a draft. Prove only the authorized category was read, no content entered public knowledge or model training, and deletion/export works with declared backup limitations. Evidence: permission audit and memory store tests.

**S-14 — Complete desktop app creation:** Owner requests a program; native Coordinator plans requirements and UI, specialists create source/build/tests, reviewer checks artifacts, host previews in safe isolation, owner accepts. Must preserve platform toolchain provenance, provide complete code and a genuinely tested package rather than text-only mockups.

**S-15 — Safe game-playing experiments:** In approved simulation, model reads observation, chooses legal actions, learns from virtual reward, compares with baseline and records results; no real payments or unauthorized accounts. Evidence: game action logs, environment seed, safety and stop control.

**S-16 — Verified ordinary user question:** Native-only ATLAS receives a fact/procedure request, finds a canonical answer card, verifies provenance, returns correct answer with no external inference; a separate case requiring learned interpretation shows the original model’s causal contribution. Evidence: counterfactual host-only comparison and model-origin trace.

**S-17 — Scientific paper comparison and translation:** Authorized PDFs, figures and data are parsed by installed appropriate tools; multilingual passage translated with aligned originals; model proposes claims, trusted math checks units/sample sizes; reviewer flags missing evidence. Evidence: page citations and no unsupported causal claims.

**S-18 — Real model training UI:** Owner chooses Improve a Model, selects native role and resource budget, watches real loss/validation/VRAM, pauses/resumes safely, compares checkpoints and sees accepted/rejected outcome with exact dataset and evaluator receipt. No fabricated telemetry or automatic promotion.

**S-19 — Accessibility and 4K desktop:** Owner moves between chat, project, knowledge, model lab and tasks on multiple monitors and DPI settings; all tested controls work, keyboard/screen-reader output is meaningful, no clipped panels or dead buttons. Evidence: automated UIA plus manual spot-check.

**S-20 — Safety stop and account isolation:** Attempt an unapproved host command, external account, payment operation or private-file access through adversarial instructions; trusted host denies it, logs minimally and preserves source boundaries. Evidence: negative red-team tests, not just policy prose.

# PART XVII — REQUIREMENT TRACEABILITY, DOCUMENT PROVENANCE AND SOURCE RECONCILIATION

## 95. How historical instructions were reconciled

The attached source set spans different ATLAS generations, early Astra implementations, original ATLAS Milestone 11, the PLATZ ecosystem and recent ATLAS Next native intelligence. This consolidation preserves **enduring intent and distinct functional requirements** while retiring conflicting historical execution orders. The following transformations are binding:

 1. **Old developer names:** GPT Astra Ultra/GPT-6.1 Sol historical prompts become reference documents; current external implementation owner is **GPT-6.2 Sol Extra High**, with **Gemini Argon Orchestrator** as independent batch reviewer. Neither names ATLAS's own native Master.

 2. **Old repository:** References to `7742514`, legacy `C:\ATLAS\Entire ATLAS`, earlier standalone snapshots or empty-repo startup are historical. Current implementation repository is `C:\ATLAS\ATLAS-Next`. Do not migrate/reinitialize based on an old prompt.

 3. **Model-centered bootstrap:** Older directives prioritized restoring Qwen chat/Ollama and model downloads. The present priority is **original ATLAS-native learning, language, plans and self-improvement**. Optional local reference remains useful as a separate interface and benchmark, but cannot be counted as native intelligence.

 4. **Scale floor:** Older “all specialists must be at least 1B, Masters 3B–12B” is reclassified as a *research architecture target*. Sub-1B policies are allowed when effective; train larger models only with defensible data and hardware evidence.

 5. **Effective parameter equivalence:** “1B = 80B” and “12B = 3T” are not accepted scientific facts or automatic marketing claims. They are optional experimentally falsifiable comparisons on a defined task and cost basis.

 6. **Automatic knowledge supremacy:** Canonical files are preferred factual/procedural evidence only when current, in-scope and verified. They never outrank owner permissions, law, host security, or newer corrected evidence.

 7. **Automatic model promotion:** Historical training council scripts mentioning automatic Stage 13 promotion are now interpreted as **candidate promotion proposals**, requiring independent qualification and owner/host authorization. No self-approval.

 8. **Security:** Broad old host/Developer/Arena automation is not grandfathered into Next. Unsafe Windows containment findings remain regression cases. Isolation and privileged tools require fresh independent qualification.

 9. **UI rewrite:** Historical Tauri/React/WinUI shell options are not instructions to discard the current .NET WPF product. Preserve tested current code; add interfaces or migrate only when justified, reviewed and owner-authorized.

10. **Personal vault:** Old v5 personal biography import is *not* a grant to read/import actual private data. Consent, encryption, scope separation and explicit owner action remain mandatory. Do not pull private source materials into this master file.

11. **PLATZ:** The attached ATLAS/PLATZ manual describes a complete PLATZ future product, but ATLAS's current milestone does not own that source tree. PLATZ's own master directive prevails on engine implementation details.

12. **Schedules and fixed benchmarks:** Older aggressive schedules, mandatory milliseconds/VRAM numbers, 2,000 models, infinite knowledge, full autonomy and “100% correctness” are experimental ambitions only. Report measured conditions and blockers.

13. **Agent prompt relays:** Any older instruction telling Sol/Astra to control the other model's chat via screen/browser tools is retired. The owner manually relays short prompts; agents write full assignments to the canonical handoff.

14. **NEXT update numbering:** All historically completed/rejected/accepted milestones retain their actual numbering. The immediate native-first batch after NEXT-020 is **NEXT-021–025**, conditional on the true independent review status. Do not rerun old NEXT-010R or NEXT-014 tasks unless required by an actual regression or bounded repair.
