* `01_GPT_SOL_FULL_IMPLEMENTATION_PROMPT_V5.md`: broad original-ATLAS Developer/Trainer/Just Do It, 12 vaults, 19 privacy categories, Training Council, model catalogue, memory and pairwise response semantics. Retain future functional vision, not stale ZIP import or old Sol identity instructions.

* `ATLAS_PLATZ_COMPLETE_PRODUCT_MANUAL(2).docx`: comprehensive owner experience, 105 product topics, creative/game workflows, separate PLATZ Engine system, concrete scenarios and permissions; integrated across Parts I, VI, IX, X, XI, XVI. Its earlier NEXT-003 status is obsolete.

* `ATLAS_Complete_Master_Specification_and_Prompt (1)(2).docx`: 124-page five-tier sovereign-model vision with 12 vaults, 20 cluster Masters, 100 domain Masters, proposed specialist catalog, training council and ambitions. Preserve role catalogs and positive goals; quantitative EPE and fixed performance are **hypotheses**, not current facts.

* `ATLAS_Unified_Master_Specification_v2_0(1).docx`: 39-page source-aware reconciliation with canonical role/vault catalogs, 28 missions and 30 historical test concepts; preserves usable catalog data in appendices while correcting old unrealistic assumptions.

* Current ATLAS Next source/reports from user conversation and `ATLAS-Next-main (8).zip`: operational snapshot through NEXT-020; checked against source archive. Git, live handoff and future independent verdict override this static snapshot.

The historical source files should remain **read-only archival references**, with a machine-readable manifest listing original filenames, hashes and whether their content was merged, revised or intentionally retired. Do not embed entire sensitive personal files or hidden evaluator data into this document.

## 97. Requirement families and coverage trace

`ID-01–08` original native intelligence, identity, external facts/procedures, broker, hybrid retrieval and evidence sufficiency; `ID-09–15` source credibility, staging verified web finds, corrections, summaries, personal storage and translation; `ID-16–23` Master planning, parallel scouts, project inspector, specialist execution, independent review and iterative synthesis; `ID-24–32` model creation/training, evaluation, protected data, comparator controls, scheduler, checkpoint/rollback and resource budgets; `ID-33–40` weakness diagnosis, opt-in idle training, validated training data, Training Observatory, Prompt Lab, Model Arena, promotion gate/rollback, and sandboxed capability packs; `ID-41–50` *(v3.2 RECONSTRUCTED_FROM_CONTEXT via Appendix K verbatim list)* adaptive external-memory context (8K–64K), hardware resource manager, Windows desktop + optional private server, 4K–720p responsive UI, truthful model/provider registry, zero silent cloud proxying, privacy/filesystem/archive security, repeatable session recovery, real packaged app with no mock features, and inspectable change-provenance trails. Preserve the exact original `08_REQUIREMENTS_AND_ACCEPTANCE.md` IDs through a separate crosswalk in the repo, but never mark a requirement complete without current executable evidence. An agent modifying a covered subsystem must update its traceability record and tests.

## 98. What the owner should be able to say to the mature ATLAS

*“Use your own intelligence, not Qwen, to understand this project; look up the exact current sources; propose a correct plan; make a complete code change in a safe approved environment; test it; ask an independent reviewer; show me what passed and what you are unsure about; improve the specialist that made the mistake; preserve the lesson as a verified procedure; and be ready to use it next time without my writing another development prompt.”* This is the product destination, not its present verified ability.

## 99. A permanent ‘do not forget’ control test

On a fresh GPT Sol session, fresh Argon session or future ATLAS restart, a bootstrap must verify that the canonical master is installed, digest-pinned and current; the latest accepted product and pending review are correctly identified; the agent can explain the distinction between original and pretrained models, facts and procedures, owner accounts and agent isolation, native intelligence goals and deterministic host work, PLATZ separation, and next bounded milestone. Then the agent must prove its changes do not silently contradict these rules. If the document is unavailable or stale, stop and request recovery rather than pretend the rules are remembered. Test this startup behavior in automated development checks. The owner can always revise doctrine; old revisions remain archived for audit.

# PART XVIII — EXPANDED BINDING PRODUCT CONTRACTS AND OPERATING DETAILS

> **Status of this part:** This is an expansion of the current ATLAS owner vision, reconciling the historical source package. It defines target behavior and engineering acceptance contracts, **not** a claim that ATLAS Next already implements these features. If a clause would loosen a stricter security rule elsewhere, the stricter permission and review rule wins. Existing independent review evidence still defines current completion. This section should be maintained alongside Parts I–XVII, not silently copied as a second program.

## 100. Owner's exact final objective and non-substitution rules

The product is **ATLAS — Adaptive Tiered Learning, Analysis & Search**. The desired end state is a locally owned, increasingly independent, broadly useful AI ecosystem, not a collection of agents that simply route prompts to Qwen/Gemini/GPT. ATLAS learns original weights on the owner's hardware, develops specializations when they demonstrably improve outcomes, uses externally filed and validated facts and reasoning procedures, assembles bounded teams, assesses results by tests rather than confidence, and gradually undertakes more complex approved tasks. Real value includes daily assistance, reliable source-grounded thinking, software development, creative production, project continuity, research, training, and eventually controlled computer use. It must be straightforward enough that an ordinary owner asks for a result instead of managing a laboratory manually.

The following distinctions must remain visible in every feature and status report:

* **ATLAS-original from-scratch neural inference**: checkpoint initialized without pretrained weights; exact architecture, tokenizer, data and training lineage retained. Using standard GPU numerical libraries does not disqualify originality.

* **ATLAS-adapted pretrained inference**: adapter/LoRA/fine-tune of someone else's foundation; useful as a tool but not original weights from scratch.

* **Local external reference**: a Qwen, Gemma, GPT-OSS or other installed model that performs generation or decisions. Local hosting is not model originality.

* **Hosted external inference**: externally processed input with explicit approval and data-flow disclosure; cannot be the invisible native path.

* **Deterministic zero-neural behavior**: a trusted search, parser, compiler, unit converter, database query, or recipe executor; useful and sometimes preferable, but not evidence of a trained model.

* **External engineering-agent labor**: Sol/Argon may design and write ATLAS source; their work is not evidence that ATLAS's own models performed those engineering acts.

A feature is *present* if code exists; *demonstrated* if it executed a meaningful real workflow; *author-verified* if its author tested it; *independently qualified* after a separate reviewer and protected evidence; and *authorized for production* only if its own release, trust and permission gates pass. These states never collapse into the same badge.

A mature ATLAS should increasingly handle a request in **native-original mode** without using a pretrained model. A separate optional reference-model lane may remain available for comparison, fallbacks and owner-directed tasks, but must never silently replace an original-model result and then claim native capability. An original-model-only task should succeed or candidly abstain when its ability is insufficient. The system should show how much the accepted result depended on original learned inference, deterministic tools, external knowledge, local pretrained inference, hosted services, or human decisions.

## 101. Comprehensive everyday user journeys and expected receipts

### 101.1 'Just Do It' is the default, with Command Center and Advanced layers

The **Just Do It** surface accepts ordinary questions, files, folders or project goals and presents a simple usable answer with meaningful status and citations. Users should not have to understand routing graphs, token budgets, training datasets, adapters or taxonomy. The **Command Center** reveals project, task and model choices; the **Advanced/Research** surface exposes exact contracts, checkpoints, runtime graphs, evaluation data, permissions, and logs. All three reflect the same backend state and are not three separate implementations.

A user can open ATLAS, select a project, ask a question, see which source or original model answered, inspect evidence, edit or correct the task, and optionally let ATLAS continue an approved sequence. The app should remember previously authorized workspace context across restarts, not silently expand permission into new folders. A sidebar should always reveal the active project, model provenance and whether anything is being transmitted externally. The owner can cancel tasks, pause approved training, revoke permissions, and export a complete reproducible task package.

### 101.2 First-run setup

The setup wizard should detect Windows and hardware, GPU model and available VRAM, RAM, storage and compute runtimes, installed approved reference models, and permission boundaries. It asks for an ATLAS home directory, backup path, local-project import scopes, data retention, telemetry preferences, and whether *optional* loopback model inference is enabled. It must not create online accounts, start downloads, access credentials or subscribe to external services by default. It should initialize a small local demo project with safe read-only sample files and explain native vs reference vs deterministic execution without inflating claims.

The first launch should offer a valid core no-cloud experience even if no pretrained model or CUDA stack is present. GUI errors must identify unavailable capability and next approved setup action, not substitute synthetic success. GPU training is opt-in with actual budgets; home IP–concealing remote research requires a separately authorized isolated environment.

### 101.3 Task classes

A mature owner can ask ATLAS to: research a scientific topic with multiple independent sources and citations; reconcile conflicting historical measurements; read an uploaded book, report, spreadsheet or code repository; locate an API method and explain safe integration; prepare a C# or Python game script with complete imports and configuration; propose, test and roll back a patch in an approved guest; write or edit a book with continuity; plan a 3D asset workflow; create sprites or character concepts in a separately qualified generation stack; study an unfamiliar project and build an actionable feature plan; compare two candidate model outputs; train one original specialist and explain its actual evaluation; prepare a verified reusable debugging procedure; translate a foreign-language source without breaking names or code; and inspect old work weeks later without losing source context. Each requested class has a declared support status rather than being implied by a role name.

### 101.4 Five owner-facing result artifacts

For nontrivial tasks, the user receives: (1) the actual requested content/artifact; (2) an optional exact-evidence inspector; (3) a provenance and model-attribution summary; (4) a test/verification and limitations summary; and (5) a durable task record with resumption/review instructions. The default interface hides the last four until needed; they remain accessible in Advanced. Content that is only suggested, generated, untested or partially complete must be labeled accordingly.

## 102. Sovereign intelligence: detailed five-tier model hierarchy and research interpretation

The historical five-tier sovereignty proposal is retained as a **research architecture**, not proof of scale equivalence or a mandate to train thousands of billion-parameter replicas.

**Tier 5 — canonical file and deterministic substrate:** The authoritative data, verified procedures, permission contracts, source snapshots, tools, claims, models, evaluations, registry and provenance live here. The trusted host enforces permissions and exact validation. Files are not automatically true because they are files: provenance, authority, recency, evidence support, scope and conflict status control their use. Pure-file/0B mode can solve a task with no neural inference when an exact validated answer or safe deterministic operation is available.

**Tier 1 — principal coordination capability:** Historically envisioned as one 12B Supreme Master, with a claimed enormous effective-parameter multiplier; practically begins with a small **Primary Master** trained for verifiable task understanding, routing, budgets, decomposition and synthesis. A 12B from-scratch role is a possible long-term research ambition, not a guaranteed required size or consumer-GPU fit. It remains subordinate to trusted host approvals.

**Tier 2 — executive cluster roles:** The historical roster lists 20 clusters around major disciplines. These are capability and ownership domains; no requirement exists to materialize a separate 8–9B checkpoint per name. A role is a typed policy plus eligibility rubric, not a neural model until trained and independently qualified. The full named roster is preserved in Appendix A.

**Tier 3 — domain Masters:** A historically proposed 100-role registry across fields. Each has a role ID, owner-facing goal, accepted input types, output schema, allowed tools, required fact/procedure vaults, quality measures and failure/abstention behavior. These might be implemented by shared models, smaller specialists, deterministic tools or separately trained models depending on performance. Full proposed roster is preserved in Appendix A.

**Tier 4 — micro-specialist opportunities:** The archives describe 25 specialist families, 125 explicit examples, a 2,125-slot conceptual set, then later 2,000 distinct v4 specialty descriptions and 150 extra personal proposals in separate ZIPs (2,150 conceptual entries). These conflicting historical counts are **snapshots of proposals**, not claims of installed model weights. Retain every available original ID; do not rename/delete a proposal merely to force one count. The external v4/v5 archives mentioned in older documents are not among the present attachments and cannot be recreated verbatim; the index must mark them as `source_not_supplied` until the owner supplies them. Never fabricate their exact content. Registry search is allowed without training thousands of checkpoints.

**The three indispensable functional Master competencies**, even if tier labels evolve, are **Primary Coordination**, **Engineering/Execution**, and **Research/Verification**. Evidence and permissions remain external to the neural Master. Secondary historical roles—Knowledge, Review, Training, Personal Memory, Language/Translation, Writing, Book, Organizing, Analysis, Creator, Game Development and Domain Masters—are typed specialist competencies that must earn deployment through outcomes and resource fit. A high-level role may orchestrate multiple narrow specialists in one task graph, without all weights loaded simultaneously.

Do not accept unproven EPE formulas such as "1B = 80B", "12B = 3T", unlimited factual capacity from files, universally sub-2-ms answers, automatic <250-ms model swaps or emergency GPU release within an absolute time as established facts. Preserve these as **falsifiable historic claims** and design microbenchmarks where relevant. Use grounded tokens/sec, task success, memory, accuracy per joule, cost per correct result and resource footprint to assess alternatives.

## 103. The twenty-cluster / hundred-domain / specialist registry as an operational system

Role registry records should include `role_id`, `canonical_name`, `revision`, `family`, `scope`, `status`, `task_examples`, `competencies`, `model_lineage`, `test_suite`, `tools_allowed`, `source_vaults`, `sensitivity`, `resource_estimate`, `failure_modes`, `route_conditions`, `owner_approval_class`, `success_metrics`, `promotion_record` and `last_verified`. `status` should distinguish `catalog_proposal`, `contract_defined`, `tool_implemented`, `experimental_neural`, `independently_qualified`, `active` and `retired`; a catalog entry never appears as an active installed AI model.

An owner may request a new role in natural language. ATLAS first checks for overlapping roles and source files, suggests extending a current specialist or a new typed skill, predicts tests and data needs, and requests approval when the new role needs new tools, persistent memory, or external access. Creating a role definition must not automatically train a separate model. The role is trained when enough distinct eligible examples and a measurable improvement opportunity exist. A retired role preserves evidence and rollback history.

A full registry explorer should filter by task, tool, discipline, example, provenance, code language, qualification, deployment status and resource cost. Lookup must be indexed; never stuff thousands of descriptions into a model prompt. Any role count displayed must distinguish opportunities, contracts, actual pretrained references, adapters, original from-scratch checkpoints, qualified deployed policies and live loaded models.

The historical 20- and 100-role names in the appendices are preserved exactly where supplied. Missing v4/v5 JSONL registries are treated as external dependencies, not reconstructed from marketing claims. Scope-specific knowledge import is a different operation from executing a role or authorizing training.

## 104. Complete knowledge-vault semantics and file-first sovereignty

The twelve canonical conceptual Tier-5 vaults retain their original stable keys, with **separate physical sensitivity boundaries** when required:

* **V-01 Answer Cards:** exact or parameterized verified answers with applicability conditions, source IDs, proof type, effective dates, original task references and expiry. A card may yield no-neural response if scope and evidence remain valid. Generated prose does not establish a verified answer card.

* **V-02 Procedures/Playbooks:** reusable plans for debugging, interviewing, analysis, code inspection, training, project creation and writing. Store exact inputs/preconditions, sequencing, allowed tools, expected outputs, test oracle, failure signatures, known limits and measured outcomes on distinct tasks. A procedure is retrievable reasoning support, not a permission grant.

* **V-03 Deterministic Tools:** allowlisted, signed or hashed functions/scripts with typed arguments, accepted output shapes, bounded resources and sandbox/host authority policy. An untested tool cannot be invoked merely because a retrieved document mentions it.

* **V-04 Code Intelligence:** authorized live source trees, AST/symbol indexes, dependency and call graphs, commit identity, tests, API/library versions and verified code recipes. Historical architecture notes are never substituted for live source inspection.

* **V-05 Personal Memory:** opt-in private memories and corrections with category, retention, encryption/access policy, consent version and deletion semantics. Not stored in general Developer knowledge or trained into global weights by default.

* **V-06 STEM & Quantitative References:** formulas, units, measurement uncertainty, standards editions, calculation procedures and source reliability, with explicit jurisdiction/version when applicable.

* **V-07 Creative Bibles:** story universes, character profiles, timeline continuity, worlds, brand identity, approved style references, scenes and source relationships. Distinguish fictional canon from verified real-world facts.

* **V-08 Game and Creative Assets:** versioned sprites, models, textures, animation rigs, engine configuration, licenses and asset metadata, with separate executable/tool risk checks.

* **V-09 Verification Oracles (`atlas.vault.verification_oracles`):** independently owned benchmarks, hidden tests, frozen rubrics, evaluator lineage, and access controls in **Zone E (Protected Evaluator)**—never readable by candidate agents or training code.

* **V-10 Episodic Ledger (`atlas.vault.episodic_ledger`):** append-only hash-chained task receipts, project manifests, plans, trajectories, rollbacks, and outcomes (absorbs historical *Projects and Tasks* operational receipts alongside `V-04`).

* **V-11 Training Corpora (`atlas.vault.training_corpora`):** license-verified, decontaminated SFT/DPO datasets, split manifests, negative-outcome clusters, and consent/revocation ledgers.

* **V-12 Model Lineage & Governance (`atlas.vault.model_lineage`):** original and reference checkpoints, tokenizers, optimizer states, evaluation scorecards, ADRs, and canonical architecture/governance metadata (`docs/ai/`).

> **v3.2 Vault Reconciliation Note (resolves CONF-01):** Earlier v3.0/v3.1 drafts reused `V-09`–`V-12` in Section 104 with different meanings than Section 17 and Appendix A. `V-09`–`V-12` above are the single binding registry definitions. Optional future *Communications and Personal Workflows* remain disabled by default and, if ever owner-enabled, reside strictly inside encrypted Zone D (`V-05`) rather than colliding with `V-11`.

These descriptions supplement, not silently replace, the historical vault identifiers. The Knowledge Broker maps stable IDs and typed queries to physical files and indexes. File movement/reindexing must preserve stable logical IDs. Storage can use human-readable Markdown/JSON/JSONL as durable canonical records and SQLite FTS/vector indexes as rebuildable accelerators. Exact source copies and hashes remain available. Search should combine exact ID, lexical/FTS, semantic, symbols, links, date/validity and procedure applicability. Read only the passages needed; track token budgets and citation spans. A shortened summary must never become independent ground truth when the original is available.

### 104.1 Four-layer information access

At increasing detail: (1) domain/navigation summary, (2) section outline, (3) selected evidence excerpts, (4) full original source. Each layer identifies its parent source and offsets. A hallucinated summary or translation cannot override original evidence. Index version, method and semantic model lineage are recorded. Source staleness should trigger targeted reindex/reverification, not silent reuse.

### 104.2 Claim and procedure trust lifecycle

A claim begins `raw`/`candidate`, moves to `source_anchored`, is independently checked and may reach `verified_for_scope`; conflicting claims are `contradicted`, out-of-date ones `stale`, unsupported assertions `insufficient`, and invalid examples `rejected`. The process should support corrections, retractions, supersession and historic views. A procedure adds measured success contexts and failure logs; never declare universal success from one demonstration. Knowledge promotion is source- and claim-specific. Owner-approved web fetches begin in **candidate research**, not trusted answer storage.

### 104.3 Exact knowledge record contracts

At minimum a knowledge claim has unique ID, schema version, author/source type, immutable original-source pointer, hash, retrieval timestamp, relevant publication/validity date, project/user scope, exact supporting spans, claim text, source relation, verifier ID, verification method, status, predecessor/successor, and revocation history. A procedure has applicability, ordered step contracts, tool permissions, required evidence, expected tests, observed success/failure rates, resource budgets, versions, and provenance. An answer card includes match semantics, preconditions, content digest, verified source links and confidence/calibration notes. A source manifest tracks licensing and permitted use as well as MIME, extraction pipeline and integrity hashes.

### 104.4 Knowledge import and verification proof

When the owner supplies a technical knowledge archive, ATLAS validates manifest hashes, extraction limits, canonical path confinement, zip-slip/symlinks, licensing, scope and duplicates. It stages sources, indexes them with stable IDs and trust metadata, then proves retrieval **from the running app** with exact citations. It must not claim installation merely because a ZIP was copied or counted. Technical history documents are imported as historical reference, not assertions of current source. No default model-weight training arises from documentation import. Personal archives must be handled separately with consent and encryption when supported.

## 105. Sensitive memory, Personal ATLAS and user-sovereign identity separation

The older specifications included three **distinct** owner-facing personal modes. All remain in the long-term vision, but the privacy boundary must be stronger than convenience:

1. **Private Model Playground:** explicit temporary session with no ATLAS-side chat retention, no training, no automatic memory, no cloud and no unapproved external telemetry. A local model service may have its own logs/cache; the UI must not misleadingly promise zero persistence outside its own control. End/delete clears ATLAS-owned session traces under verified retention policy.

2. **Personal ATLAS:** opt-in, scoped, source-anchored personal memory. User can inspect, correct, delete, export, set retention and category grants. Work/project/public knowledge cannot automatically search personal records. Sensitive information stays separated from developer/training/export directories. Distinguish current fact, old claim, personal goal, derived recommendation and owner-corrected memory.

3. **Personal Style/Thinking Counterpart:** a separate explicitly authorized modeling effort to learn preferences from **selected approved** samples and actual owner votes. It must not assert that predicted preferences represent actual human choice. Training on health, identity, financial or other sensitive personal records requires separate informed affirmative authorization and should be avoided by default. Deletion/forgetting of training-derived information has special limitations and must be described truthfully.

The original 19 category names in Appendix E are a proposed personal memory organization, not permission to ingest those categories. The v5 roadmap also describes a `Get to Know Me` question flow and `My Life Progress` tracker. These must be user-initiated, skip-able, frequency-controlled, nonintrusive and clearly different from covert profiling. Every personal question explains its purpose, storage, retention and whether an actual answer changes any model. Optional family, learning, goals, games, books, creative interests and routines are categories only after the owner opts in.

**Three security zones:** (A) public/project technical evidence; (B) private temporary assistant context; (C) authorized encrypted persistent personal memory. In practice separate these in encryption keys, storage roots, access checks, indexes, application permissions, backup policy, telemetry and model-training eligibility. Backups must not recombine isolated zones into a shared dump. No private material should be included in `docs/`, Git, datasets, training logs or external API prompts without a distinct scoped owner grant.

User identity and payment are especially protected: ATLAS may not use the owner's personal accounts, credentials, subscriptions, payment methods, money or identity for autonomous operations. No autonomous trials or real-money spending. An ATLAS-created online account, if a future separately owner-approved workflow ever exists, cannot be logged into the owner's physical host. Registration/download tasks must use a genuinely isolated remote environment, and do not promise anonymity, hidden home IP or malware-free results without verifying the entire route.

## 106. Pairwise preference, comparative intelligence and human-label integrity

ATLAS's comparison studio belongs in ordinary chat **and** eligible training evaluation. Two outputs carry exact model/checkpoint IDs, data/source context, inference settings, task identifiers, execution provenance, timing, token/compute use and exclusion status. The complete historical preference vocabulary must be preserved: `A better`, `B better`, `Both good`, `Tie`, `Neither good`, `Too similar`, `Completely different`, `Abstain`, `Exclude from training`, `Exclude from research` (as a separate permission when supported), and `Skip`. Equivalent aliases from old UI flows may be supported, but stored semantics must remain unambiguous.

* **A better/B better:** actual owner relative preference on this pair, not an absolute truth label unless task ground truth independently supports it.

* **Both good/Tie:** distinct data categories; a pair can be equally acceptable or exactly tied by judgment without proving identical outputs.

* **Neither good:** both need improvement; preserve rationale and error types.

* **Too similar:** detect duplicate, near duplicate, semantically indistinguishable or nondiverse output; may trigger a distinctly different candidate, but not repeated uncontrolled generation.

* **Completely different:** a request for diversity or category separation, not automatic endorsement of either answer.

* **Abstain/Skip:** meaningful missing label; must never be silently converted into a negative/positive outcome.

* **Exclude from training/research:** propagate eligibility exclusions through derived outputs, dataset entries, caches and future lineage; training usage already completed may need additional explicit deletion/retraining policy.

An ATLAS Trainer or local critic can **recommend** one of these controls, but its stored vote must have `judge_type=ai_judge` or `predicted_preference`, not `owner_human`. Human votes require a real recorded owner action with appropriate consent. Track model bias, agreement with verified task results, pair diversity, uncertainty and distribution shifts. The comparison view should allow owners to inspect both source and generated answer differences, not just a preference percentage.

## 107. ATLAS Developer: one coherent safe programming assistant

The owner-facing ATLAS Developer is a genuine system capability, not a model name or a duplicate code chatbot. Eventually it must support: explicit project selection; a current Git/source index; language/framework detection; full-file navigation with exact symbols and insertion points; source dependency graph; retrieval of approved manuals and verified repair procedures; an original coding/reasoning specialist; generation of complete code or a bounded patch; independent tests; reviewer critique; a staged diff; rollback; owner-controlled merge; and durable learning from accepted and rejected outcomes. In native-only mode, a qualified original model does the learned planning/proposal steps; an optional reference-model lane can remain for clearly attributed comparisons.

### 107.1 Every code answer must be practically paste-ready

For requests that need code, provide the exact language, target engine/framework version, filename and relative path, fully declared imports, class/function wrapping, exact insertion/replace location, configuration variables, expected values, required assets/dependencies, build/compile commands, example invocation, and caveats. Explain how to modify each user-facing variable, the performance and compatibility implications, and what to do if a symbol already exists. If code was not compiled or executed, say **untested suggested code**. On supported authorized projects, verified applied changes should have a reproducible diff and test receipt.

Primary desired languages and tools: Unreal Engine C++ and Blueprints, UEFN Verse, Unity C#, Blender Python scripting, C++, C#, Python, Rust, JavaScript/TypeScript, HTML/CSS, .NET, and extensible support for shader languages, SQL, build systems, APIs, game data and asset pipelines. Code generation must know that a UEFN Verse implementation and Unreal C++ are not interchangeable; report API compatibility by actual version/evidence, not generic syntax guessing.

### 107.2 Live project indexing and correct source attribution

Importing a reference archive or knowledge ZIP is different from indexing the **current** source. The live source index uses repository commit, relative paths, exact bytes, source digests, AST/LSP symbols, dependency/call graph edges, test associations, language version and project permission. Exclude secrets, `.env`, credentials, private memory, unsafe binaries, model weights and large generated artifacts unless separately permitted. Refresh or invalidate indexes on changes, including file renames, project switches and cross-file symbol changes. The code assistant should retrieve a small evidence bundle, not dump the entire repository into context.

### 107.3 Candidate software change lifecycle

Requested goal → owner-authorized project → source/evidence inspection → explicit edit plan → resource/permission preflight → isolated worktree or guest snapshot → candidate patch → affected test selection → clean build/test → independent adversarial review → owner-visible diff and risk summary → owner approval for protected merge or state change → reversible commit → recorded accepted procedure or negative lesson. Unsafe unqualified execution must remain disabled until a guest isolation capability passes independent security review. The ability to suggest code is not permission to execute it on the physical host.

### 107.4 Self-development and software-evolution research

The full Evolution Arena vision includes multiple variants of one application (reliability-first, aggressive high-reward, performance, feature-heavy, UX-first, optional hybrid), each receiving at least ten successive controlled updates for comparison under identical starting tasks and budgets. Evaluate correctness, verified coverage, performance, memory, features, maintainability, UX and resource cost; retain every failure and provenance chain. Independently test transferable successful improvements, combine compatible branches only after conflict/semantic review, and explore whether original developer policies learn reusable strategies. This is a future isolated **research** program; do not turn it on as unattended host-source mutation just because the arena interface exists.

## 108. ATLAS Trainer, Training Council and native-model lifecycle

The desired Trainer is an original learned or trainable decision system for planning legitimate training experiments, not just a script that starts a GPU process. It identifies capability gaps from verified tasks, chooses eligibility and curriculum, checks privacy/data rights, proposes architectures and hyperparameters, schedules resource-safe training, observes actual metrics, diagnoses failed experiments, compares candidates, and generates an independent review request. It can eventually operate the real ATLAS Training UI under a bounded **owner-authorized** computer-use grant with accessibility-based observation and action receipts, but development agents Sol and Argon must not use cross-agent Computer Use for communications.

### 108.1 Nine-role training council

Preserve the historical council role set in the appendices. At minimum the functional responsibilities are (1) Training Director/Chair, (2) Data Curator, (3) Curriculum Designer, (4) Architecture Analyst, (5) Experiment Planner, (6) Trainer/Executor, (7) Independent Evaluator, (8) Resource Governor, and (9) Model Registry/Promotion Steward. Some archives give slightly different labels; maintain source identifiers and map to these responsibilities without claiming independent installed neural checkpoints. No council member may grant permissions outside the trusted host or certify its own model.

### 108.2 Four independent training tracks

* **A — Original from scratch:** new tokenizer/weights where needed, random initialization, independently sourced authorized data, no pretrained foundation. Begin with task-specific policies; scale by data, compute and measured gain, not an arbitrary 1B floor.

* **B — Pretrained comparisons:** use selected installed/authorized open weights as benchmarks or owner-approved alternative tools; this is explicitly *not* native-original intelligence.

* **C — Shared base/adapters:** optional matched LoRA/QLoRA efficiency controls, with base lineage and quantization compatibility recorded.

* **D — Teacher/student research:** an optional independently validated external teacher can propose candidate examples or strategies when authorized and permitted by licensing; teacher confidence or private reasoning is never accepted as truth and protected tests remain unseen by the student.

A Track A run may be fully native and offline; do not make it depend on a reference service being available. When comparisons use references, the report must attribute their exact work and any privacy/network cost. Product success should be reported as a function of verified real task outcomes, not model size or loss alone.

### 108.3 Fourteen-stage improvement lifecycle

Map the original 14 named lifecycle stages in Appendix E into persistent transitions: observe signal → establish legitimacy/consent → diagnose root cause → select measurable target → verify eligible evidence → freeze data/split and benchmark → propose candidate plan → enforce hardware/permissions → train or procedure/tool change → save checkpoint and lineage → evaluate protected/independent tasks → review safety and regressions → decide reject/shadow/canary/approved promotion → monitor and preserve rollback. Keep exact historical names searchable even if the implementation uses a normalized state machine. Transitions require events, reason codes, failure recovery and replay; the agent cannot skip directly to promotion.

### 108.4 Live training observatory and one-click workflows

The owner's preferred experience: **Open ATLAS → Improve a Model / Let ATLAS Choose / Create a Model → Set Goal → Start → Watch Real Training → Compare Versions → Continue / Pause / Stop**. Display real training/validation loss, task success, checkpoint revisions, examples accepted/rejected, data-source consent, compute budgets, GPU/VRAM/RAM, elapsed time, scheduler phase, stability, actual model identities, retries, and exit outcome. If there is no live job, show Idle instead of simulated graphs. Controls include Pause at Next Safe Checkpoint when optimizer-state continuity is supported, Stop After Current Cycle, Cancel With Rollback, Resume With Exact Checkpoint, and Revoke Autonomous Lease; never claim instant interruption if the backend cannot guarantee it.

### 108.5 Fourteen curriculum dimensions and correct difficulty progression

Preserve the original fourteen named dimensions in Appendix E; examples include task wording variability, source ambiguity, multi-document retrieval, adversarial noise, procedural length, tool constraints, cross-project generalization, conflicting evidence, time-dependent staleness, budget ceilings and uncertainty/abstention. Increase one or more dimensions after sufficient real success under preregistered rules. Distinguish a harder verified challenge from a synthetically impossible target. Stop when data eligibility is insufficient rather than loop endlessly. Do not claim endless exponential growth, guaranteed improvement or universal benchmark transfer.

### 108.6 Credible native-model evaluation

Strong baselines include exact-match/regex, FTS/TF-IDF, decision trees, logistic regression, the incumbent original checkpoint and a pretrained reference only when authorized. Split by project family, source version, task-generating process and time to reduce leakage; isolate evaluation custodian from the candidate author. Evaluate native model-only decisions and final verified host task success separately. Report wrong deliveries, false abstentions, coverage, calibration, cost, GPU/CPU time, latency, memory and success variation across seeds. Repeat genuine experiments; preserve null and negative outcomes. A model that ties simple search but adds costs is not promoted merely because it is neural.

### 108.7 Model-originality and training lineage contract

Model metadata must record model architecture/parameter count, initialization method and seed, tokenizer origin, training corpus and licenses, checkpoints (weight and optimizer hashes), learning schedule, hardware, total compute, test suite versions, known limitations, reference/teacher assistance, comparison conditions, qualified supported task kinds, deployment status and rollback. Failed refits remain in experiment history. A successful low training loss or one held-out win is not sufficient for general capability claims.

## 109. Autonomous work, desktop control and security authorizations

ATLAS should eventually work through multiple authority levels rather than a crude on/off switch:

* **Observe:** read permitted status/events only; no task changes or write actions.

* **Assist:** propose steps, answers and user-visible drafts; no protected execution without an explicit grant.

* **Simulation:** exercise typed virtual or disposable-fixture tasks with no effect on real projects.

* **Task Autopilot:** one scoped task grant with allowed tools, target files, duration/cost cap, network constraints, and rollback plan; may perform many routine steps within its envelope without repeated owner clicks.

* **Unattended Workstation / Idle Training:** explicit, revocable owner opt-in for specific classes of already qualified tasks, resource/time budgets and protected guest execution; never implies general OS admin.

Only the native trusted host and owner policy can grant capability. Models may propose tools/actions but cannot create or expand their own grants. Background actions have leases, heartbeats, journaled steps, bounds, cancellation/expiry and post-run owner reports. A model cannot turn research consent into account creation, shopping, downloads, social posting, email sending, protected source merge or arbitrary shell control.

**Hard prohibition:** ATLAS must never autonomously use the owner's personal identity, financial resources, cards, payment credentials, subscriptions/trials, password stores, private accounts, private personal vault, or data not granted for the exact purpose. No real-money transactions, including in-game microtransactions; in-game fictional currency may be used within an explicitly approved game environment. No account on behalf of ATLAS may be logged into the physical host. Online registration and downloading require an owner-authorized isolated remote environment; no promise of anonymity or zero malware risk. Do not weaken host security to enable an experiment.

### 109.1 Source, web and computer threat boundaries

Treat every downloaded page, repository file, document, model response, tool stdout, clipboard and network message as untrusted instructions unless a trusted owner control path explicitly authorizes an action. Threats to test: prompt injection, malicious file paths/symlinks/reparse points, Windows ADS and drive-relative path aliases, path casing, trailing-dot normalization, archive extraction escape, process inheritance, shell quoting, network redirect, local server rebinding, credential exfiltration, user-scope confusion, source swap after hash, task receipt substitution, evaluation contamination and unsafe generated code. Validate paths before read/copy/execute; revalidate immediately before action and at trust-boundary crossings. A sandbox must be independently tested rather than declared isolated.

### 109.2 Web research without owner exposure

Owner-approved web research should use a dedicated isolated network boundary with explicit domain/objective/size/time rules, fresh request credentials, downloadable artifact hashing, malware scanning where feasible, publication/licensing metadata, and no access to owner identities. Distinguish publicly observable website contact from anonymity guarantees; isolate home IP only when routing and DNS actually prove that. Raw web data goes to a staged, untrusted research vault. Only source-backed claim-specific independent verification can promote reusable knowledge. Authenticated website tasks and account creation remain separately restricted.

### 109.3 Development agents versus ATLAS internal agents

Sol and Argon are temporary external software developers with access according to the owner's developer environment. ATLAS's internal model agents receive much narrower, typed host grants. Policies describing future UI automation do not authorize Sol or Argon to screen-control the other's chat. Human relays short handoff messages. No background monitoring, waiting/polling or cross-agent Computer Use in the external development loop.

## 110. Hardware, model scheduling and practical compute budgets

Target Windows PC: NVIDIA RTX 5080, approximately 16GB GDDR7 VRAM, 96GB RAM and 4TB NVMe. GPU compute and installed driver/runtime must be detected; do not assume that all 16GB remains usable, or that CUDA, PyTorch, WSL2, compiler toolchains, or Ollama are installed and compatible. The old roadmap's hard-coded 12GB budget is a conservative example, not a fixed hardware property; measure allocator headroom and leave room for Windows graphics and foreground applications.

A resource governor records per-model quantized weight size, KV cache, gradients, optimizer state, embeddings, activation peaks, current VRAM/RAM, thermal and power limits, foreground priorities and storage growth. Prefer CPU/disk overlapping for low-cost scouts, serialize GPU-heavy specialists where necessary and overlap independent I/O work. Do not load 20 large Masters simultaneously just because the role registry has 20 names. Model swaps are benchmarked with cold and warm context, disk and GPU budgets rather than promised microsecond timings.

Idle training uses explicit windows, cooldown periods, foreground-interruption policy, maximum energy/time/GPU temperature/VRAM usage, checkpoint recovery, data consent and automatic stop conditions. If an attempt exceeds budget, it should stop safely, record why and allow later authorized resume. If a model architecture cannot fit, report an evidence-based alternative instead of implicit offloading that degrades user experience unpredictably.

## 111. Complete modern app navigation, responsive behavior and accessibility

The historically specified **five easy primary areas** are a user-experience proposal, while the **thirteen advanced workspaces** are capability destinations, not requirements to keep thirteen permanent tabs open. Preserve the source rosters in Appendix E. A practical navigation design:

* **Home / Just Do It:** goals, current tasks, recent projects, resume, progress and clear failure notifications.

* **Projects & Developer:** source trees, history, code inspector, exact file patches, generated suggestions, tests and approvals.

* **Research & Knowledge:** local/web evidence, claim states, procedures, knowledge cards, corrections and provenance.

* **Models & Learning:** native vs adapted vs reference model registry, training laboratory, Arena, live Observatory, scorecards and checkpoint lineage.

* **Personal & Settings:** private playground, consented personal memory, comparisons, autonomy permissions, hardware, exports and recovery.

Advanced subspaces include Dashboard, Assistant, Projects, Task Graph, Knowledge, Personal Memory, Models, Model Arena, Training Laboratory, Training Observatory, Developer/Code, Evolution/Automation, and Settings/Diagnostics as the source names dictate. Other historical sources may organize some screens differently; consolidate backend behavior instead of multiplying tabs. Keep accessible navigation to all original capabilities.

Test at 1280×720, 1920×1080, 2560×1440 and 3840×2160; Windows DPI 100–200% and high-refresh multi-monitor configurations; keyboard-only workflows; clear focus, screen readers, high contrast, reduced motion, scalable text, error/retry state, localization/translation labels, and remembered owner layout. Panels should dock/resize, code and long outputs should be searchable/copyable, and project/source evidence should open with exact line/span context. All training graphs and resource gauges must bind to real data. Never show fake progress or unimplemented controls as enabled.

The default UI should not ask the owner to configure ten models before making a simple request. Advanced model selection must still be discoverable. Model identity and provenance should be visible without overwhelming the main response.

## 112. Universal file intelligence, modalities and creative production

ATLAS should ingest and understand only supported formats through explicit, versioned, resource-bounded parsers. Targets include plain text/Markdown, source code and repositories, PDFs with layouts/citations, DOCX, spreadsheets, presentations, HTML, images, screenshots, audio, video and 3D/game assets; no format is considered supported until extraction/validation and user journey pass real tests. Preserve original bytes and source links. OCR/vision and speech models need their own original-training path or clearly attributed reference tool where owner-authorized; do not relabel third-party model perception as ATLAS-original.

**Creative writing and books:** maintain a story bible with chapters, voices, characters, scenes, timeline, setting, goals, foreshadowing, version history and continuity constraints. Draft and revise narrative in a project workspace; distinguish factual claims that need verification from fictional canon. Deliver editable manuscript/doc exports when supported. Measure continuity across long works and user-approved style, not merely character count.

**Game development:** create project-aware designs, gameplay systems, scripts, art requests, level logic, save/load, quest/dialogue systems, testing checklists and performance profiles. Support Unreal C++/Blueprints, UEFN Verse, Unity C#, Blender Python, browser games, and eventually PLATZ Engine via versioned interoperability. The mature system should produce complete files and precise integration steps or apply scoped verified patches in qualified isolation, not an unlabeled snippet.

**Art/3D/pixel/voxel:** planned specialized production includes reference understanding, sprite sheets, character/prop asset generation, rigging, animation, procedural materials, scene composition, exports and licensing. The old vision includes multi-domain creation capabilities and a separate graphics engine; no creative role should claim operational generation until real models, tools and asset workflows exist and pass tests.

**Analysis/science/translation:** combine source-grounded reasoning with deterministic calculators, unit-aware formulas, code execution only where qualified, structured tables and uncertainty reporting. Translation must preserve source IDs, code, equations, named entities, register and bilingual source alignment. Mathematical and scientific results need reproduced computations and source validity rather than fluent text alone.

## 113. PLATZ Technologies boundaries, product family and engine vision

The umbrella brand is **PLATZ Technologies**. **ATLAS AI** and **PLATZ Engine** are independent primary products. The PLATZ acronym is **Platform for Logic, Art, Tools & Zero Limits**; slogan **“One Place. Zero Limits.”** The Engine product family is PLATZ Engine, PLATZ Studio (editor), PLATZ Core (foundation), PLATZ Runtime (execution/export), PLATZ Hub (ecosystem) and PZ symbol. Preserve its distinct identity, source tree, delivery, independent product master directive, test gates and release history. No ATLAS milestone authorizes edits to PLATZ, FORGE or the protected legacy ATLAS repo.

PLATZ Engine is envisioned as a modular, high-performance, no-code-capable and code-extensible 2D, 2.5D, 3D, pixel, voxel, game/world and creative platform. Its canonical project data must be editable and non-destructive. Visual Variables→Events→Conditions→Actions should have code parity. The renderer is GPU/Vulkan-first with OpenGL and CPU-reference fallbacks, early real-rendering proof, numeric precision, accessible workflows, performance/correctness instrumentation, and ambitious high-refresh/8K/16K-asset goals subject to benchmarks. A fresh build may selectively reuse verified design ideas from FORGE, but the original FORGE remains read-only. PLATZ source authority remains `PLATZ_MASTER.md` / `PLATZ_WORKING.md` in its own repo, not ATLAS docs.

A future ATLAS–PLATZ connection is **proposed**, not guaranteed by either current source tree. When independently approved it should be an explicit permissioned versioned interface for project read/index, recipe retrieval, candidate assets/scripts, editor events, safe previews, build/test receipts and approved writes. Shared project formats or APIs should use a separate interoperability specification. Do not merge the two repos or make PLATZ dependent on unfinished native model training.

## 114. Engineering program, version continuity and complete definition of done

Every developer activation begins with the canonical master plan, current Git and worktree state, active handoff, accepted baseline, current source and protected evidence boundaries. A historical document cannot overrule a newer owner change or an actual accepted source revision. Developers should use `docs/ai/ATLAS_MASTER_PLAN.md` as lasting product intent, `docs/ai/ATLAS_HANDOFF.md` as immediate task authority, `docs/ai/ATLAS_OWNER_REPORT.md` as owner truth, and source-backed review receipts for acceptance. If a conflict exists, preserve evidence, document it and resolve explicitly rather than silently deleting history.

The normal workflow is Sol Extra High as major implementer in **five substantial updates per batch**, with separate preregistration, source and author-evidence commits per update and an integrated frozen checkpoint. Gemini Argon Orchestrator independently reviews the entire batch, issues per-update verdicts and integrated dependency-aware disposition, and returns one consolidated repair register. Sol may locally repair and continue development provisionally but cannot independently promote or approve its changes. Argon also cannot independently approve source it authored. Reviewer private tests remain outside the candidate author's workspace. Only owner-authorized reviewed baseline transitions become accepted. When the agent is unable to finish all five, it records exact partial status; it must not call missing milestones complete.

The owner manually relays a short next-agent message. Sol and Argon must not screen-control each other's chat, use cross-agent Computer Use, wait, poll, monitor or create an unsupervised external-agent loop. The canonical handoff carries all substantive technical instructions with exact Git hashes and next-role designation. Do not push, force-push or change external remotes without explicit authorization.

### 114.1 Every milestone must report the same core truth

A milestone receipt records stated scope and testable requirements; exact commit/branch and baseline; changed paths; build/test commands and outputs; reproducible data and fixture hashes; best deterministic and learned baselines; original and external model attribution; correct/wrong/abstained counts; latency/memory/resource cost; unexpected side effects and security constraints; previous feature regressions; isolated failure reproductions; private evaluation ownership; owner-visible example workflow; and exact downstream dependencies. Actual product code and package bytes must exist, not be claimed from a progress report.

### 114.2 Owner-visible success scorecard

Report not just how many unit tests passed, but (i) independently verifiable new user tasks; (ii) unfamiliar-family success and error rates; (iii) reduction in reliance on external inference; (iv) ATLAS-native model causal contribution and measured advantage; (v) knowledge/procedure reuse success; (vi) trusted source fidelity and stale/conflict handling; (vii) time and compute per correct result; (viii) security and privacy tests; (ix) user controls and product ergonomics; and (x) real progress against the original ATLAS. A more complicated neural fleet that ties a deterministic baseline must be flagged as unqualified for deployment, even if training succeeds.

### 114.3 Full application acceptance examples

Mandatory demonstration families include: first-run zero-cloud operation; project import/reopen; valid and rejected source import quotas; exact factual lookup and contextual clarification; method lookup with typed overload, nested declaration, documentation and source revisions; multi-source contradiction and recheck; append-only task journal and recovery; correct private memory consent refusal and deletion; native Master/specialist inference with no pretrained calls; model dataset eligibility and protected split; a rejected bad candidate; independent improvement if evidence supports it; model-training interruption/resume; pairwise output choice with provenance and exclusion; actual UI identity and status; safe signed/hashed procedures; offline library and optional authorized web candidate staging; qualified isolated code test; source patch approval and rollback; undo of source changes; no leakage across users/projects; denial of personal-account/financial use; restoration from backup; isolated remote-download gate; full packaging, clean install and update; optional private-server authentication; and final owner report generated from recorded events rather than invented counts.

## 115. NEXT-021 through NEXT-025 detailed native-original intelligence contract

**Precondition:** Independent Argon review of NEXT-015R and NEXT-016–020 must be reconciled before the accepted baseline advances. Until reviewed, new features may be developed locally only where owner-authorized, remain provisional, and must not be deployed with additional authority. The only known author results of NEXT-016–020 are the 65-parameter native specialist, 37-parameter Master, 65-parameter rejected refit, 360 host action measurements from 60 authored questions and 15 families, and public completions of 16/20 deterministic versus 16/20 native combined. These are evidence of original training and an honest negative experiment, not native superiority or broad reasoning.

### 115.1 NEXT-021 — Data and Knowledge Foundry

A new native training-data pipeline must first identify a task family where learned decisions can causally affect real outcomes. Collect independently measured, consented, license-compatible, exact-task-and-report-bound examples. Separate source-audit actions from distinct independent questions, and record failed attempts rather than relabeling them as successes. Group split by project, source family, problem template, writer and temporal origin; prevent duplicates, paraphrase clones, snippets from the same repo and teacher-suggested expectations from leaking across training and protected evaluation. Build a machine-readable map from `observed_failure → needed_skill → example provenance → eligible outcome → current best baseline → candidate experiment`. Integrate verified facts and applicable procedure IDs from Tier-5 vaults. Measure coverage and reject low-diversity datasets. User-facing demo: browse one model weakness and evidence behind each eligible training record. Ship a frozen dataset hash, a split manifest and real distinct project-family counts.

### 115.2 NEXT-022 — Original language understanding

Train from scratch an original tokenizer/encoder or constrained sequence learner appropriate for eligible corpus size and hardware. It should learn to map unfamiliar utterances to typed task structures (intent, project, entity, target/source, qualifiers, operations, safety scope, desired output) without relying on external pretrained text inference at native runtime. Define a narrow but real language curriculum: paraphrases, synonymy, qualifiers, multi-intent, negation, references to previous grounded tasks, noisy spelling, and unsupported requests. Compare against lexical rules, TF-IDF, logistic/text-feature baselines, pretrained references only when explicitly marked, and the incumbent original Master. Evaluate on unseen authors/project families and adversarial wording. A fluent open-ended chatbot is **not** promised; grounded limited response generation must cite verified evidence and abstain when unsupported. Show exact learned-model versus deterministic fallback contribution.

### 115.3 NEXT-023 — Learned planning and reusable procedures

Implement a typed small multi-step action vocabulary: inspect scope, search fact/procedure, retrieve source, compare evidence, ask for missing detail, choose candidate, run safe deterministic tool, verify, revise plan, hand off specialist, stop/abstain. Train an original policy using verified trajectories with actual downstream outcomes, including failed and inefficient plans. Store each action's budget, reason, evidence lineage, execution result and whether the action improved task completion. Compare to a hardcoded pipeline and a simple stochastic/heuristic planner on unfamiliar tasks requiring multiple steps. No free-form shell or network execution; the host executes only allowlisted read-only actions in this milestone. Prefer a learned improvement in complete success or cost per correct answer over an isolated planning label accuracy gain. Library procedures must include context applicability and failure conditions, not magical universal recipes.

### 115.4 NEXT-024 — Three Master competencies cooperating

Introduce functional original Primary Coordination, Research/Verification and Engineering/Execution competency policies with distinct data, observables, actions and tests. These need not be billion-parameter independent language models; they may share representations but must have genuinely different learned decisions and measurable effects. Use a small dependency-aware task graph with parallel read-only scouts where justified, an evidence package to the selected role, independent verifier and final synthesis. Compare the three-role workflow with single specialist, deterministic graph, no-coordination, random/linear coordinator and optional reference policy on the same frozen tasks. Show where coordination helps and where it adds only cost. No role may grant its own permissions or approve its own outputs. Engineering remains proposal/planning and read-only source evidence until execution is separately qualified.

### 115.5 NEXT-025 — Genuine measured self-improvement

Implement a bounded closed experiment: observe a verified deficiency → validate eligible new examples → preregister candidate hypotheses and protected holdout → train at least one original candidate (or report data-insufficient blocker) → freeze weights and data hash → evaluate from independent custodian → compare useful complete task success, mistakes, abstentions, calibration, latency and compute → reject/regress/retain candidate with all negative results → request separate authorization for any eventual promotion. Run identical tasks and budgets against deterministic and strong simple learned baselines. The training policy cannot modify the protected suite, judge itself, or silently expose evaluation expectations. Explicitly distinguish a successful **test of self-improvement machinery** from a successful **actual model improvement**. A rejected refit is scientifically useful but does not mean intelligence grew. No learning loop is production autonomous until owner-controlled scheduling, isolated execution boundaries and independent review are qualified.

### 115.6 Batch integration acceptance

The five milestones must form one working chain: owner enters unfamiliar natural-language research/code inspection request → original language learner maps to typed actions → original coordinator selects safe plan and specialists → host retrieves authorized source/procedure evidence → original policies make measurable choices → host/verifier checks exact answers → ledger records real outcome → Training Director identifies a documented weakness and proposes a candidate → independent evaluation accepts or rejects with reasons. Demonstrate at least one full native-only task without pretrained inference, one deliberately unsupported request that is safely withheld, and one failed candidate that is not promoted. Report actual counts and baseline comparison. The Windows app should expose at least a basic honest native-run/experiment inspector without inventing decorative panels.

## 116. Long-range original intelligence research beyond NEXT-025

After basic native-language parsing and learned procedures are qualified, further programs include wider knowledge acquisition and source credibility, richer natural-language generation, learned code edits in disposable projects, multimodal perception, visual/programmatic asset synthesis, personal preference learning with consent, long-context procedural retrieval, stronger cross-domain task decomposition, model ensemble/selection efficiency, generative models only where data/compute support them, multi-branch developer evolution, private-server distributed safe compute and independent self-maintenance. The progression must be capability-based, not bound to a timetable or model-size slogan.

When an original model achieves a real narrow skill, it should be captured as both a versioned checkpoint **and** reusable external procedure or answer card where the result can be made deterministic. A verified zero-model shortcut can replace expensive model calls for stable exact tasks; this is a desirable efficiency result, not evidence that the model's reasoning disappeared. Remain prepared to reject additional neural complexity when deterministic alternatives win.

## 117. The owner's permanent 'nothing forgotten' repository mechanism

A long document alone cannot ensure every future model session reads or obeys it. Establish durable repository controls:

 1. `docs/ai/ATLAS_MASTER_PLAN.md` is the canonical **current** owner product constitution, with explicit version, SHA-256, change log, policy anchors and related files.

 2. `docs/ai/ATLAS_HANDOFF.md` is the canonical **current assignment**. It names accepted baseline, pending candidate, writer/reviewer, review/repair state, exact revisions and next bounded task.

 3. `docs/ai/ATLAS_OWNER_REPORT.md` is derived from accepted commits/test evidence; it distinguishes accepted, author-verified, blocked and proposed capabilities.

 4. `docs/ai/ATLAS_REVIEW_PROTOCOL.md` defines Sol/Argon five-update batch reviews, single writer, protected evaluator custody, no self-acceptance, owner manual relay and no cross-agent Computer Use/waiting.

 5. `docs/ai/ATLAS_REQUIREMENTS_LEDGER.json` contains stable requirement IDs, current priority, source origin, category, status, code/test paths, accepted revision, external blockers and owner signoffs. Every historical owner requirement must map to an active entry, an archived/deprecated entry with reason, or an explicit missing-source entry.

 6. `docs/ai/ATLAS_SOURCE_CROSSWALK.csv` maps each original document chapter/heading/requirement to a canonical section or preserved archived source reference, with `full`, `partial`, `historical`, `conflict`, or `unavailable` status. Never assert all coverage if meaningful gaps remain.

 7. `docs/ai/archive/` preserves exact original source attachments, versioned extracted text and hashes. An archival copy is nonbinding unless promoted through an explicit owner-approved canonical edit.

 8. A startup preflight in each external developer prompt requires checking these files, Git status and a concise readable policy digest. A repo CI/docs check may verify existence, crosswalk integrity, catalog counts, doc hash and absence of stale canonical pointers; it cannot guarantee a neural agent remembered the content after context truncation.

 9. Future internal ATLAS agents receive the relevant bounded portions of the plan as task-authorized context plus a short non-negotiable constitution. The broker retrieves detailed sections on demand; do not flood a tiny model with a quarter million words.

10. Changes to product intent are made as versioned amendments with owner authority and review evidence; old requirements are superseded with reason and archive, not silently deleted. The trusted security policy remains external and noneditable by unqualified AI agents.

### 117.1 Source completeness standard

The bundle includes the exact original submitted sources byte-for-byte, an extracted text transcript for each, and a heading-level crosswalk. Current master text incorporates their governing substance while moving obsolete model names, historical branch hashes, impossible speed/equivalence guarantees, and duplicate implementation demands into nonbinding archive status. Some older prompts reference **external ZIPs that were not uploaded** (e.g., private vault, public knowledge v4/v5, full 200 initiatives/2,000 specialties). These cannot truthfully be declared included. Keep their promised manifests listed as `NOT_SUPPLIED` until the actual bytes arrive; never invent them or silently claim they were imported.

# PART XX — IMPLEMENTABLE DATA CONTRACTS, TEST PROTOCOLS AND READINESS GATES

> The records below define **target interfaces**, not assertions that matching endpoint names or schemas exist in the current repository. Version names are examples until an actual implementation and tests establish them. For every schema: reject unknown privilege-expanding fields, preserve older readable versions through migration, validate at the trust boundary, and do not conflate a model's JSON text with a trusted host operation.

## 118. Typed end-to-end task contract

A future internal task includes `task_id`, `graph_id`, `request_id`, parent/dependency IDs, owner/project scope, authorization-grant ID, project revision or immutable snapshot, objective, accepted sources, allowed tool/action set, output schema, safety category, CPU/GPU/VRAM/time/token limits, cancellation/expiry time, provenance and current state. The Master proposes a typed plan. The trusted host validates the scope and each transition before scheduling. Revisions at every source boundary are checked against original evidence. Independent reviewer receives a frozen candidate with read-only access and its own protected tests. Task output preserves both answer and failed attempts.

Illustrative logical object:

```
{
  "schema_version": "atlas.task.v1",
  "task_id": "task-example-042",
  "graph_id": "graph-example-007",
  "owner_scope": "owner-local",
  "project_id": "project-example",
  "objective": "Compare two approved project implementations for missing-value handling",
  "source_scope": ["project-example:src/example_a.py", "project-example:src/example_b.py"],
  "authorization_id": "grant-readonly-example",
  "allowed_actions": ["project.symbols", "knowledge.search", "source.read_exact", "evidence.compare"],
  "dependencies": [],
  "limits": {"tool_calls": 12, "wall_seconds": 90, "gpu_vram_mib": 0},
  "required_output": "atlas.evidence_answer.v1",
  "review_required": true
}

```

`task-...example` and permission strings above are illustrative, not live credentials or implemented API values. The plan scheduler must handle cancellation, retries, stale evidence, duplicate task IDs, dependency cycles, cross-project errors, worker crashes, read-limit violations and replay. Every recovery decision needs a journaled event. Parallelism is bounded by resource pressure and only independent graph nodes may run concurrently; serial fallbacks are acceptable and measured.

## 119. Agent and Master action schemas

Every trained agent emits a narrow structured proposal rather than arbitrary OS code: `inspect_source`, `retrieve_claim`, `retrieve_procedure`, `compare_evidence`, `request_clarification`, `propose_code_patch`, `propose_test`, `run_approved_tool`, `suggest_specialist`, `stop_with_evidence`, or `abstain`. Each proposal includes task ID, version, agent/checkpoint identity, target IDs, limits, evidence references, predicted outcome, uncertainty and rationale/trace identifiers. The host rejects an unregistered action or a requested tool not in the grant. The Master cannot rename actions or write a new grant into a task document and thereby gain privileges.

Model outputs and host-side validated actions must be separately logged. Confidence is not authorization or proof. A separately trained Evidence/Review Master challenges source support and test completeness, but any protected acceptance decision remains with independently enforced tests and approval policy. No internal agent may rewrite the evaluation oracle, source digests or host permissions that verify its own output.

## 120. Source, claim and procedure schema examples

```
{
  "schema_version": "atlas.source.v1",
  "source_id": "source-example-a",
  "origin": "owner_authorized_import",
  "project_id": "project-example",
  "path_in_project": "docs/example.md",
  "source_revision": 3,
  "sha256": "EXAMPLE_SHA256_PLACEHOLDER_NOT_A_REAL_DIGEST",
  "encoding": "utf-8",
  "license": "owner_asserted_pending_verification",
  "retrieved_at_utc": "2026-11-19T00:00:00Z",
  "trust_state": "candidate",
  "sensitivity": "project_internal"
}

```

```
{
  "schema_version": "atlas.claim.v1",
  "claim_id": "claim-example-01",
  "text": "The sample tool accepts an optional filename.",
  "source_refs": [{"source_id": "source-example-a", "source_revision": 3, "start_utf16": 145, "length_utf16": 62}],
  "assessment": "source_anchored_not_independently_verified",
  "scope": "project-example",
  "validity_policy": "recheck_on_source_revision_change",
  "verified_by": null,
  "supersedes": null
}

```

```
{
  "schema_version": "atlas.procedure.v1",
  "procedure_id": "procedure-example-test-python",
  "domain": "software/python",
  "goal": "Determine whether a declaration is syntactically valid",
  "preconditions": ["authorized_source_snapshot", "supported_python_version"],
  "allowed_actions": ["source.read_exact", "parser.syntax_check"],
  "steps": [{"operation": "parser.syntax_check", "inputs": ["source_snapshot"]}],
  "success_oracle": "syntax_tree_available_and_source_spans_match",
  "observed_successes": 0,
  "observed_failures": 0,
  "qualification": "proposed_unverified"
}

```

These are schematic examples. Persisted hash and scope fields must use actually verified bytes; forged placeholders never pass the live verifier. Knowledge Broker must use stable typed IDs, not freeform absolute filesystem paths suggested by an agent.

## 121. Outcome ledger and learning-data manifest

A real training-eligible outcome must bind the exact original task request and normalized intent to immutable source snapshots, action set, chosen proposal, verified result, independent evaluator identity, permit/consent, full cost and time, failure category, and dataset rights. Matching a report digest without task identity is insufficient (the historical NEXT-008 review exposed this failure class). For negative data, preserve rejected proposals and **why they failed**; do not pretend every negative is an example of a correct answer. Human feedback and AI predictions have different enum values.

Minimal conceptual fields: `outcome_id`, `task_request_sha256`, `project_family_id`, `origin`, `input_artifact_hashes`, `candidate_checkpoint_sha256`, `host_policy_revision`, `action_proposed`, `action_executed`, `verified_result_status`, `error_type`, `evaluator_id`, `evaluation_receipt_sha256`, `consent_scope`, `license`, `eligible_for_training`, `eligibility_decision`, `partition_group`, `duplicate_cluster`, `timestamp`, `latency_ms`, `rss_bytes`, `gpu_peak_bytes`. A source change creates a new version, not silent replacement.

Before training, write a dataset manifest containing immutable record IDs and hashes, a grouping strategy, train/validation/test family IDs, contamination checks, independent protected-custodian location and source-mix summary. Reject a dataset containing only repeated variants of one solved template when the claim is cross-domain generalization. Distinguish action measurements (multiple actions on the same authored question) from independent tasks.

## 122. Model registry, promotion and independence contracts

For every candidate preserve `checkpoint_id`, `architecture`, `parameter_count`, `tokenizer_lineage`, `initialized_from_scratch`, `pretrained_base_id_or_null`, `training_data_manifest_hash`, `training_code_revision`, `seed`, `optimizer_state_hash`, `hardware_profile`, `training_compute`, `author`, `model_only_results`, `host_pipeline_results`, `baseline_results`, `protected_review`, `calibration`, `latency`, `memory`, `deployment_status`, `owner_signoff` and `rollback_checkpoint`. Track A native original cannot claim scratch provenance if it initialized from a pretrained weight. A LoRA rank count is not the full model's independent parameter count. Multiple aliases pointing to one underlying foundation should not be double-counted as distinct original models.

Promotion ladder: `REGISTERED → CHECKPOINT_INTEGRITY → DATA_ELIGIBILITY → HELD_OUT_PUBLIC → PROTECTED_INDEPENDENT → SECURITY_REGRESSION → SHADOW → CANARY_IF_AUTHORIZED → OWNER_APPROVED_PRODUCTION`, with failure to `REJECTED_ARCHIVED`. The reviewer must have a different responsible agent than the implementation author; a third party's teacher output cannot be the correctness oracle. Independent “acceptance with limitations” qualifies only the explicitly described task scope. Models with no measurable improvement over simpler baselines stay research candidates unless independent utility justifies a different deployment objective (e.g., lower cost or improved calibration).

An independence scorecard distinguishes six contributions per task: (1) native original neural, (2) deterministic host/verified procedure, (3) locally pretrained model, (4) fine-tuned/adapted reference, (5) cloud-hosted external inference, and (6) human/developer intervention. Report strict native-only end-to-end success separately from native-model suggestions that never influenced the final answer. Model generation by external GPT during development is a development dependency and should be reported separately from ATLAS runtime inference.

## 123. Protected security permission grant contract

Capabilities are issued by the native host with owner authentication. A grant defines `owner_id`, `project`, `operation_class`, exact `allowlisted_tools`, normalized path roots and network domains, `expires_at`, total call/compute/byte budgets, sensitive-data policy, preview/audit visibility and rollback support. Model-output text cannot forge approval or expand scope. Privileged operations (writing user files, shells, changing system settings, creating accounts, external communications, downloads, cloud service use, purchases, model promotion, protected merge and security policy edits) require independent specific approval and risk control. Owner's categorical no-money/no-personal-account prohibition overrides convenience.
