Trusted host preflight must evaluate canonical paths, Windows component aliases and reparse points, file types, source byte hashes before action, archive entries and symlinks, network destination and DNS/TLS identity, inherited environment variables, subprocess permissions and potential file-write targets. Recheck immediately before effect. Audit denial cases and handle interrupted/rolled-back operations. No one should equate an allowlisted executor with a correctly isolated guest absent an independent escape and exfiltration test campaign.

## 124. Product state, journal and disaster recovery contract

Store every task transition append-only where feasible, with parent IDs, source/object hashes, actor, timestamp, result and reason; use atomic replace/rename for manifests/checkpoints. At startup repair a truncated record only when safe, quarantine corrupted journals, and preserve earlier accepted evidence. Training job pause/resume should preserve exact model + optimizer state if claiming continuity. Project changes should preserve previous imported revisions. A restoration dry run must verify model registry, knowledge indexes, user consent records, project sources, and protected evaluator barriers. Backups must keep personal vault encryption and access separation. No unattended “cleanup” can delete owner project work, personal data or historical failures.

## 125. Repository authority and deployment topology

The immediate ATLAS Next repository is `C:\ATLAS\ATLAS-Next`, with the canonical master at `docs\ai\ATLAS_MASTER_PLAN.md` and handoff at `docs\ai\ATLAS_HANDOFF.md`. The original ATLAS source at `C:\ATLAS\Entire ATLAS` remains untouched/read-only for reference and recoverable engineering lessons. PLATZ Engine and FORGE are separate authorities. A proposed future layout distinguishes trusted host, UI client, service adapters, knowledge broker, role/policy registry, task graph, source/project tools, native model factory, evaluation custodian, training lab, packaging, docs, evidence and archived source specifications; do not destructively reorganize a working tree just to match a diagram.

A private server may host a local ATLAS core with authenticated, encrypted, owner-approved desktop access and physical isolation of data/permissions. No public SaaS or mobile client is the current target. Project mounts must be scope-limited; home network addresses and remote service access must be auditable; admin/debug endpoints cannot be available unauthenticated. The existing WPF program is the current app truth; historical React/WebView2/Python service architecture is a reference, not a mandate to throw away the accepted newer host.

## 126. Acceptance-test matrix and regression families

The original requirements file has **50 explicit ID-xx requirements** and a **32-scenario acceptance suite**; the older 124-page master additionally enumerates 30 major tests. All must remain findable by ID, with a status tied to actual code and evidence. The master source archive preserves the exact original wording. The following groups define the integrated modern gate without replacing those tests:

**Native model and learning:** scratch-weight proof; hostile checkpoint metadata; tokenizer provenance; deterministic and learned baseline comparison; train/test contamination; package family holdout; repeated paraphrase cluster; wrong model checkpoint; malicious learned-action proposal; non-improving refit rejected; multiple seeds; optimizer resume; resource budget exhaustion; zero model dependency trace.

**File/knowledge correctness:** immutable byte digest; stable logical source IDs; exact UTF-16 or byte spans; reordered/renamed files; cross-project deny; duplicate claims; raw vs verified source; conflicting sources; stale publication date; updated evidence; corrected knowledge card; procedure applicability and failed transfer; summary source fidelity; untrusted web document injection.

**Source coding:** C# overload binding with actual compilation context; Python AST/syntax-valid declaration; UTF-8/non-ASCII and CRLF; multiline annotation; nested declarations; caller/callee binding limits; missing import; source quotas and rejected import occupancy; source tampering between preview/action; final output provenance; patch file scope; build/test/rollback; dangerous path aliases.

**Personal privacy and comparison:** private playground leaves no app record; local server retention caveat; owner category scope; personal vault encryption; opt-in retention; user correction and export; data deletion; preference AI/human distinction; Too Similar and Completely Different behavior; both answers excluded from training and research when requested; attempts to ingest unapproved private content denied.

**Developer/trainer autonomy:** task graph dependencies; concurrent vs serial fairness; approved tools only; cancellation mid-experiment; idle preemption; crash recovery; direct UI action receipts and wrong-target refusal; remote guest failure without host fallback; archive exfil; HTTPS/DNS/proxy leak; no owner-account creation, payments or subscription access; protected test rewrite refusal.

**Product quality:** fresh install no-cloud; project switch and recovery; accessible keyboard UI; 720p/1080p/1440p/4K DPI; actual model identity; truthful training graphs; imported data lineage; supported file types; meaningful errors; two supported PowerShell package workflows; clean checkout and reproducible hashes; no old repo/PLATZ/FORGE modifications; manual Sol/Argon handoff and correct verdict custody.

Each recorded acceptance case must identify exact source, expected result, actual result, whether public or protected, and the relevant review scope. A final “all tests pass” aggregate never hides wrong trusted deliveries or skipped tests.

## 127. Complete owner task examples and nonfunctional quality

A mature ATLAS should handle requests such as: “Learn the source structure of my project, explain where X is, and suggest the smallest correct change”; “Tell me what you know about this scientific measurement and which source it came from”; “Compare all these claims and recheck them next month with permission”; “Build a Unity movement script that I can paste directly into PlayerController.cs and explain every public setting”; “Make a Blender procedural mesh script with scale/UV/material defaults and test instructions”; “Write chapter four while respecting the story timeline and character arcs”; “Propose three enemy AI strategies, measure their game behavior in an isolated test project and keep the best”; “I want ATLAS to train a new source-selection specialist from our authorized outcome ledger without borrowing pretrained weights”; “Find a new validated research procedure, store it and use it on a different project”; “Run a parallel research-and-project inspection and show me the separately verified results”; “Explain what the last unattended training experiment actually improved”; “Show a real comparison between original ATLAS native and optional Qwen reference on exactly the same tasks”; “Make a PLATZ Engine component through an approved interoperability API”; and “Perform a complete approved coding repair but stop before touching my real project until I inspect the diff.”

Performance goals are measured under cold and warm conditions, CPU-only/no-internet fallback, foreground load and different project sizes. A task's output must remain correct after restart and not expose private data in logs. Long memory is an external indexed evidence system, not an imaginary unlimited attention window. Local models should give timely partial results and bounded retries, without fabricating step completion. The owner can always stop, inspect data sources and reverse an accepted project change where a rollback is supported.

## 128. Source reconciliation, missing archives and explicit non-claims

This plan includes all physically supplied documents in an unabridged appendix and preserves original files separately with SHA-256. Some earlier directions refer to ZIPs of 2,000 role records, 150 personal proposals, 200 future initiatives, 442+ technical documents, and a private biography/personal vault. **Those archive bytes were not part of this upload set**, so their contents are not silently invented, copied from memory, declared installed or put into the public master. The new 200-initiative list is explicitly labeled `RECONSTRUCTED CURRENT BACKLOG` to avoid impersonating the lost source. The 20/100/25-family/125-example names and 28 historical goals that are present in the supplied files are preserved in the full source annex and previous appendices. Similarly, source history about model counts, benchmark speed, role “equivalence,” older Git commits and unavailable feature labels is historical, not current proof.

When the owner later supplies a missing exact archive, the intake path is: verify hash/manifest and contents; classify sensitivity; preserve exact raw copy; extract/search under approved scope; create a source crosswalk; decide which substantive requirements to promote; version the canonical master after owner approval; and add tests. Never silently change product identity or import private material to a general public knowledge namespace.

# FINAL PRODUCT DEFINITION

ATLAS AI is a local-first, owner-controlled system of **original learned intelligence, verified factual/procedural files, capable specialized agents, independent tests, and safe gradually authorized action**. Its ultimate success is not the existence of a model with a familiar name, a huge role catalog, a beautifully rendered dashboard, or a successful training-loss curve. It is the ability to complete meaningful unfamiliar tasks correctly, attribute how that happened, learn from real outcomes, reduce dependence on external AI inference, and keep the human owner and trusted verifier in control. Every release should make progress toward that destination while preserving the distinction between **vision**, **implemented source**, **author-tested behavior**, **independently accepted behavior**, and **qualified deployed autonomy**.

# PART XXI — ADDITIONAL CRITICAL SYSTEM CONTRACTS AND REAL-WORLD READINESS

> **2026-11-19 expansion note.** The requirements in Part XXI and Appendices L–O are new, explicitly authored extensions to the existing ATLAS vision after a gap review; they are not asserted to have appeared verbatim in the historical uploads, and they are **not claims of implemented software**. They strengthen the native-first, file-first, security-first plan without superseding the already-preserved source texts. `MUST` applies to a capability when its development phase reaches the corresponding trust boundary. The accepted repository baseline, source code and independent review receipts establish present functionality. All safety and owner restrictions outrank optional product ambitions.

## 129. Native-intelligence sovereignty and dependency attestation

**Why this is missing if not made explicit.** Running a small newly trained classifier beside a large pretrained generator is not the same as an original ATLAS system solving the task. A strong task result must remain attributable to the component that actually supplied the intelligence. A system-level answer can have deterministic assistance and still count as native-assisted, but a local reference model must never be relabeled as original merely because both ran on the same PC.

ATLAS SHALL maintain an inference-dependency graph for every completed task. For each step it records the exact checkpoint hash, initializer lineage, tokenizer lineage, executable and library versions, loaded model identity, input artifact IDs, retrieval/tool calls, permitted network endpoints, candidate action, action-selection outcome, host verification and final attribution. A "from-scratch" checkpoint means all trainable weights used by that model began without a borrowed pretrained weight; using a standard tokenizer algorithm or tensor library does not by itself make weights pretrained, while copying pretrained embeddings, vocabulary vectors, adapters or hidden teacher outputs does. An optional teacher may provide openly labeled *training evidence* only if authorized, legally usable and separately verified; that lineage is not erased after later fine-tuning.

Define five non-overlapping report labels: `NATIVE_MODEL_DECISION`, `NATIVE_MODEL_GENERATION`, `DETERMINISTIC_HOST_ONLY`, `LOCAL_PRETRAINED_ASSISTED`, and `EXTERNAL_SERVICE_ASSISTED`. Mixed tasks additionally preserve an ordered contribution ledger, rather than choosing a misleading single label. A native-only runtime is physically capable of completing its specified bounded task with all third-party LLM services stopped and network access denied. A capability that requires a pretrained model to turn an internal decision into the result is a hybrid capability, not a native-only one.

**Required experiment.** Start with all Ollama and external AI endpoints unavailable; run a frozen unfamiliar project task; inspect the trace and assert zero pretrained inference calls, zero unintended egress, correctly identified original checkpoint hashes and a verifiably correct or correctly withheld answer. Repeat with optional pretrained chat enabled and prove its outputs are separately marked and cannot enter trusted fact or model-qualification evidence without independent verification. Introduce a fake pretrained model behind a local endpoint; native-only tests MUST detect and reject that dependency. Track native-only task completion and quality as a product KPI, rather than percentage of model files with an ATLAS name.

## 130. The original language curriculum: understanding before eloquence

ATLAS needs original learned *language competence*, not just character matching or a prettier explanation generated by Qwen. Design a staged curriculum with tasks whose correct outcome is externally checkable. Stage L0 recognizes words, numbers, names and basic syntax in owner-authorized text; L1 binds natural paraphrases to canonical intents, entities and constraints; L2 tracks references across two or more turns and binds them to current source revisions; L3 turns a request into a typed evidence-gathering goal; L4 composes several grounded findings into an answer with explicit uncertainty; L5 produces constrained explanatory prose grounded in cited passages; L6 supports richer domain-specific generation and repair with tests. Every stage adds new held-out families and preserves old-family regression requirements.

Use an explicit tokenizer and normalization policy suitable for English, source code identifiers, numbers, paths and quoted literal text. The model should preserve case, punctuation, raw source spans and language-specific semantics when those matter; normalization for retrieval must never change what an original file actually said. Evaluate non-ASCII names, Unicode combining characters, Windows paths, emojis, source delimiters and filenames with similar spellings. Keep a distinct confidence model for intent interpretation and separate verification for factual content. An answer's polish must not be scored as source correctness.

Training a character-level or subword Transformer on a tiny domain corpus can demonstrate real original sequence modeling but does not prove fluent open-ended conversation. Ensure results include a strong n-gram or retrieval baseline, a TF-IDF/linear baseline, the incumbent native policy, and an upper-bound optional reference model labeled as such. The evidence must include unseen *combinations*, not only paraphrases sharing all content words with training queries.

**Release gate.** Demonstrate that the original model maps unfamiliar natural requests to correct constrained task records and that the host can carry out a trusted read-only workflow from those records. Report wrong action selection, unsafe requested operations, unsupported utterances, confidence calibration, abstention and resource use. Do not declare a "general original language model" on the basis of a successful ten-prompt showcase.

## 131. Generalization design, contamination firewalls and task diversity

The next useful dataset is defined by **independent task families**, not raw row count. Six source choices measured on one authored question are six action measurements but one task family for generalization. Build manifests with separate `source_family`, `project_family`, `question_family`, `template_family`, `author`, `generator`, `license`, `semantic_cluster` and `time_slice` fields. Split at the broadest unit likely to leak repeated structure, using project and template clusters together. A benchmark is no longer protected if its inputs, expected labels or failure descriptions enter the implementation author's training environment.

Establish a contamination service that checks exact input hashes, normalized texts, near duplicates, phrase fragments, code-symbol overlap, and imported knowledge procedures against public, validation and protected sets. It must preserve a reasoned exception mechanism for standard unavoidable vocabulary such as common Python keywords: a shared word is not automatically leakage. Strictly prohibit direct and indirect use of private evaluation answers for fitting, prompt optimization, dataset selection, hyperparameter tuning or accept/reject thresholds. A custodian may publish aggregate metrics without disclosing test cases; if a private set is exposed, rotate it and record the compromise.

Predeclare evaluation families by challenge type: direct evidence lookup, paraphrase, ambiguity, contradictory evidence, stale source, unsupported grammar, multi-file interaction, novel procedural combination, rare project convention, deliberately misleading local file, and safe abstention. A model performing well on one family must not inherit claims about others. Include absent-answer cases even when inconvenient for completion statistics. All reports must give sample size by family and number of unique projects, not just a pooled percentage.

**Acceptance tests.** Put a nearly duplicated project in training and a new project from the same template in evaluation; assert detection. Put the same developer's different family in protected data and verify permission. Tamper with a dataset split manifest after a run; invalidate the candidate receipt. Supply a test input with hidden oracle text in an imported document; reject it as evidence of generalization.

## 132. A legitimate, license-aware learning-data supply chain

The learning foundry must never solve the data shortage by quietly importing private user material, scraping restricted repositories, copying third-party copyrighted books or labeling unverified generated answers as ground truth. Data sources are classified as `OWNER_APPROVED_PUBLIC`, `PROJECT_LICENSED`, `OWNER_SPECIFIC_NONTRAINING`, `SYNTHETIC_VERIFIED`, `REFERENCE_GENERATED_UNVERIFIED`, `OBSERVED_TOOL_RESULT`, or `PROTECTED_EVALUATOR`. Eligibility is a decision on an individual record with a purpose and revocation trail, not a blanket flag on a folder.

For each dataset version preserve a **dataset card**: allowed uses, origin, scope, rights/license, personal-data review, source hash, collection method, transformation pipeline, annotation/oracle method, count of unique tasks and project families, known biases, known errors, expiry or consent limits, split strategy, and documented removal instructions. A dataset never gains rights because an AI agent rewrote the content. Optional external-model teacher outputs must remain clearly attributable and require both authorization and an independent outcome oracle before becoming trainable as a result of verified work.

Build acquisition from authorized *outcomes*: a read-only task attempted on a real owner-imported project, host test or parser outcome, scored source selection, actual compiler result from an approved isolated environment, or an owner-approved feedback label. Preserve failed action proposals with the actual counterexample and applicability conditions. Avoid silently treating "not selected" as "incorrect" when multiple actions might have succeeded. Record the outcome-oracle version separately from model identity to avoid circular labels.

**Required data-rights controls.** Exclude user personal memory, credentials, family/private records, account tokens and nonconsenting shared files from training by default. Implement future revocation by locating dependent dataset versions and checkpoints, marking affected artifacts and requiring retraining or withdrawal when appropriate. A user-supplied project's use for inference does NOT automatically authorize storing it permanently or using it for model training. The system must still work when training consent is entirely off.

## 133. Active learning, experiment economy and intelligent data acquisition

ATLAS should learn what evidence is *worth collecting*. The Training Director evaluates whether an observed weakness is a missing source, a bad parser, an unsuitable procedure, a wrong route, a poorly calibrated learned model or an impossible task under existing tools. Only the last few categories may justify new neural training; many errors should be fixed with a source correction, revised trust rule or deterministic code patch. Record the chosen remedy and why it was selected, then test its impact on complete tasks.

Introduce a bounded **active-learning proposal** mechanism: identify high-uncertainty or high-regret examples; suggest an authorized, checkable additional measurement; estimate the cost of producing a trustworthy label; prioritize diversity and expected reduction in error; and stop when the budget or data eligibility is exhausted. Implement at least two acquisition baselines, such as uniform sampling and failure-family-balanced sampling. Compare active acquisition using a frozen evaluation suite after equal labeling/compute budgets, rather than counting how many requested examples were produced.

Distinguish informative failures from repeated failures: fifty copies of one missing parser feature should be one root-cause cluster for model-learning purposes. Keep a separate store for incident recurrence and severity, because repeats can still matter operationally. A new training candidate must have a hypothesis about which decision it will improve and which measurements will falsify that hypothesis. Protected evaluator scores must not be fed back to a model as labels.

**Minimum demonstration.** On a local, authorization-safe project curriculum, show a measurable budget allocation decision, a reproducible acquisition batch, real eligible labels, negative examples and a comparison to random or uniform acquisition. If no reliable eligible examples exist, report `INSUFFICIENT_ELIGIBLE_DATA` with specific gaps instead of generating ungrounded labels.

## 134. Causal value of a learned model: ablations, counterfactuals and cost

A native specialist's usefulness cannot be established simply by running it in parallel with a deterministic host that produces the same final answer. ATLAS shall measure the model's **causal contribution**: did selecting the learned action change the answer, reduce wrong claims, lower total latency/energy, or materially reduce the amount of costly work needed for an equivalent verified result? Every integration experiment includes a model-off baseline, a model-proposal-only mode, a learned policy actually allowed to choose among trusted read-only actions, and a safe host fallback. Each mode keeps identical project and evaluation conditions wherever possible.

For routing and source selection, predefine an action set and run controlled counterfactual outcomes where eligible. When feasible, log outcomes for several candidate actions on the same frozen task; do not assume an untried action would fail. Report the distribution of situations where learned decisions changed the path, where they helped, where they hurt, and where they were redundant. Measure total pipeline cost including input preparation, retrieval, model loading, verification and fallback—not only the few microseconds of a tiny neural network's inference.

For multi-agent cooperation, compare a deterministic single workflow, the strongest single learned specialist, a two-specialist arrangement and the Master-coordinated graph under matched task and resource budgets. Record contention, repeated evidence retrieval, duplicate token or tool use, reviewer cost, canceled tasks and coordination overhead. A larger team is not automatically more intelligent or efficient. Report paired task differences and confidence intervals where sample size permits; very small samples must state that uncertainty plainly.

**Qualification criterion.** Define a nontrivial primary end-to-end metric, such as higher verified completion at no increase in wrong trusted deliveries; or equal verified completion with a meaningful preregistered reduction in total resource cost. A large source-selection accuracy boost without end-to-end benefit counts as specialist research progress, not system-level intelligence improvement.

## 135. Calibrated uncertainty, safe abstention and knowledge gaps

Correctly refusing an unsupported request can be a success, but refusing every difficult request is not intelligence. Maintain a three-outcome measurement for `CORRECT_DELIVERY`, `CORRECT_WITHHOLD`, and `INCORRECT_DELIVERY`, plus explicit `FALSE_ABSTENTION` and `UNSUPPORTED_SCOPE` cases. Use an additional task-family-conditioned abstention cost so the model cannot game accuracy by withdrawing from all unfamiliar work. Reports must show both precision on delivered answers and useful coverage of eligible answerable tasks.

Train uncertainty calibration only on permissible validation data; preserve protected evaluation exclusively for final independent qualification. Compare reliability diagrams or expected calibration error where probabilities are meaningful, plus selective-risk curves for abstention thresholds. Do not treat arbitrary neural softmax scores as calibrated certainty. Separate uncertainty about natural-language intent, source relevance, source validity, action safety, model generation and final claim evidence; a high relevance score does not prove an answer.

A request requiring an unavailable file or a source that has changed after plan creation must return a precise evidence or reauthorization need. A model may propose a clarification to the user or a new allowed lookup. It must not invent an explanation and mark it verified, and the verified knowledge library must distinguish `UNKNOWN`, `NOT_TESTED`, `CONTRADICTED` and `RETRACTED`. Errors and safe abstentions remain valuable evidence for future active learning without becoming false facts.

**Independent tests.** Supply answerable but linguistically novel questions, unsupported sources, misleading evidence, malicious forged certainty, and changed project revisions. Verify that ATLAS's confidence and delivery gate are grounded in independent proof, not the model's self-reported confidence or the number of agents agreeing.

## 136. Reasoning procedures as typed, independently testable programs

The procedural library must encode more than reusable prose prompts. Define a versioned **Procedure Contract** with `procedure_id`, domain, objective, trigger conditions, required tools, permitted permissions, typed inputs, intermediate artifacts, ordered or conditional steps, expected evidence, failure modes, termination criteria, estimated resource costs, verifier, observed success and failure family counts, provenance, source revision and expiry policy. Steps may be deterministic, model-proposed or human-reviewed, but a freeform document cannot secretly become an executable command.

Provide a limited safe procedure expression language containing `LOOKUP`, `READ`, `COMPARE`, `TRANSFORM_READONLY`, `ASSERT_EVIDENCE`, `ASK_CLARIFICATION`, `CALL_QUALIFIED_SPECIALIST`, `REQUEST_REVIEW`, `ABSTAIN` and `STOP` as initial actions. Any future effectful `APPLY_PATCH`, `RUN_TEST`, `DOWNLOAD`, `WEB_REGISTER` or `EXECUTE` opcode needs explicit host grants and qualified isolation; it cannot be unlocked by editing a procedure file. The grammar must express bounded loops and branch conditions with explicit step/time limits, not arbitrary scripting. A procedure version is content-addressed and testable against frozen fixture inputs.

Train a native policy to select an applicable procedure and parameterize it for a novel task. The trusted interpreter executes only registered actions; it checks preconditions and applicability before and after each retrieval and records deviations or interruptions. A learned policy may suggest a new procedure, but newly generated procedures remain `CANDIDATE_UNVERIFIED` until independently tested, and cannot edit their own acceptance oracle.

**Demonstration.** Solve a held-out two-source discrepancy task by retrieving a verified comparison procedure, using its correct branch, citing all inputs and detecting when a precondition no longer holds. Compare with a fixed procedure and with choosing one at random. A procedure that merely replays a memorized training sequence should not qualify as novel reasoning.

## 137. Task-graph correctness, concurrency and distributed-state safety

The autonomous task graph is a workflow engine with explicit semantics, not merely a visualization. The graph must be acyclic at submission, have typed dependencies, immutable input revision IDs, explicit project/owner identity, stable task IDs, deadlines, cancellation and retry policies. Dynamic plan revision requires a new auditable graph version and cannot silently remove a required reviewer node. A task may be retried only when effects are idempotent or a transaction/rollback protocol covers them; use idempotency keys and record actual effect status separately from proposed actions.

Define concrete states such as `DRAFT`, `VALIDATED`, `AUTHORIZED`, `QUEUED`, `RUNNING`, `WAITING_EVIDENCE`, `PENDING_REVIEW`, `COMPLETED`, `FAILED`, `CANCELED`, `BLOCKED` and `QUARANTINED`. The trusted host owns transitions. A model may request a transition, but no model output can self-declare an independently reviewed task complete or authorize a permission upgrade. Dependencies must be checked against exact accepted artifacts, not merely task titles. If a dependency is replaced or retracted, downstream cached results become invalidated or require re-verification.

Support parallelism only where independent task nodes and hardware headroom justify it. Resource locks and priority reservations prevent GPU deadlocks, unbounded model loading and starvation of user foreground work. Make cancellation cooperative and, where possible, preemptive at bounded safe points. Crash recovery should reconcile in-flight effects before retrying; a replayed download, patch, payment or remote account action is never assumed safe. Task execution over private servers must authenticate both endpoints and avoid distributing personal contexts to unapproved workers.

**Test matrix.** Dependency cycle, duplicate event replay, interrupted worker, stale resource lease, concurrent edits, timeout, GPU exhaustion, conflicting project scopes, malformed graph revision, reviewer bypass attempt, cache reuse after source change, failure during rollback and shutdown under two active tasks.

## 138. Differentiated Master competencies, disagreement and arbitration

The future Primary, Research and Engineering Masters require **distinct learned skills and objective evidence**. The Primary learns global decomposition, risk-aware delegation, budget allocation and when to stop. Research learns relevant source selection, freshness/contradiction assessment, evidence gathering and information-value estimates. Engineering learns project structure, code constraints, proposed patch/test choices and safe failure diagnosis. They may share an original tokenizer or a base model if that is a scientifically valid design, but role-specific policies, data, benchmarks and acceptance must remain explicit. Three names pointing to the same fixed routing rule are not three independent master models.

Build disagreement mechanisms that preserve independence: different evidence slices or task hypotheses where useful, blinded candidate review, separate negative-case training and a trusted adjudicator that checks empirical outcomes. Do not treat correlated model agreement as corroboration. The Research Master may supply sources to Engineering, but a source note must not be reclassified into a trusted patch instruction. The Engineering Master may propose a test, but cannot alter the private test custodian's hidden oracle. The Primary cannot force either to accept a conclusion by simply renaming a task outcome.

For an incomplete task, define escalation paths: retry with a different permitted source, ask for clarification, request an independent reviewer, fall back to an established deterministic tool, or stop. Each has a resource cost and evidence status. The default for unsupported actions is `BLOCKED`, not a spontaneous new permission. Track model confidence separately from trust and safety; the host, not any Master, enforces privileges.

**Qualification.** A three-role system should complete a meaningful set of unfamiliar multi-step tasks under a fixed compute budget; show ablations without each role and compare with the strongest single policy. Publish actual contribution, wrong decisions, abstentions, latency and cost per task family. Until gains are demonstrated, keep the role as a bounded research prototype.

## 139. Original-model architecture, tokenizer, scaling and runtime budgets

Model scaling is governed by empirical learning curves and the owner's actual workstation, not inherited fixed parameter equivalence claims. Begin with architectures sized to eligible data and measurable tasks. Candidate families can include linear/MLP baselines, compact Transformer encoders, encoder-decoder or small autoregressive language models, retrieval-aware state policies, and hybrid policies that emit structured actions. Each proposal records its objective, input/output schema, parameter count, initialization proof, tokenizer rights and lineage, maximum context, expected throughput, RAM/VRAM footprint and training compute budget. Compare quality per unit cost, not just raw parameter count.

For tokenization, test byte, character, word-piece and alternative methods on natural text, source code, paths and Unicode. Preserve exact source locations outside the tokenizer, with a reliable mapping back to original file bytes and line endings. Test an unseen identifier longer than the tokenizer's training vocabulary. Explicitly measure whether a model has learned compositional interpretation rather than memorizing prompt templates. Quantization, sparsity, KV caching, adapters and model swapping require separate compatibility tests and cannot silently change original weight provenance.

On an RTX 5080 with roughly 16 GB VRAM, 96 GB RAM and 4 TB NVMe, the practical concurrency and training budget depend on measured model, optimizer, activations, context and other running applications. Do not promise that a hypothetical 12B training run fits GPU memory merely because a quantized 12B inference configuration might fit. Compute memory estimates before launching; provide dry runs and operator-visible caps. Training CPU fallback may be slow; display real progress rather than fabricated time estimates. Support deterministic seeds when possible, but document kernels or floating-point behavior that prevent identical results across hardware.

**Model-size release rule.** A larger candidate earns its footprint only if it improves independent task outcomes or produces an explicitly justified research result at acceptable cost. A 1B target is a medium-term exploration goal, not a universal minimum, and the proposed 2,000+ roles are an expandable catalog rather than a mandate to train thousands of heavy checkpoints.

## 140. Continual learning, forgetting, reversible model upgrades and retirement

Improvement is not just whether a new checkpoint has higher accuracy on a recent batch. Build a **longitudinal competence ledger** tracking prior strong skills, new curricula, known catastrophic-forgetting cases, calibration shifts, throughput changes and safety regressions. Keep immutable baseline checkpoints, optimizer states where needed, tokenizer versions and model/data lineage. Periodic rehearsal may use eligible historical examples, but training cannot read the independent protected evaluator's hidden responses.

At every upgrade, compare on old accepted tasks, new unfamiliar tasks, hostile abstention cases and real owner-authorized use. Reject a candidate if it improves average performance but exceeds a preregistered harmful wrong-delivery budget or loses critical security behavior. Multiple candidate variants should have separate experiment IDs, no survivor bias in reporting, and explicit invalidation when the training dataset is discovered to have corrupted or unauthorized records. Checkpoint retirement must preserve rollback until a later independent cutover proves safe.

Distinguish `TRAINED`, `EVALUATED`, `SHADOW_QUALIFIED`, `CANARY_APPROVED`, `PRODUCTION_APPROVED`, `SUPERSEDED` and `REVOKED`. A successful source-selection policy cannot be silently upgraded to host action authority or model-training rights. A model's privileges depend on independently qualified capability and owner grants, not model size, age or an internal "Supreme Master" title.

**Acceptance.** Reproduce interrupted training with exact optimizer-state continuity where claimed; test a saved model after restart; simulate a tokenizer or training-license revocation; prove a rejected candidate never becomes active; compare regressions by skill; and demonstrate rollback without loss of previous trusted knowledge or project files.

## 141. Independent evaluation custody and adversarial red teaming

The evaluator must be protected from the implementation and training pipeline, whether the implementation author is Sol, Argon or an internal ATLAS specialist. A protected test custodian owns the task family manifest, hidden inputs, expected results, scoring code and review receipts. Candidate code receives a controlled interface to run on inputs but not to enumerate or modify the held-out oracle. The evaluator checks source-level behavior, executable outputs, package effects, process/network traces and model lineage as appropriate.

Create adversarial classes for: syntactically plausible but incorrect code; wrong overloaded method binding; Python Unicode and malformed syntax; Unicode/path aliasing; content poisoning; source changes during task execution; task journal truncation; private-data exposure; train/test contamination; forged model checkpoint metadata; output self-certification; unsupported knowledge; case-sensitive vs case-insensitive filesystems; cancellation/restart races; and apparently high accuracy achieved through blanket abstention. Fuzz bounded parsers and document/schema decoders with reproducible seeds and fixed timeouts. Pair property-based testing with targeted regression tests; never claim full formal correctness merely because a fuzzer found no problems.

Evidence receipts use signed or access-controlled manifests when feasible and independently verifiable hashes. A matching digest alone is not sufficient if it is bound to the wrong request, project, source revision or evaluator role. Implement review receipts that bind all these fields, with monotonic versions and accurate counts. The reviewer must not silently modify the candidate while evaluating it; if it repairs defects, that new revision becomes a new author-owned candidate requiring independent verification.

**Scientific gate.** Where possible, preregister the chosen primary metric, task family split, significance threshold, wrong-answer constraints, hardware budget and fallback policy before fitting. All skipped, timed-out or unavailable tests are reported as such. Avoid creating a benchmark that measures the training policy's own definition rather than the user's real task.

## 142. Host isolation threat model: Windows filesystem and process boundaries

ATLAS's trusted Windows host is a separate security principal from model-generated text, downloaded bytes and sandboxed tasks. Enumerate attack surfaces: Win32 path normalization, NTFS alternate data streams, junctions, symbolic links, hard links, short 8.3 aliases, drive-relative paths, UNC/device paths, trailing dots/spaces, case folding, reserved device names, archive traversal, environment variables, shell metacharacters, inherited handles and race conditions between preflight and use. A lexical string check alone is not sufficient; the host must resolve real objects through suitable platform APIs and revalidate immediately before every privileged operation.

Use capability-scoped filesystem access: read-only snapshots by default; controlled staging outside trusted source; destination allowlists and canonical path checks; no ambient home-directory read; no arbitrary process invocation; no network access in code test sandboxes unless specifically authorized. For privileged patch application, verify exact base revision and diff before merge. Create a trusted external rollback point and prove restore. Do not let project source files embed instructions that raise execution authority. Test denial behavior as carefully as successful execution.

Treat virtual machines, WSL, containers and Windows Sandbox as *different isolation technologies*, not interchangeable guarantees. A guest VM is not considered trustworthy merely because its process starts. Verify storage mapping, host clipboard and drag/drop, network interfaces, DNS, proxy configuration, shared folders, hypervisor escape boundaries, guest-initiated process paths, image integrity and clean snapshot restoration. Unverified execution environments remain disabled; fallback to the physical host is forbidden. Personal project data must not be mounted read/write into untrusted guests by convenience.

**Concrete security tests.** Reproduce the legacy Arena case/alias/path class; attempt traversal via all supported archive formats; attempt overwrite through reparse points or swapped junctions; attempt ADS writes, drive-relative paths and bad journal recovery; verify that denied operations do not read or copy protected bytes even temporarily. Keep evaluator private directories outside every candidate workspace and archive.

## 143. Network research, remote isolation, anonymity limits and online identity

The default ATLAS mode is offline. When the owner grants web research, a read-only Research Scout uses allowlisted outbound services, source-specific provenance and network logging. Treat HTTP response bodies, HTML, PDF metadata, browser UI and embedded instructions as untrusted task data. Never automatically interpret a page's login prompt, subscription suggestion, ad, payment screen or download button as permission. A fetched fact may be copied into a candidate source record, not directly into trusted knowledge. Promotion requires claim-level evidence and independent validation.

Registration for ATLAS-created online accounts and risky download execution must occur only through an approved isolated remote environment; the user's physical computer must never log into such accounts. Separate identities, credentials and storage from the owner's real accounts. Do not use the owner's name, personal email, payment methods, saved cookies or browser profile. Remote isolation should reduce exposure of the home IP/location, but must not falsely guarantee anonymity, legal compliance or malware-free downloads. Explicitly log uncertainty about what network services can see.

Network egress controls need destination and proxy validation, DNS rebinding defenses, certificate checks, redirects, IP-literal rejection where configured, local-metadata endpoint denial, rate limits, and protection against using legitimate-looking external domains as exfiltration targets. No silent fallback from the permitted remote guest to a host browser or host-level terminal if the environment is unavailable. A networked model provider is not authorized merely because a loopback app registered a default model.

**Acceptance tests.** Attempt embedded instruction injection in a retrieved page; attempt a redirect from permitted HTTPS to loopback, private-address and unapproved remote endpoints; attempt account creation without a grant; attempt using existing host browser cookies; simulate proxy failure; verify the task is blocked with an inspectable explanation. Retain source and connection evidence without leaking private browsing contents into general training records.

## 144. Secrets, prompt injection, data poisoning and trusted execution separation

A model sees files, chats and web content as *data*; none of them can change host permissions, modify evaluation rules, authorize money, or redefine project scope. Implement an explicit instruction trust stack: trusted host and owner authorization > canonical constitution and developer-assigned tasks > external knowledge and tools > untrusted source text. Retrieved code comments or Markdown front matter saying "ignore previous instructions" must be rendered inert at the trust boundary. Only the owner through a trusted UI may change grants for an effectful operation.

Secret handling must use secure OS or service credential storage when introduced, with purpose-limited access, short lifetimes, rotation and revocation. Never copy passwords, authentication cookies, API keys, recovery codes or personal account identifiers into model prompts, logs, training sets or diagnostic packages. Before exporting support evidence or a task archive, scan for incidental secret-bearing material and permit a reviewable redaction path. A model-generated claim that data is public is not authority to publish it.

Data poisoning can affect both knowledge and model training. Require provenance checks at ingestion, source isolation, anomalous label detection, independent oracle validation and dataset revision logging. Quarantine a corrupted source rather than overwriting trusted knowledge with its claims. A verified document can still include false assertions outside the independently checked claims; do not elevate the whole file's trust state because one field was validated.

**Attack exercises.** Attempt to inject a new action through a source excerpt; include an encoded secret in an ordinary-looking citation; swap a training manifest after checkpoint creation; replay a valid receipt for a different request; feed an instruction in a web result to alter the canonical master; run an internal model that confidently asserts it already has owner approval. All must fail closed without losing original evidence.

## 145. Backups, migrations, power-loss recovery and owner-controlled restoration

ATLAS must outlive a crash, Windows update, storage migration, model-training interruption and accidental program reinstall without losing approved knowledge or the owner's project. Define separate backup classes for immutable source snapshots, verified knowledge and procedures, public training datasets, native checkpoints and optimizer state, task journals, application configuration, permissions, and private memory. Personal/private backups must remain encrypted and access-separated. Protected evaluator material requires a separate custodian policy and must not be copied into regular data archives.

A restore point includes application version, data-schema versions, content hashes, encryption-key recovery requirements, expected disk footprint and dependencies. Database migration is a transaction with dry-run validation, backup before destructive change, forward and rollback strategy and no silent reinterpretation of trust states. Reindexing must be able to recreate search indexes from immutable originals and metadata; corruption of an index should not invalidate an otherwise intact source. Training interruption must not falsely claim full optimizer continuity if only model weights survived.

Implement an owner-friendly "Verify backup" and "Test restore" workflow that can run on an isolated test profile without overwriting the current installation. Show a human-readable recovery report listing which artifacts were restored and which cannot be decrypted or replayed. Automated retention/cleanup never deletes accepted checkpoints, provenance, private memory or prior negative results without a separately bounded owner policy. During a power loss, use atomic manifests, journaling and checksummed data so a partially written checkpoint does not become the default model.

**Acceptance suite.** Force shutdown during source import, evidence promotion, journal write, model checkpoint save and a simulated migration. Reopen the app; verify exact byte hashes and trust states, source scope and model registry, task-state recovery, no leaked permissions, and an explicit error for unrecoverable partial data.

## 146. Personal data lifecycle, per-user identity and meaningful deletion

The owner must be able to use ATLAS without having personal information silently converted into reusable training data. Separate `PUBLIC_KNOWLEDGE`, `PROJECT_INTERNAL`, `PERSONAL_OWNER_ONLY`, `PRIVATE_PLAYGROUND_EPHEMERAL`, `TRAINING_ELIGIBLE`, `EVALUATOR_PRIVATE` and `QUARANTINED` namespaces. Permissions follow artifact IDs and remain enforced even if a file is copied, indexed, summarized, embedded or converted into a checkpoint. Default private memory and user-project content to not trainable; absence of a consent record is denial.

Provide a **data lineage ledger** connecting a saved user record to summaries, indexes, embedding vectors, exports and any later authorized training derivative. For correction, preserve provenance and make the new version current without pretending old claims never existed. For deletion, show exact categories and scopes affected: source files, index records, caches, derived summaries, exports under ATLAS's control and any trained checkpoints that might require retraining or withdrawal. Where cryptographic deletion is supported, describe its real coverage and remaining backups. Do not claim that existing published or third-party data can be erased by deleting one local pointer.

Private Playground must avoid ATLAS persistence of chat content in history, evidence, logs, model training or analytics. Explain and test the retention behavior of any local inference server separately; it may maintain its own logs. Personal ATLAS uses explicit category grants, opt-in encrypted storage and owner-readable inspect/edit/export/delete controls. A Personal Style/Thinking Counterpart is opt-in only, must never be used to impersonate the owner publicly, and cannot use personal identity for online registrations or payments.

**Verification.** Conduct cross-user and cross-project denial tests, export-rights tests, training-consent revocation and profile deletion rehearsals. Include a check that background training cannot indirectly consume private context through a retrieved summary or feature vector. Personal categories and user consent cannot be silently upgraded by a model-suggested action.

## 147. Honest UX, explainability and operator control at every maturity level

A capable owner-facing AI needs a simple path from **goal to useful outcome** while retaining traceability. Maintain three interface layers: a beginner-friendly "Ask ATLAS / Do a Task" experience, a project/research/developer workspace for deeper work, and an advanced model/experiment/security console. The UI should always identify the active original model, whether a pretrained reference is involved, what knowledge was read, whether the result was independently verified, whether a permission is being requested, and how to cancel. Do not make the owner read a model trace to accomplish normal tasks.

Build polished error and limitation states: insufficient source evidence, unsupported file or language, revoked model qualification, offline model unavailable, GPU budget exceeded, awaiting owner approval, protected reviewer pending, user data intentionally excluded, and inability to safely run a task. Each should offer a bounded next action that does not misrepresent what happened. Background activity must yield to foreground work and disclose resource use; a high-level task overview and optional detailed inspector keep advanced observability accessible without clutter.

Accessibility is functional: keyboard-only navigation, screen reader labels from dynamic source text, color-independent status, scalable typography, reduced animation, visible focus, task cancellation discoverability and usable 720p/1080p/1440p/4K DPI layouts. Remember per-project workspaces, not unapproved private inputs. Provide tooltips explaining "shadow model," "verified," "candidate," "reference model" and "original training" in ordinary language, without misleading quality badges. No decorative dashboard metric may masquerade as a live sensor measurement.

**Owner journey gate.** A first-time user should be able to open ATLAS offline, select a project, ask a supported native-only question, inspect the evidence, understand a failure or abstention, and stop or export the result. A separate lab journey should train a tiny original candidate, display actual metrics, compare it with baselines and show explicit rejection when appropriate.

## 148. Provenance graph, versioned evidence and knowledge decay

Knowledge is not simply "a text file with confidence." The authoritative storage design needs an immutable source layer, extract/claim layer, derived knowledge/procedure layer, and graph of relations: `SUPPORTS`, `CONTRADICTS`, `SUPERSEDES`, `DERIVED_FROM`, `TESTED_BY`, `APPLIES_TO`, `USED_IN_TASK`, `TRAINED_ON` and `RETRACTED_BY`. Each edge carries evidence IDs, actor, timestamp, trust state, revision and applicable scope. Physical file paths are resolved behind stable logical IDs; moving or reindexing a source cannot silently change its identity.

A claim can be *faithfully extracted from a source* yet false in the world. Distinguish `SOURCE_QUOTED`, `SOURCE_SUPPORTED`, `INDEPENDENTLY_VERIFIED`, `DISPUTED`, `OUTDATED`, `RETRACTED` and `UNKNOWN`. A source's reputation is context-dependent and never substitutes for verifying the exact claim. Record relevant source publication date, retrieval time, review time, expiry conditions and method used to corroborate. For a procedure, track observed outcomes and applicability rather than a single global success percentage that hides project mismatch.

Create freshness policies based on source type: language syntax and compiler flags change by version, government statistics change by date, personal memory depends on owner correction, and a project's source changes by commit. Prior answers must be recheckable against current inputs while retaining the original receipt. Summaries remain navigational hints, never replacements for exact underlying evidence. Semantic embeddings, caches and entity indexes are reconstructable from source; cache keys include scope, trust state and revision to prevent cross-project leakage.

**Acceptance.** Modify one file to contradict two earlier sources; ATLAS detects that a previously verified comparison needs rechecking but does not erase the historical outcome. Retract a claim and ensure dependent procedures and learning examples become candidates for revalidation rather than silently staying trusted. Verify knowledge lookup still works after moving the file storage directory and rebuilding indexes.

## 149. Project engineering from a user request to a verified patch

The mature coding assistant must go beyond file lookup and templated snippets. It should parse a project manifest and dependency structure, identify the relevant source surface, select a constrained plan, produce a complete context-aware patch or paste-ready code, run authorized tests in qualified isolation, independently review the change, and present a traceable diff with exact paths and insertion points. The user should receive instructions for configurable fields, imports, tool versions, expected result and rollback, not a vague "add this somewhere" suggestion.

Treat a change as a transaction: original Git commit and source hashes; explicit allowed file list; intended diff and security checks; sandbox copy with restricted capabilities; formatter/linter/build/test commands; recorded output; reviewer verdict; owner approval; atomic application to the real project and post-apply validation; rollback if required. Do not write into the owner's live game or code files before explicit approval. When test tools are absent, produce a *proposal labeled untested*, not a fictitious green check.

Support languages and engines by measured capability matrices: Python, C#, C++/Unreal, Unity C#, Verse/UEFN, Rust, TypeScript/JavaScript/HTML/CSS, Blender Python and later domain-specific systems. For Unreal Blueprint, distinguish generated text specification from an actual importable graph asset; for game engines distinguish project settings, engine version, plugin requirements and asset pipeline from stand-alone source code. Do not promise successful compilation without a matching toolchain.

**Code-evolution evidence.** Evaluate unfamiliar repositories with seeded bugs, competing source hypotheses, multi-file dependencies, test flakiness and reverting regressions. Compare deterministic search plus human-copy assistance, native learned routing/planning, and optional reference-model drafting under equal budgets. Preserve rejected patches and reviewer findings for future procedural learning only when the outcomes are genuinely verified.

## 150. Creative production, game development and artifact-centered testing

ATLAS should eventually create useful game systems, simulations, sprites, character concepts, scenes, textures, UI assets, levels, manuscripts and other artifacts. Treat each output type as its own contract: source project/asset metadata, editable canonical format, artistic or gameplay objectives, file version, license/rights, actual preview, objective validation where possible, owner aesthetic judgment and known limitations. A generated screenshot is not a working game level, a prose description is not a Blender `.blend`, and a code block is not an Unreal Blueprint graph.

For games, require playable or independently simulated test scenes and metrics that matter to the game design: controls, frame-time stability, collision behavior, save/load, spawning, enemy behavior, UI responsiveness, difficulty, replayability, network boundaries and asset import integrity. For art and writing, preserve project continuity bibles, character facts, story events, source constraints, style authorizations, revision lineage and exportable files. Avoid claiming objective artistic superiority from model confidence alone; use consistent owner-informed rubrics and, where meaningful, blinded preference tests.

Large 3D and pixel/voxel/world creation belongs to the separately governed PLATZ Engine roadmap. ATLAS should eventually interface via a permissioned protocol to perform tasks, never merge the repositories or take over the PLATZ renderer. Generated source and assets must carry provenance and rights metadata, and default to owner-private until explicitly shared.

**Demonstration gates.** A generated Unity component should compile in the specified Unity version or be labeled untested; a Blender script should create an actual mesh in an isolated fixture; a small game feature should pass objective input/output tests; a chapter should preserve at least the declared story continuity constraints. In every case a reviewer can inspect the actual artifact and tests, not just a rendered preview.

## 151. Multimodal intelligence and assistive modalities without false claims

ATLAS's long-term capacity may include locally processed speech, image understanding, video summaries, diagrams, code screenshots, spatial/3D information, MIDI or audio descriptions, and camera/desktop inputs. These are optional specialist capabilities and must not become implicit collection of a user's microphone, camera or screen. Each modality requires explicit permission, task-specific scope and a user-visible indicator. Images and screenshots can contain private names, credentials and addresses; extract only the needed approved content and restrict logs and training use.

Separate *perception* from *generation* and *tool action*. An image classifier trained from scratch is not a general visual reasoner. A text-to-image prototype is not evidence of autonomous 3D content creation. Source-image metadata, crop/coordinate transforms, provenance, uncertainty, copyright and output format must be preserved. For speech include recognition, speaker boundary, language, local retention and error correction; for video include frame selection, timestamps and authenticity. A safe robot/desktop action needs a qualified computer-use execution boundary and cannot be authorized by visual model output alone.

For accessibility, invest in natural-language and multimodal explanations that remain verifiable and allow owner override, but do not fabricate captions or OCR text where uncertain. English-first internal training is a sensible first step; translation specialists should preserve named entities, code, technical terms and original citations when handling Japanese or other languages. A translated passage must remain traceable to its original.

**Gate.** Qualify every modality with its own out-of-distribution tests, latency/memory costs, privacy boundary and fallback. Do not list a media feature as complete merely because an optional pretrained provider can produce an example.

## 152. Performance, energy, failure budgets and the owner's real workstation

Measure user-facing latency in cold start, warm inference, first-token delay if applicable, model-swap time, retrieval time, full task duration, UI interaction responsiveness and time to safe cancellation. Record CPU, GPU memory, system RAM, storage writes, thermal throttling and energy use where instrumentation actually exists. Do not substitute fixed historical microsecond promises or parameter-equivalence ratios for a benchmark on the owner's present hardware. Reports include sampling methodology, task size, model/checkpoint identity, device state and p50/p95 where sample sizes support them.

Define separate budgets for foreground work, idle experiments, background research, model training, inference and isolated test execution. The scheduler must pause or throttle training when foreground GPU demand increases and must safely honor the owner stop control. No workload is allowed to starve the desktop or fill the 4 TB NVMe with retained checkpoints without an explicit storage/retention quota. Avoid hard-coded VRAM assumptions; inspect the available headroom at runtime and handle insufficient capacity by serializing models, selecting a smaller qualified checkpoint, or withholding the task.

Track total cost per verified completion. When local pretrained references are optional, compare complete task costs inclusive of model setup, tokens, tools, verification, network/energy and human review; do not claim that a 37-parameter policy is "faster AI" when the deterministic host performs all substantive work. A native system can earn value by lower latency or resource use even when final accuracy ties, but that result must be measured under controlled conditions.

**SLO policy.** Establish milestone-specific service objectives for crash-free launches, data integrity, prompt cancellation, no wrong trusted answers on protected safety tests, and predictable degradation with offline or missing models. Failing safety or owner-data integrity controls overrides cosmetic performance wins.

## 153. Dependency supply chain, licensing and reproducible release artifacts

All code dependencies, runtimes, model checkpoint sources, datasets, licenses and build tools should be discoverable and versioned. Maintain a software bill of materials (SBOM) and a model/data bill of materials: exact packages, executable hashes, original checkpoint source, license constraints, download location, whether code is open source, and whether artifact redistribution is permitted. Test untrusted update packages and reject altered or unlicensed binaries; never automatically install a model merely because a task requests one.

The Windows application release should be reproducible from source and pinned toolchains as much as practical, with deterministic packaging manifest, archive inventory, known runtime prerequisites, offline install and rollback documentation. Security-sensitive dependencies receive explicit review for network behaviors, telemetry, executable hooks and license changes. Any telemetry is owner opt-in, minimized and independently auditable; no "anonymous" label is enough to justify collecting private prompts or project content.

Maintain clean-room separation when using third-party reference models and data. Open-source ML libraries are acceptable implementation tools for original training; pretrained weights are different artifacts with separate provenance. If a downloaded open-source project includes examples with personal data or unclear reuse rights, it is not automatically eligible for ATLAS's shared knowledge or training set.

**Release gate.** A clean Windows profile installs and launches offline; package manifest checks all files; no model checkpoint differs unexpectedly; revoking an optional model provider does not break native-only operations; and the full release identifies exactly which qualifications are pending, which features are gated and which tests could not run.

## 154. Long-term observability, operations, maintenance and total cost

The owner needs to understand the cost of running an AI laboratory for months or years. Track actual disk usage by project, knowledge snapshots, data versions, temporary sandboxes, checkpoints, optimizer states, caches, audit receipts and training logs. Use deduplication only with correct permission boundaries; two owners' private artifacts cannot be merged into a globally readable content-addressed store. Define quotas and retention choices with a preview and reversible trash policy where practical.

Operational dashboards should show actual task and training throughput, failure reasons, background resource budget, last independent accepted baseline, active candidate checkpoints and pending approvals. Train/eval runs remain replayable after software upgrades; if an old evaluator can no longer be executed, preserve its source and receipt and mark it as a historical nonreproducible result rather than silently rewriting the metric. Store structured diagnostics with stable schema and appropriate privacy scrubbing. Protect the trusted host's log and source-review metadata from agent editing.

Define product maintenance responsibilities: issue triage, source/version upgrade compatibility, deprecation, migration rehearsals, model retraining needs, security patch review and disaster recovery. Ensure a single-writer handoff and signed-off document revisions so future Sol/Argon sessions cannot overwrite each other's evidence. Evaluate whether the modular complexity is providing useful completed tasks; remove or consolidate redundant components rather than keeping thousands of dormant files only to satisfy role-count optics.

**Owner check.** After a month of usage, ATLAS can produce a concise ledger of meaningful completed tasks, native-only completions, costs, saved knowledge, retired models, regressions and unresolved gaps. Historical errors and failed experiments remain visible.

## 155. PLATZ Engine integration: a narrow, versioned interoperability contract

PLATZ Engine, PLATZ Studio, PLATZ Core, PLATZ Runtime and PLATZ Hub are a separate product family under PLATZ Technologies. PLATZ's canonical master is governed by its own repository and editor/renderer roadmap. ATLAS MUST NOT copy the PLATZ source tree into ATLAS Next, modify FORGE, redefine PLATZ's asset format or quietly turn ATLAS's document library into a game-engine database. Integration remains future and optional; these products can succeed independently.

When authorized, define a versioned **PLATZ Project Adapter** that communicates through stable project IDs, opaque asset IDs, read-only metadata, scene/component queries, task descriptions, previews and later bounded edit proposals. Maintain separate security principals and owner approvals for all effectful project actions. A PLATZ scene is created by its actual engine/editor and verified through real rendering, not merely inferred from ATLAS-generated code. Preserve exact engine version, graphics backend, hardware profile and asset-transform settings in task evidence.

ATLAS specialists should eventually be able to propose material graphs, behaviors in Variables→Events→Conditions→Actions, scene setup, level organization, sprite/voxel workflows and performant C#/C++/script interfaces as appropriate. Their proposals must respect PLATZ's authoritative data model, edit history and undo/redo architecture. Preserve the PLATZ objective of accessible no-code workflows plus precise code extensibility, GPU/Vulkan-first rendering with suitable fallbacks and verifiable 2D/2.5D/3D/pixel/voxel output.

**Interoperability gate.** A read-only adapter demonstrates correct project inventory and asset references with owner-supplied fixtures. Only after independent review and permission may an isolated candidate apply a versioned edit into a disposable PLATZ project, render a preview, compare it with the expected behavior and offer a reversible owner-approved merge.

## 156. Self-improvement economics, Goodhart resistance and experiment stopping

A self-improvement system can accidentally optimize the measured score instead of useful intelligence. Establish **anti-Goodhart** controls: frozen task families, blinded independent tests, explicit wrong-delivery penalties, task diversity, model-free baselines, negative-outcome retention, and a cap on how frequently an exposed benchmark may guide design. If a benchmark saturates, version a new unseen challenge without rewriting prior history. Repeated hyperparameter searches on the same protected test are not independent evaluation.

Select candidate projects by expected outcome benefit relative to estimated experiment cost and safety impact. A model that improves one source family while degrading another should be assessed by task priority and the preregistered acceptance policy, not pooled accuracy alone. Track whether learned success comes from memorized aliases, leaked project metadata, a hidden reference-model call or actual transferable decisions. An upgrade with lower training loss, more parameters, faster inference or higher headline accuracy is not necessarily better for the owner.

Provide stopping rules for insufficient eligible data, repeated failed trials, unstable seeds, thermal/resource budgets, inability to reproduce checkpoints, no verified causal improvement and evaluator access violations. The Training Director may propose additional tasks or a revised hypothesis, but cannot secretly run unlimited experiments or self-certify a breakthrough. Failed experiments and discarded architectures become durable negative lessons only after correct source-level evidence and context are stored.

**Release criterion.** The owner can ask "Did ATLAS get better?" and receive a task-family-by-task-family comparison, accepted confidence/limitations, actual cost, regression list, independent review identity and an explicit `NO_VERIFIED_IMPROVEMENT` when appropriate.

## 157. Architecture governance, drift prevention and design-decision records

The canonical master is a durable product constitution, not a file every coding agent can rewrite to justify its latest implementation. Keep a separate register of **Architecture Decision Records (ADRs)** with a stable ID, problem, constraints, options, selected design, why alternatives were rejected, security effect, benchmark evidence, compatibility and migration plan. Every material departure from a binding product doctrine needs an owner-reviewed proposal, not an unnoticed rewrite. The master may evolve but requires an explicit revision receipt, source crosswalk update, independent consistency scan and owner-visible summary of changes.

A feature is maintained in a machine-readable requirements ledger with `requirement_id`, owner intent source, current status, claimed code paths, test paths, acceptance receipt, revision, blockers and next action. The actual code and independently accepted receipts outrank stale progress summaries for statements about current behavior. The master defines future goals; the handoff defines today's task; the source and tests decide what works. An internal learned agent may retrieve the relevant part of the master for its task, but the model's instructions do not replace trusted host permissions or tool schemas.

Run an automated **non-drift check** before handoff: verify canonical hashes and current master revision; ensure no newly authored text promotes Qwen into mandatory native intelligence; ensure no new roles are counted as trained without checkpoint evidence; ensure private source material has not entered public Git; ensure the accepted baseline and review statuses are coherent; ensure no source directories for legacy ATLAS, PLATZ or FORGE were modified. Distinguish `CONFLICTING_HISTORICAL_PROPOSAL`, `SUPERSEDED_IMPLEMENTATION_HINT`, `RETAINED_LONG_TERM_GOAL` and `CURRENT_BINDING_REQUIREMENT` in the source crosswalk.

**Operational rule.** Future Sol and Argon agents read this current master (or its full relevant sections with a verifiable version), the canonical handoff and the acceptance history before changing the repository. They write a completion handoff containing exact commits, tests, provisional features, owner approvals needed and next task. No arbitrary concurrent edits to the same handoff file; no contacting counterpart through Computer Use.

## 158. NEXT-021 through NEXT-025: harder, audit-ready release definitions

**NEXT-021 Data & Knowledge Foundry.** A working CLI or UI command ingests multiple authorized project families, creates hash-bound eligible outcome records, produces a versioned dataset card, stores a knowledge/procedure graph, reports unique task families, and rejects at least the following cases: duplicate leakage, swapped request digest, wrong evaluator identity, revoked permission, unlicensed source and public/protected set overlap. A dataset with zero legitimate positive examples is rejected as inadequate for ordinary supervised learning, even if it is useful for abstention studies. The output is a real dataset and evidence ledger, not just a folder hierarchy.

**NEXT-022 Original Language.** A newly initialized model trained on authorized original tasks can map novel paraphrases and combinations to a typed goal representation; it is evaluated on source/project families absent from fitting, with lexical, deterministic, n-gram and linear comparators. It preserves exact source constraints and acknowledges unsupported requests. If training data is too small for meaningful generation, demonstrate grounded structured language understanding honestly; do not make a fluency claim based on borrowed reference output. Record tokenizer/model hashes, actual training metrics and memory/latency.

**NEXT-023 Procedures.** A bounded policy chooses sequences of at least two distinct read-only actions, consults a versioned verified procedure and adapts to a missing or contradictory source. The controller enforces action limits, source revisions and stop/abstain decisions. Use evaluation cases where step order and choice matter; compare with fixed pipeline and simple learned planners. Save actual action trajectories and reviewer outcomes, not merely generated plan text.

**NEXT-024 Master Cooperation.** Three genuinely differentiated role policies or a scientifically justified shared original model with distinct role-conditioned competencies solve unfamiliar composite tasks under one frozen budget. Compare to the best single policy and deterministic orchestration. Prove which learned choice changed the resulting path. Master consensus alone cannot confer truth, and unqualified model authority stays shadow-only.

**NEXT-025 Self-Improvement.** ATLAS identifies an eligible weakness, selects new authorized data, freezes a candidate experiment, trains from an independently tracked baseline, evaluates without hidden-set leakage, and retains or rejects the candidate based on preregistered downstream criteria. If no model actually improves, preserve the failed experiment as valid research and state `NO_VERIFIED_IMPROVEMENT`; do not mark the objective "autonomous learning breakthrough" complete. A production promotion remains separately gated by independent Argon acceptance and owner authority.

**Batch-level must-pass demonstration.** An unfamiliar offline project task runs with all pretrained services disabled, uses the learned language interface (if qualified) to construct a typed goal, retrieves facts and a procedure, selects a native specialist, records the path, produces a host-verified answer or a justified abstention, writes a provenance receipt, and shows a side-by-side comparison to the incumbent deterministic route. If any component remains unqualified, the demonstration honestly labels its fallback and the integrated batch remains scoped to what was truly achieved.

## 159. Definition of an actual product release: user journeys and acceptance witnesses

A feature may be called implemented when source exists; author-tested when repeatable product tests pass; independently accepted only after a reviewer-controlled test; and qualified for trusted authority only after the relevant permissions and safety gates. Every owner-facing release note must include **three kinds of proof**: (a) real application experience, (b) source/test evidence, and (c) causal capability measurements. A screenshot of a new button alone is not evidence of the feature behind it.

Define five permanent owner journeys: (1) open ATLAS offline and complete a native-only verified evidence task; (2) import a real authorized code project and receive a correct project-aware explanation with source citations; (3) ask ATLAS to research a multi-document contradiction and later recheck it; (4) train an ATLAS-original specialist using approved outcomes and observe a fair evaluation; (5) request an isolated coding change, inspect a diff and independently verified test receipt before approving any application. Later add an owner-authorized safe computer task and an independent PLATZ project adapter journey. Each journey should be replayable after restart and a fresh install.

Maintain a capability grid listing `AVAILABLE_NATIVELY`, `AVAILABLE_DETERMINISTICALLY`, `AVAILABLE_REFERENCE_ASSISTED`, `SHADOW_RESEARCH`, `OWNER_APPROVAL_REQUIRED`, `BLOCKED_SECURITY`, `UNAVAILABLE`, and `FUTURE`. Surface that grid in the product UI and the owner report. A feature must not become trusted because it is visible in the navigation. Every result should name its source/model identities and receipt, with a human-readable explanation of limitations and what to do next.

At release, Argon should independently challenge the entire frozen Sol batch, with verdicts and raw evidence for each update plus integrated behavior.
