The accepted baseline remains unchanged for any blocked dependency. Report five-update progress as demonstrated capabilities, not a count of milestones. The owner must have a clear recovery path to the last independently accepted and packaged version.

## 160. Source archives, historical completeness and owner acceptance registry

The unabridged companion document and supplied original bytes are part of the archival provenance of this living constitution. They preserve old model names, large role catalogs, prior hardware claims, proposed numeric targets, testing plans, UI ideas and specialist examples that would be cumbersome or dangerous to turn into a single current to-do list. The canonical master SHALL have a source crosswalk: every original document ID and major heading maps to one of `ACTIVE_CURRENT`, `HISTORICAL_REFERENCE`, `SUPERSEDED`, `PROPOSED_RESEARCH` or `NOT_SUPPLIED`. It must preserve the exact 50 historical requirement IDs and the owner's original mission goals and indicate what has or has not been implemented.

The absence of historical source ZIPs is not permission to reconstruct their contents as if authentic. In particular, old references to multi-thousand specialist records, personal-vault biographies, and a numbered 200-initiative archive must be labeled missing if their exact bytes were not provided. New initiative lists may be useful but must declare themselves reconstructed. Missing private material should never be requested for the general master or bundled in public Git; require explicit owner selection and encrypted private storage before handling it.

Add a separate **owner decision register** for unresolved product choices: preferred offline-only default, reference-model fallback rules, native inference graduation thresholds, acceptable background GPU/energy budget, private-memory retention, guest isolation provider, permitted remote research sources, and whether a new experimental model can receive live action authority. An open owner choice is not a license for Sol or Argon to guess permission. The system should offer safe reversible defaults and propose decisions with consequences, rather than blocking harmless work waiting for answers.

**Transfer verification.** A generated transfer archive records SHA-256 of the full canonical Markdown, Word edition, source originals, crosswalk, requirements trace and agent prompts. The master and prompts use the same document version string. The repository installer verifies expected files and refuses to overwrite a newer owner-approved master without diff review.

# PART XXII — GAP-FILLED OPERATIONAL CONTRACTS & GOVERNANCE PLAYBOOK (v3.2 ADDITIONS)

> **Provenance Notice (v3.2 — 2026-11-19):** Part XXII (Chapters 161–166) is a newly authored operational contract expansion added during the v3.2 specification audit to close concrete schema, state-machine, security, hardware-arbitration, and repository-governance gaps. Every contract below is `PROPOSED` until implemented and independently verified.

## 161. Strict Data Foundry schema and family-level decontamination firewall (`NEXT-021`)

All Foundry outcome records MUST validate with `additionalProperties: false` at trust boundaries. Records lacking cryptographic binding to exact request bytes, immutable source digests, and independent verifier receipts are routed to `quarantine/`.

```
{
  "$schema": "https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema",
  "title": "AtlasOutcomeRecordV1",
  "type": "object",
  "additionalProperties": false,
  "required": [
    "schema_version", "outcome_id", "task_id", "task_request_sha256",
    "project_id", "project_family_id", "template_family_id", "author_id",
    "source_snapshots", "model_origin", "checkpoint_sha256",
    "action_proposed", "action_executed", "host_overrode_proposal",
    "verifier_id", "verifier_version", "evaluation_receipt_sha256",
    "outcome_class", "failure_taxonomy", "data_rights_class",
    "consent_grant_id", "eligible_for_training", "recorded_at_utc"
  ],
  "properties": {
    "schema_version": { "const": "atlas.outcome_record.v1" },
    "outcome_id": { "type": "string", "pattern": "^out-[a-z0-9-]{8,64}$" },
    "task_id": { "type": "string" },
    "task_request_sha256": { "type": "string", "pattern": "^[a-f0-9]{64}$" },
    "project_id": { "type": "string" },
    "project_family_id": { "type": "string" },
    "template_family_id": { "type": "string" },
    "author_id": { "type": "string" },
    "source_snapshots": {
      "type": "array",
      "minItems": 1,
      "items": {
        "type": "object",
        "additionalProperties": false,
        "required": ["source_id", "source_revision", "sha256"],
        "properties": {
          "source_id": { "type": "string" },
          "source_revision": { "type": "integer", "minimum": 1 },
          "sha256": { "type": "string", "pattern": "^[a-f0-9]{64}$" }
        }
      }
    },
    "model_origin": {
      "enum": ["ATLAS_ORIGINAL_SCRATCH", "ADAPTED_PRETRAINED", "LOCAL_EXTERNAL_REF", "HOSTED_EXTERNAL", "DETERMINISTIC_0B"]
    },
    "checkpoint_sha256": { "type": ["string", "null"], "pattern": "^[a-f0-9]{64}$" },
    "action_proposed": { "type": "string" },
    "action_executed": { "type": "string" },
    "host_overrode_proposal": { "type": "boolean" },
    "verifier_id": { "type": "string" },
    "verifier_version": { "type": "string" },
    "evaluation_receipt_sha256": { "type": "string", "pattern": "^[a-f0-9]{64}$" },
    "outcome_class": {
      "enum": ["CORRECT_DELIVERY", "CORRECT_WITHHOLD", "INCORRECT_DELIVERY", "FALSE_ABSTENTION", "UNSUPPORTED_SCOPE", "EXECUTION_ERROR"]
    },
    "failure_taxonomy": {
      "enum": [
        "NONE", "UNSUPPORTED_HOST_GRAMMAR", "WRONG_SOURCE_SELECTION",
        "AMBIGUOUS_EVIDENCE", "SOURCE_CONTRADICTION", "MISSED_PROCEDURE",
        "BAD_MODEL_ACTION", "TIMEOUT_OR_BUDGET", "SECURITY_DENIAL", "EVALUATOR_MISMATCH"
      ]
    },
    "data_rights_class": {
      "enum": ["OWNER_APPROVED_PUBLIC", "PROJECT_LICENSED", "SYNTHETIC_VERIFIED", "OBSERVED_TOOL_RESULT"]
    },
    "consent_grant_id": { "type": ["string", "null"] },
    "eligible_for_training": { "type": "boolean" },
    "recorded_at_utc": { "type": "string", "format": "date-time" }
  }
}

```

**Family-Level Split & Decontamination Rules:**

1. **Composite Group Key:** Assign `group_key = sha256(project_family_id || template_family_id || normalized_repo_root)`. All rows sharing a `group_key` MUST reside in the same partition (`train`, `val`, `public_eval`, or `protected_eval`).

2. **Three-Stage Decontamination Firewall:** (a) Exact hash gate on `task_request_sha256` and `source_snapshots[*].sha256`; (b) normalized 13-gram shingle overlap check (`LEAK_13GRAM`, excluding an explicit standard-keyword allowlist); (c) MinHash LSH (128 permutations, 5-gram shingles) with Jaccard `>= 0.15` quarantined as `LEAK_NEAR_DUP`.

3. **Minimum Diversity Gate:** Reject supervised qualification datasets (`INSUFFICIENT_DIVERSITY`) with fewer than 3 distinct `project_family_id`s in `train`, fewer than 2 held-out `project_family_id`s in `eval`, or 0 verified positive outcomes. Always report `num_unique_project_families`, `num_distinct_tasks`, and `num_action_observations` separately.

## 162. Four-arm causal evaluation and statistical promotion gates (`NEXT-022`–`NEXT-025`)

Every native model evaluation MUST run four matched arms under identical frozen task sets, source revisions, and tool/wall budgets:

* **Arm 0 (`DETERMINISTIC_0B`):** Trusted host + exact/FTS/TF-IDF rules (zero neural inference).

* **Arm 1 (`SIMPLE_LEARNED`):** Strong linear/logistic or character n-gram baseline on the exact same split.

* **Arm 2 (`INCUMBENT_NATIVE`):** Last independently accepted ATLAS-original checkpoint (or Arm 0 if none).

* **Arm 3 (`CANDIDATE_NATIVE`):** New from-scratch candidate evaluated in both `model_proposal_only` (no host fallback rescue) and `full_system_verified`.

**Promotion Criteria:**

* **Safety Gate:** `incorrect_trusted_delivery_rate == 0` on protected safety/provenance checks and no regression vs. incumbent on general held-out tasks.

* **Causal Attribution:** Report `causal_path_change_rate` (fraction of tasks where Arm 3 chose a different valid action than Arm 0) and paired `causal_net_gain = wins - losses`.

* **Qualification Threshold:** Advancing from `SHADOW` to `QUALIFIED_FOR_SPECIFIC_TASK` requires either (a) strictly higher verified held-out completion than Arms 0, 1, and 2 with zero increase in wrong deliveries, or (b) verified completion parity with a preregistered `>= 25%` reduction in end-to-end task cost. Ties without cost savings remain `SHADOW` or `REJECTED`.

## 163. Procedure state machine, three-Master arbitration and self-improvement rollback (`NEXT-023`–`NEXT-025`)

**Multi-Step Procedure State Machine (`V-02`):**

```
[DRAFT] -> [VALIDATED] -> [AUTHORIZED] -> [RUNNING]
   |--(missing/stale source)--> [WAITING_EVIDENCE] -> [RUNNING]
   |--(budget hit / unsupported)--> [ABSTAINED]
   |--(all steps + oracle pass)--> [PENDING_REVIEW]
         |--(verifier pass)--> [COMPLETED]
         |--(verifier fail, retries < max)--> [RUNNING]
         |--(verifier fail, retries == max)--> [FAILED]
   |--(security violation / tamper)--> [QUARANTINED]
   |--(owner cancel)--> [CANCELED]

```

Default `NEXT-023` bounds: `max_steps <= 8`, `max_wall_seconds <= 60`, `max_retries <= 2`, `effectful_actions_allowed = false`. Preconditions (project scope + source SHA-256) are re-verified before every `READ` and `ASSERT_EVIDENCE`.

**Three-Master Coordination & Arbitration (`NEXT-024`):**

* Inter-Master handoffs use host-validated `MasterMessageV1` (`graph_id`, `from_role`, `to_role`, `checkpoint_sha256`, `evidence_ids[]`, `proposal_payload`, `uncertainty_score`, `remaining_budget`).

* Disagreement between Research and Engineering is never resolved by majority vote; the host dispatches one bounded `REQUEST_REVIEW` against raw source spans/oracles and fails closed to `ABSTAINED` if unresolved within budget.

* When VRAM cannot hold multiple checkpoints concurrently, the host falls back to deterministic topological serial execution.

**Bounded Self-Improvement, Stopping & Rollback (`NEXT-025`):**

* Halt automatically if 2 consecutive refits yield `NO_VERIFIED_IMPROVEMENT`, seed variance exceeds preregistered bounds, eligible new project families = 0, or any evaluator custody anomaly occurs.

* Incumbent checkpoints and manifests remain immutable; rollback is an atomic pointer flip in `models/registry/active_pointers.json` verified by SHA-256.

## 164. Four-layer retrieval, five-zone isolation, Win32 canonicalization and `CapabilityGrantV1`

**Four-Layer Retrieval Token Budgets:** L1 Navigation (`<= 512` tokens), L2 Section Outline (`<= 1,024` tokens), L3 Verbatim Evidence Excerpts (`<= 4,096` tokens adaptive, with `source_sha256` + UTF-16 offsets), L4 Full Original (host/parser only). Overflow MUST emit `truncation_occurred: true` with omitted `source_id`s. Only L3/L4 verbatim spans can satisfy `ASSERT_EVIDENCE`.

**Five Security Zones:** Zone A (Public Technical), Zone B (Private Playground — ephemeral RAM/session, zero ATLAS disk persistence, never trainable), Zone C (Project Workspace — per-`project_id` ACL, not trainable by default), Zone D (Personal ATLAS `V-05` — opt-in SQLCipher AES-256-GCM, never trainable without separate per-category consent), Zone E (Protected Evaluator `V-09` — outside Git and candidate read paths).

**Win32 Host Canonicalization (`SEC-L06`):** Resolve every file handle via `GetFinalPathNameByHandleW` with reparse points blocked by default; reject NTFS ADS, 8.3 short aliases, trailing dots/spaces, drive-relative paths, UNC/device names, out-of-scope symlinks/junctions, and zip bombs (`ratio > 100:1`). Revalidate file SHA-256 immediately before action (TOCTOU guard).

```
{
  "schema_version": "atlas.capability_grant.v1",
  "grant_id": "grant-20261119-001",
  "issued_by": "atlas_trusted_host",
  "owner_id": "owner-local",
  "project_id": "proj-012",
  "zone": "ZONE_C_PROJECT",
  "allowed_opcodes": ["LOOKUP", "READ", "COMPARE", "ASSERT_EVIDENCE", "STOP"],
  "allowed_roots_canonical": ["C:\ATLAS\Workspaces\proj-012"],
  "network_egress": "DENY_ALL",
  "max_tool_calls": 16,
  "max_wall_seconds": 90,
  "expires_at_utc": "2026-11-19T12:00:00Z",
  "revocable_immediately": true,
  "host_hmac_sha256": "<host-only-keyed-digest>"
}

```

## 165. Windows 11 workstation resource arbitration and honest degradation

At startup, autodetect GPU/VRAM (DXGI/NVML) and system RAM rather than hardcoding 16 GB / 96 GB. Compute `VRAM_usable = VRAM_total - VRAM_DWM_reserve (>= 1.2 GB) - VRAM_other_apps - VRAM_frag_margin (>= 512 MB)`. Pre-launch preflight checks `weights + KV_cache + activations + optimizer_states <= VRAM_usable`.

* **Preemption & Thermal Stop:** Foreground interactive tasks preempt idle/background training at the next safe step; training pauses if GPU temp `>= 82°C` or foreground VRAM demand exceeds 25%.

* **Safe Pause/Resume:** Atomically persist `{model_weights, optimizer_state, lr_scheduler, rng_states, step_index, dataset_cursor}` with SHA-256 verification; if optimizer state is unavailable on resume, report `WEIGHTS_ONLY_RESTART`.

* **Degradation Ladder:** (1) Serialize concurrent models -> (2) Spill cold models to system RAM -> (3) Select smaller qualified checkpoint or CPU fallback with real ETA -> (4) Fail closed with `RESOURCE_BUDGET_EXCEEDED` and exact MB deficit.

## 166. Repository layout, CI non-drift gates and context-budget boot protocol

* **Unified Archive Path:** Retire conflicting `docs/ai/history/` references; preserve all prior canonical versions and the unabridged archive under `docs/ai/archive/master-plan/`, and the 18 original files/transcripts under `docs/ai/archive/source_originals/` and `source_transcripts/`.

* **Single Version Authority:** `docs/ai/ATLAS_MASTER_PLAN_VERSION.json` replaces stale `ATLAS_MASTER_SOURCE_AUDIT.json` as the CI-checked SHA-256 authority.

* **Context-Budget Boot:** External agents (Sol/Argon) load `AGENTS.md` (Pinned Doctrine Card, `<= 700` tokens), verify `ATLAS_MASTER_PLAN_VERSION.json`, read `ATLAS_HANDOFF.md`, and retrieve only task-relevant Master Plan chapters via indexed lookup—recording `canonical_master_sha256` and `sections_consulted[]` in every handoff receipt.

* **Manual Relay Rule:** Long technical instructions live in `docs/ai/ATLAS_HANDOFF.md`; the owner manually relays a short 2–4 line copy-ready trigger between Sol and Argon. Cross-agent Computer Use, screen control, and polling remain strictly prohibited.

# PART XXIII — DELIVERY-READY PRODUCT, RESEARCH, AND OPERATIONS CONTRACTS (v3.3 EXPANSION)

> **Status and use:** Chapters 167–192 add operational detail to the existing owner constitution. They are **target behavior and acceptance requirements**, not evidence that the present executable already provides these capabilities. They must be interpreted together with the earlier security restrictions, original-model-first doctrine, current independent-review receipts, and owner permissions. These chapters do not change the historical NEXT-014 accepted baseline or the pending NEXT-015R–020 review snapshot; agents must inspect current Git and independent verdicts before using any milestone number. A capability that lacks compatible data, verified tools, or an approved isolation boundary must remain visibly blocked instead of being simulated.

## 167. A complete owner experience: from first launch to advanced autonomy

**First launch and recovery.** The owner installs a signed or integrity-verified local package, sees a readable description of data storage and network defaults, chooses whether to create a new profile or recover a previous one, and can inspect a storage destination before any migration. Default startup must not scan the entire disk, connect to the internet, download a model, train on personal files, register an account, enable automation, or execute imported code. An offline deterministic demonstration must work without the optional model service. Every install should be reversible and leave prior user data intact unless a separately confirmed deletion is requested.

**The normal Home screen.** Six understandable paths—Ask ATLAS, Work on a Project, Research, Create, Improve a Model, and Evidence/History—cover the core experiences. They are navigational conveniences, not six competing backends. The same project scope and permission receipt follow the user across workspaces. Actions whose backend is not verified are disabled with the specific blocker rather than presented as nonfunctional buttons. The selected original model, reference model, or deterministic mode is always visible. A new owner should be able to tell whether ATLAS *knows*, *is guessing*, *is testing*, or *is blocked* without opening developer logs.

**Progressive disclosure and accessibility.** Beginner mode exposes one request box, concise evidence and a clear approval. Command Center adds projects, status, costs and model provenance. Advanced Studio exposes task DAGs, dataset cards, experiment hypotheses, checkpoint diffs, protected-review outcome summaries and isolation status, but never the private evaluator answers. Views should share a coherent navigation and design language. Test light/dark/high-contrast modes, keyboard-only use, assistive technology, zoom/DPI changes, large font choices, fast project switching, focus return after modal closure, long histories, disconnected optional services and cancellation during streamed responses.

**Owner journey examples requiring end-to-end evidence:** (a) Start offline, select existing evidence, ask a supported question, receive an exact citation; (b) import a known C# project, locate a method, verify its signature, ask a contextual follow-up; (c) ask the native model to plan a bounded local research task, observe each read-only step, obtain a verified conclusion; (d) explicitly activate a local pretrained reference, ask a question, observe model lineage, then return to native-only with the reference process disabled; (e) start an owner-authorized native training experiment, observe real measured metrics, cancel safely, reload checkpoint state and inspect a rejected candidate; (f) archive a project, restore it on a clean profile, and verify all supported claims are still tied to their source revisions.

**Acceptance:** Independent user-journey scripts must name the real executable and exact selected project, show input and output evidence, exercise error paths, and distinguish mock UI from backend behavior. Owner effort should be measured as clicks, manual selections, repeated text and approvals required to finish a useful task. Accessibility failures that block critical permission/recovery controls are release blockers. A frontend that only renders a status generated by a test fixture is not a working feature.

## 168. Canonical capability graph and prerequisites: prevent feature inflation

Every user-visible function must have a canonical `capability_id`, owner purpose, current lifecycle, implementation artifact, runtime entry point, required inputs, grants, evidence type, dependencies, success oracles, independent reviewer, fallback and known unsupported cases. A separate **dependency graph** shows when a feature is blocked by a missing primitive. For example, autonomous repair cannot qualify before source-scoped editing, isolated execution, evaluator separation, owner change approval and recovery are demonstrated; voice interaction cannot qualify merely because an audio menu exists; research cannot be called verified merely because an HTTP response was received.

A canonical registry entry must distinguish: `DESIGNED_ONLY`, `IMPLEMENTED_UNTESTED`, `AUTHOR_TESTED`, `INDEPENDENTLY_ACCEPTED`, `ACCEPTED_WITH_LIMITATIONS`, `REPAIRS_REQUIRED`, `BLOCKED`, `DISABLED_BY_POLICY`, `SHADOW`, `RETIRED`. Feature state is different from model state. Every status link should open the exact acceptance receipt and code revision. The owner report must show what is *newly usable*, what was already usable, and what changed only in test coverage or UI.

**No status laundering:** A failed independent review cannot be overwritten by the author's subsequent report without a new frozen revision and eligible independent verdict. A downstream milestone using a provisional predecessor remains provisional, even if its own public tests pass. Any role or tool catalog entry may remain `DESIGNED_ONLY` indefinitely. Catalog size is not a measure of capability. A provisional feature must not quietly become default on next boot.

**Required dependency examples:** Native-only conversational reasoning depends on a validated tokenizer and original model, language tests, trust-aware retrieval and citations; practical native coding depends on correct semantic project ingestion, verified source references, typed code proposal, independent validation and eventually guest execution; native learning from outcomes depends on independently measured labels, data eligibility, leakage controls and baseline comparisons; safe background autonomy depends on revocable host leases, resource arbitration, crash recovery and a separately qualified scheduler.

**Acceptance:** Build a machine-readable registry and auto-generated human capability matrix. Challenge the registry by deleting a required executable, corrupting a dependency receipt, disabling the optional reference service, revoking an owner grant, or switching to an unaccepted commit. The UI and API must downgrade claims and capabilities appropriately. Run compatibility tests across accepted baselines; do not infer implementation from a heading in the master plan.

## 169. Language understanding, explicit intent contracts, and safe ambiguity resolution

ATLAS's eventual conversational intelligence must distinguish four layers: (1) linguistic understanding of words and context; (2) typed task intent with constraints; (3) a proposed action plan; and (4) trusted host authorization and execution. A native model may learn layers 1–3 but never infer layer 4 by itself. When a user asks 'fix my project', ATLAS must identify which project, scope and tests are in use; a plausible filename or prior history is not permission to write. When the same question can be answered safely without an action, prefer a read-only answer before requesting more privileges.

**Language competence tests** must vary names, paraphrases, grammar, ordering, negation, quantification, conditional statements, code identifiers, numerical constraints, dates, conflicting instructions, uncertain referents and multi-turn ellipsis. Hold entire template families, project families and semantic compositions out of training. Challenge tasks with intentionally swapped semantics ('find the caller' versus 'find the callee'), simultaneous constraints, requests to quote versus execute text, and cross-file references. A model's high classification score on near-duplicates must not be called language reasoning. Compare native learned inference against symbolic patterns, TF-IDF, calibrated linear encoders, and no-neural templates. Evaluate task correctness, false abstention and trustworthy handling of uncertainty.

**Structured action proposal:** The interpreter emits a versioned `IntentPlanCandidate` with owner request digest, language and context, project reference, requested output, constrained tool proposal, necessary evidence, uncertainty and a request for additional information only if essential. The host rederives allowed tools from current permissions. The model must not write `authorized=true` and thereby obtain authority. Confidence must be calibrated against outcome families; a high raw softmax is not proof of truth. For unsupported intent, return a readable limitation, not a phantom workflow.

**Acceptance:** Native-only inference must complete a representative set of previously unseen paraphrases while using zero pretrained inference and zero external network calls. Record exact model checkpoints and decision traces. A pretrained fallback may be used only in separately opted-in reference mode and must be labeled. Report linguistic quality and end-to-end task utility separately. If the native model cannot express a grounded natural-language explanation, a deterministic explanation from evidence is acceptable but cannot count as learned generation.

## 170. Reproducible project intelligence beyond simple file lookup

A mature Project Inspector should build a **versioned project graph** containing source files, symbols, imports, modules, dependency edges, package versions, build targets, tests, assets, engine/editor versions, task history, owner goals, current Git or source-control revision, and uncertainty. The graph is a cache of observed project state, not authority over the original files. For every parsed symbol store language grammar version, exact source span, digest and project-specific compilation settings. Separate physical file identity from case-insensitive Windows aliases; do not accept unresolved symlinks, unusual alternate data stream spellings or network paths as safe worktree entries by default.

**Practical coding path:** `select project → inspect snapshot → build compact evidence context → choose procedure → produce a complete typed proposal → compile/type-check in approved qualified isolation → run applicable tests → separate review → present exact diff → owner authorize apply → audit and rollback`. If a compiler is unavailable, the proposal remains untested, and the UI must say so. There is no right to patch trusted repository files simply because a local model generated a diff. Proprietary, private and licensed source require separate read/training rights.

**Engine-specialized project plans:** Unreal C++/Blueprint: module/plugin boundaries, UPROPERTY/UFUNCTION reflection, asset references, packaging versions, replication, ability systems, level and Blueprint interactions; UEFN Verse: supported language/device APIs and version-specific event wiring; Unity: assembly definitions, packages, .meta GUIDs, scene/prefab references, serialization, platform defines; Blender: Python `bpy` version, object-data ownership, meshes/modifiers, UV/material/rig/animation/export; Web: TypeScript, frontend/build contracts, accessibility and route/state behavior; Rust/C++: lifetimes/ownership or ABI/linker/compiler flags. A specialized model must obtain project-specific documentation and constraints rather than hallucinate API support.

**Reusable procedures:** Save verified successful fixes with a precondition predicate, exact engine/tool version, source/test lineage, hazards and confidence. A 'common fix' scraped from a forum is a candidate until tested on a compatible task. Rejected patches and regressions are learning evidence but must not be promoted as working examples. The project assistant must output full paste-ready code with target path and insertion points when such code was requested, and must explicitly identify remaining unknown dependencies.

**Acceptance:** Independent test suites include renamed projects, changed package versions, generated code, duplicate symbols, partial compiler contexts, unsupported dynamic dispatch, deliberate malicious project instructions and mixed C#/Python references. Measure correct edit-location selection, compilability when actually compiled, passed tests, unintended changes, owner approvals and rollback correctness. Never use an executable guest merely because the tool returned a success string; qualify the actual guest boundary first.

## 171. Research autonomy, claim-level trust, and reproducible literature work

A mature Research Master manages a scoped **research question ledger** rather than generating a narrative from the first retrieved document. Each question is decomposed into subclaims, evidence requirements, relevant domains, freshness constraints, source credibility checks, counterexamples and a stopping budget. Prefer qualified local knowledge and owner-authorized files, then explicitly approved isolated web research when the necessary sandbox and grant exist. A read-only task may run multiple source comparisons autonomously only within its owner-defined source scope, time and compute budget.

**Reliability is claim-dependent:** An official manual is strong evidence for its own software API revision but weak evidence for an independent benchmark claim; a scientific abstract is insufficient for a detailed numerical assertion that requires the table/method; a current public website can become stale; a social discussion may identify a question worth investigating but cannot become a verified fact by majority vote alone. Save immutable source snapshots, dates, licenses, exact excerpts and counterevidence. For scientific tasks, capture experimental methods, sample size, uncertainty, units and model limitations. For legal rules, retain jurisdiction and effective dates. For prices, rates and current events, retain observation time and appropriate refresh policy.

**Research procedure:** `state objective → inspect known verified claims → identify gaps → select sources → obtain snapshots under permission → extract exact claims → check conflicts and versions → independent challenge → qualify scoped findings → produce synthesis with limitations → preserve permitted reusable knowledge`. Machine-generated prose is an output artifact, not a source citation. A factual conclusion must be reconstructable from original quoted passages or reproducible computation. Refuse to merge data from incompatible time ranges or units silently.

**Acceptance:** Use blinded collections with deliberately contradictory dates, out-of-scope private materials, plausible fake citations, unavailable sources, deceptive embedded agent instructions and expired claims. A successful Research Master must not merely return a shorter question or attractive essay; it must improve verified answer completeness/accuracy, reduce relevant source cost or correctly identify unverifiable hypotheses. For eventual web retrieval, test redirects, DNS rebinding, rate/size limits, blocked file downloads and absence of owner credentials. Internet research must not be activated during NEXT-021–025 without separate qualification and consent.

## 172. Model and dataset provenance through the entire learning chain

Each trained model must have a reconstructable lineage: raw authorized sources → exact task receipts → eligibility decisions → verified labels → filtered examples → split partitions → tokenizer/vocabulary → architecture and initialization → training environment → checkpoints → evaluation suite → independent verdict → runtime role. Use content digests and immutable manifests for every material dependency. Record potential influence from optional pretrained teachers separately from the origin of weights; a from-scratch initialized model trained on verified teacher-proposed examples can be called original-weight with teacher-assisted data only if that contribution is disclosed. It must not be characterized as fully independent-data training.

**Data governance:** Every training record has source origin, rights/license, owner training permission, sensitivity class, allowed use, revocation ID, creation date, normalization recipe, de-duplication group, project-family ID, semantic cluster, validator identity, outcome receipt and partition ID. Source access for inference is not a data-training grant. Source usage must be revocable where possible. Derived datasets, cached embeddings, generated intermediate paraphrases and descendant checkpoints must be tracked so revocation can quarantine future use and support a truthful retraining/deletion policy. 'Forget the record' must not be represented as assured removal from already-distributed model weights without suitable retraining or verified unlearning.

**Poisoning and leakage:** Source comments, third-party package READMEs, web pages and teacher outputs must not modify eligibility policies or private evaluator labels. Split entire project/template/semantic families, not only rows; check near-duplicate and textual overlap; reserve evaluator-controlled cases outside the development checkout and training process. If leakage is detected after a benchmark, revoke the score, preserve the incident and repeat with a fresh protected suite. Dataset count must present independent tasks, families, measured actions and negative attempts separately.

**Acceptance:** Independently modify or remove a training permission receipt, swap one verified outcome for another, alter a label while maintaining a superficial report digest, insert a protected example, duplicate a whole family under new filenames, and revoke a dataset. The Foundry must reject or quarantine all inappropriate rows and identify affected descendants. Qualification should fail closed, without producing a fabricated training epoch or success claim.

## 173. Model architecture and scale as a controlled scientific program

The original-model program should include at least four **research controls**: deterministic tool/lookup, strong sparse retrieval or linear learner, the current accepted original model, and the proposed original model. Add a reference pretrained model only in separately authorized comparison experiments, not as a hidden training/inference dependency. Models can be tiny policies, encoders, sequence classifiers, GRUs, small Transformers, retrieval-conditioned generation networks or modular policy/value pairs. Architecture names alone imply no capability. Small efficient models may outperform a 1B model in structured decisions.

**Scaling ladder:** Fit the data and task before choosing parameters. Demonstrate tiny-source task advantage; develop a from-scratch tokenizer and benchmark unfamiliar language understanding; validate a small text model on controlled instructions and grounded output; progress to millions/tens of millions only when data quality, compute estimates and held-out improvement justify it; investigate 100M–300M and eventually ~1B where justified by distinct useful capabilities. If a 20M model learns only to return a structured intent with no downstream benefit, record that result and stop or redesign the task. The user's workstation is a single RTX 5080 with 16 GB VRAM—not a fleet capable of training unrestricted large language models from scratch.

**Experiment design:** Freeze hypotheses, data splits and metrics before candidate tuning. Record architecture/optimizer/seed/tokenizer/context/learning-rate/precision/batch-size/early-stop, peak VRAM and realistic duration. At least one control should test whether an apparently neural advantage comes from easier source access, a changed verifier or a better retrieval index. Compare matched tool budgets, test multiple seeds when feasible and report family-level variance. For unbalanced abstention problems, use selective accuracy, false-abstention and incorrect-trusted-delivery measures rather than raw accuracy alone.

**Acceptance:** A candidate advances only if it establishes a prespecified end-to-end utility advantage or a meaningful resource saving at safety parity, without violating protected-data or security gates. Lower training loss is insufficient. Never convert speculative '1B equals 80B' or '12B equals 3T' language from historical material into an engineering promise. Show measured model size, checkpoint bytes, latency, memory and task family scope.

## 174. Original Primary, Research and Engineering roles: competence rather than labels

All three Master competencies must have independently testable objectives. **Primary** understands owner scope, decomposes permitted goals, chooses specialist tasks and budgets, handles no-evidence and stopping decisions. **Research** selects sources, resolves evidence gaps and contradictions, requests validation and manages knowledge promotion proposals. **Engineering** understands code changes, chooses inspection/tests, produces bounded patch strategies and requires independent review. They can initially share a trained original backbone with separate verified heads or operate as distinct original models; selection must be based on performance, not a decorative desire to count three checkpoints.

**Cooperation protocol:** A Master emits typed plans and evidence IDs. The host validates each requested action. Specialists return observations with provenance and uncertainty. A different review objective challenges the result. The Primary merges only qualifying claims and explicitly reports disagreement. For a cross-domain task, the Research Master may supply a versioned API fact, the Engineering Master a source change proposal, and the Primary a sequenced authorization request. All intermediate roles have max budget, max retries, and cancellation semantics. An agent must not recursively summon unlimited agents or charge only the last synthesizer for resource use.

**Disagreement handling:** Record each model's checkpoint and decision, what evidence each used, why they differ and which independent verifier is authoritative for the current question. Voting is not automatic truth; a highly confident wrong agent should not outvote a deterministic compiler or exact source digest. If disagreement cannot be resolved, produce `needs_more_evidence` or `needs_review`, not a fabricated consensus. Avoid training both 'independent' reviewer and producer on the same hidden evaluation answers.

**Acceptance:** Ablate each Master and each important specialist on the same unfamiliar tasks. Report full-system utility, solo model, pairwise cooperation, deterministic scheduler and always-call-all baseline, as well as cost. A three-role system that ties a single role while spending more time is an architectural experiment, not a completed intelligence advantage. Do not allow newly trained roles to authorize code execution or online access.

## 175. Trustworthy tool execution, verifier custody and permission gates

Model output should always be **proposed data**, including XML/JSON tool requests. The trusted host independently derives the approved capability from an owner-signed or locally secured grant with project, operation, source/destination, content hash, time, budget, user and single-use/revocation scope. A source file with the text 'ignore previous rules' cannot change host policy. A model may propose a dangerous action and receive a structured denial; the denial is valuable evidence for safety training but must not automatically be relabeled as 'task success'.

**Custody separation:** Implementation-author tests, public benchmark controls and independent private evaluation are three separate evidence tiers. Production model promotion also requires a distinct host/owner gate when risk warrants it. Candidate systems cannot change protected tests, modify scoring code, rewrite an evaluator expectation, access hidden labels or promote their own weights. Build evaluation in an isolated owner-controlled checkout with immutable fingerprints. Independent reviewer findings must be reproducible against frozen source and exact toolchains; absence of a tool is a skip, not a pass.

**Action ledger:** Every authorized effect includes an unambiguous operation, exact target/source identity, requested-by, approved-by, applicable policy, preconditions, dependency hashes, rollback or compensation plan, execution status and observed postconditions. The host rejects replay or swapped receipts even when an isolated file digest matches. Reading a source does not imply permission to train on it, export it, execute it or add it to a public knowledge vault.

**Acceptance:** Attempt to supply a forged grant, replay an expired grant, substitute a different project, change a file after approval, inject a tool schema in a README, provide malformed JSON, replace a path with Windows alias syntax or use a model-generated 'approved' flag. Each must be rejected before touching the protected resource. Report failed security tests with full owner-safe details; never mask them as correct abstentions or acceptable limitations for destructive operations.

## 176. Windows host, remote guest and boundary proof for future automation

The physical Windows host is always the highest-risk asset because it contains the owner's personal data and other projects. Future untrusted code execution must run in a proven isolated environment, not in the current WPF process, developer shell or a silently substituted host subprocess. Guest qualification must test real OS boundary behavior including mapped drives, home/profile mounts, clipboard, key stores, network adapters, device/GPU passthrough, filesystem reparse points, common Win32 path aliases and host process injection. A nonexistent Hyper-V VM is not isolation; the runtime must prove the intended guest is active before processing untrusted artifacts.

**Import/export boundary:** Owner input reaches quarantine; scanners/validators enforce size, content type, archive traversal limits, file count, compressed/decompressed ratio, symlink/hardlink handling and extension deception; a trusted copier moves specific allowlisted artifacts across the boundary by content digest. Guest output returns only an explicit tested result bundle, never arbitrary executable bytes silently mounted into the host. Approved source edits are applied by trusted host code to an exact pre-reviewed base revision with atomic rollback. Network default denies remote guest requests unless a separate domain/data/exfiltration grant exists.

**Remote research/account isolation:** If future ATLAS is permitted to register its own online account, only an owner-approved isolated remote environment may perform registration. Never use the owner's email, identity, payment method, subscriptions, personal accounts, browser cookies, saved passwords, home network identity or installed user browser profile. There can be no guarantee of complete anonymity, safe downloads or total prevention of IP correlation; state the actual provider and boundary evidence. ATLAS-created sessions must never be opened on the owner's physical computer.

**Acceptance:** Independently red-team guest escapes, filesystem canonicalization, DNS rebinding, redirects into private networks, credential theft, CPU/GPU exhaustion and malicious repository postinstall hooks. Record the guest image, network rules and exact tested build. The system must refuse to execute if required isolation cannot be attested. Do not merge boundary design with model autonomy: safe execution remains a separately qualified future milestone.

## 177. Data lifecycle, personal assistance and honest memory controls

ATLAS should support three clear modes: **Public/project** for authorized technical work, **Personal** with explicit encrypted category-scoped memory, and **Private Playground** with no ATLAS-side persistent conversation or training. Optional pretrained reference chat is an orthogonal opt-in that must still respect selected data scope and network guarantees. The current Next app's plaintext project/source records are not a substitute for encrypted personal memory. The owner must be shown which data will be stored, used for training or transmitted before consenting.

**User-data controls:** View every retained memory and its original provenance; correct with version history; set retention and scope; export in readable and machine formats; delete or revoke; inspect derivative caches and training descendants; see backup retention and actual deletion limitations. Consent to answering from a personal document is not consent to train. Personal memories must never be mixed into publicly shared benchmarks, external developer handoffs or generated examples without explicit separate authorization.

**Human preference integrity:** Compare Two Answers must preserve model names, exact outputs, prompt settings, random seeds when available and latency. Only a real owner interaction may create `human_preference`; trained judges and model-made choices must be labeled `ai_judge` or `predicted_preference`. Even when no preference is saved for training, the tool may support ephemeral display. Excluding an example from training must be honored by the Foundry and downstream dataset lineage.

**Acceptance:** Attempt private-to-public retrieval, cross-project memory leakage, encrypted-backup restoration under the wrong user, revoked training consent and deletion while a queued training job is running. No private record may appear in a general model-training set by implication. For a model already trained on revocable content, describe descendant handling accurately; do not promise impossible deletion of influence without a verified method.

## 178. User-directed creation, creative continuity and multimodal boundaries

ATLAS must eventually create useful deliverables—not merely propose ideas. For coding, complete source patches with pinned versions, file locations, integration steps and tests; for books, a story bible, character sheet, continuity timeline, edited chapter artifacts and rights/provenance; for 2D sprites, consistent tile sizes, frame counts, palette, transparent backgrounds and animation continuity; for 3D, validated meshes/materials/UVs/rigs/exported scenes; for video/audio, real project files, sync/timing and licenses; for documents, actual editable deliverables; and for research, a source-backed report with limitations. Every output is versioned, recoverable and owner-editable.

**Project workflow:** Gather owner target, audience, style, technical constraints, reference/licensing rights and desired editable format; build versioned project memory; choose an appropriate qualified specialist; create a first artifact; run format/tool validators; compare against functional examples and owner feedback; retain stable project IDs and asset dependencies. A future PLATZ Engine connection should exchange canonical assets through a versioned interface, not share control or source trees. PLATZ Engine's authoritative architecture remains in `PLATZ_MASTER.md` and `PLATZ_WORKING.md`.

**Creative evaluation:** Humans may legitimately judge aesthetic quality, but the system still tracks measurable constraints: build/import success, sprite frame alignment, mesh manifoldness where relevant, text continuity, factual assertions and resource budgets. Never present stock or pretrained model output as a new original ATLAS-trained image model without provenance. If a modality lacks a working model or tool, show that limit and offer a currently supported workflow rather than fabricated media generation.

**Acceptance:** Test one end-to-end artifact per declared supported modality and editor version, including reopen/edit/export and failure handling. Track original assets, user-supplied sources, licensed references, training rights and derived work. Do not present broad 'supports Unreal/Unity/Blender' claims based only on file extension recognition.

## 179. Hardware scheduling, training observability and experimental cost accounting

A 16 GB RTX 5080 workstation with 96 GB system RAM and 4 TB NVMe needs one shared resource arbiter that knows active VRAM allocations, display/Windows graphics usage, model weights, optimizer memory, activations, KV caches, CPU offload, reserved disk, working-set pressure and concurrent task priorities. Every model's declared parameter count should come from actual checkpoint architecture and tensor shapes, not its tag name. 'Can load' is different from 'can train'. Avoid hardcoding GPU capacity or an absolute millisecond SLA into general model policies.

**Execution profiles:** Safe deterministic; native efficient interactive; native high-quality interactive; optional explicit reference comparison; dedicated owner-started training; bounded idle training when separately approved; and a mixed profile with guaranteed foreground priority. Pause semantics must be specific: pause at checkpoint, stop after a step, cancel immediately where safely possible, or revoke future jobs. Real optimizer-state resume must restore RNG, data-loader cursor, scheduler and optimizer state when claimed, not simply reload parameters. Crash recovery should quarantine incomplete checkpoints and preserve the last qualified model.

**Performance evaluation:** Record cold and warm load, token or action throughput, p50/p95/p99 latency where sample sizes support them, first-byte/UI responsiveness, peak RAM/VRAM, disk reads/writes, model/cache churn, CPU utilization, error and cancellation rates, tests per trained candidate and useful completions per unit compute. Energy/thermal readings may be unavailable; use honest measured proxies rather than fictional numbers. Some original models may be faster on CPU than transferring them to GPU.

**Acceptance:** Replay concurrent foreground request + training, model-switch churn, no-GPU fallback, low-disk/low-memory, interrupted checkpoint, timeout and host sleep/restart. Resource overrun must fail safely and not spill training examples or passwords into crash logs. User-facing metrics are based on actual backend counters, never inferred solely from planned actions.

## 180. Release management, provenance, backup and recovery

ATLAS Next needs a reproducible product release path independent of the external development chat. Each release carries a source commit, dependency lock/manifest, binaries with digests, accepted capability set, model registry fingerprint, documentation version, migration plan, environment/toolchain record, threat review and rollback instructions. Windows PowerShell 5.1 and PowerShell 7 packaging paths are both acceptance surfaces while supported; test the actual WPF startup, import, model evidence and shutdown in packaged form. A successful source compile does not prove the ZIP launches.

**State migrations:** Data schemas evolve only through tested forward/backward or reversible migration plans. Always test an existing profile as well as a clean one. Back up the exact source state and verify restore to a fresh folder, including all required model/checkpoint and evidence manifests. For encrypted personal memory, restore must require the proper key; backup archives themselves cannot be plain copies of secrets by convenience. An interrupted installation should leave either the previous working installation or a recoverable new state, not half-migrated data.

**Update trust:** No future ATLAS self-update may replace trusted host policy, evaluator custody or owner security configuration merely because a native model recommended it. Separate release artifacts from downloaded models and untrusted project files. Signed updates and measured supply-chain checks are desirable but require an actual key-management process; do not fake signatures with a SHA-256 digest alone. Track provenance for dependencies and licensing, especially bundled external model components.

**Acceptance:** Independently perform clean install, upgrade, migration fault injection, backup restore, rollback and offline launch. Verify retained project source digests and chat/task history, without silently importing private vaults. Report exact residual risks and skipped platforms. A release version without a matching frozen reviewed source is not accepted.

## 181. Scientific experiment management and the honesty of progress

Every study begins with a question that can fail. For example: 'Does the original Master reduce incorrect source selections on unseen projects?' is better than 'Train a superior Master'. Define its causal outcome, minimal effect worth detecting, control arms, protected task family, budget, expected tradeoffs and stopping rule. Use paired comparisons on identical held-out tasks, record disagreement cases, and compute uncertainty intervals when the sample warrants them. Small-sample trends should be labeled preliminary; one 20-case tie is not parity across all projects.

**Improvement taxonomy:** New code capability, improved retrieval data, verified procedure reuse, deterministic optimization, new model weights, improved native learned decision, and full autonomous end-to-end improvement are distinct categories. A native-model candidate may have lower loss but worse task utility. A change may save energy at the same accuracy but not improve reasoning. A retrained model may fail while a newly compiled deterministic procedure succeeds; preserve both facts. An improvement claim must identify which layer changed and which controlled comparison supports it.

**Avoid Goodhart's law:** Store benchmark definitions before training and rotate protected case families under independent custody; avoid repeatedly optimizing against public expected outputs. Count unique task families, not action rows, and do not present '100% pass' without scope and denominator. Randomly sample negative examples and perform targeted hard cases rather than only maximizing a narrow preselected score. Protect independent reviewer data and make challenge cases non-author-readable during candidate design.

**Acceptance:** Model candidates cannot change success definitions or judge themselves. Records must distinguish observed outcomes, predicted preference, owner preference and derived oracle judgments. Promotion depends on a prespecified quality/safety criterion and eligibility; if a candidate fails, commit a negative experiment and return to diagnose data/model/tool causes. A rejected model is a legitimate research result, not a reason to falsify a training gain.

## 182. ATLAS-original procedural memory and inference-time competence

A reusable procedure is a versioned **testable program-like recipe**, not an unbounded prompt that says 'think harder'. It contains goal, applicability predicate, required evidence, typed actions, stopping bounds, expected observations, independent validator, source/checkpoint versions, reliability estimates and fail-safe alternatives. Keep public candidate procedures separate from verified ones; procedure promotion is scoped to tested task families. A model may learn a selector for procedures, but the host validates preconditions and action boundaries. Procedures whose utility degrades after engine/API updates become stale and trigger re-evaluation.

**Procedure-transfer test:** Collect a verified debugging or research procedure from family A, then apply it to unfamiliar family B with analogous structure but different names, data formats and failures. Compare: no procedure; nearest lexical recipe; deterministic fixed recipe; original selector plus candidate procedures; original selector plus independently verified procedures. Require improvements in task completion or justified efficiency, not merely more text. Track when using an irrelevant recipe hurts and how models recognize that mismatch.

**Knowledge-to-tool compilation:** A recurring verified calculation or routine may become a parameterized answer card or sandboxed deterministic tool with tests, versioned source and independent review. This can lower inference cost, but must be attributed as a knowledge/tool improvement rather than native neural progress. For high-risk tools, offline compilation must not grant execution authority. Each compiled card records accepted input ranges, units, error behavior and invalidation conditions.

**Acceptance:** Stale, incompatible, contradictory, malicious and missing procedure cases must be caught. The system should be able to produce 'I found no verified procedure for this version' rather than guessing. Measure reuse across project families and fraction of successful future tasks attributable to previously verified procedures.

## 183. Learned multi-step planning, bounded graphs and correction without loops

A bounded planner produces a directed acyclic task graph with typed nodes, data dependencies, resource budgets and a stop condition. Cycles in the conceptual learning process are represented as new bounded graph revisions, never an uncontrolled recursive loop. Each node has a state transition receipt; independent nodes may run concurrently when it improves outcome or latency, otherwise serial execution is acceptable. A failed node should not automatically invalidate unrelated completed evidence. Persistent graph state must survive process crashes without re-executing a previously approved effect.

**Planning challenge suite:** New tasks require 2–8 or more distinct justified actions, selective retrieval, contradiction handling, intermediate error recovery, tool-result changes, uncertain evidence and premature-stop traps. Evaluate whether the original policy chooses the right next action given unfamiliar state, not whether it can reproduce a demonstration sequence by memorization. Compare with deterministic workflow, breadth/depth search under equal budgets, prior original planner, and optional reference models only as separate control. Include cost of all scouts and synthesis; do not reward gratuitous agent spawning.

**Recovery:** A failed read-only search may be retried with corrected query within budget; failed protected effect must not be retried without refreshed authorization and exact state. A reviewer verdict can trigger a bounded corrective task whose owner permissions and code provenance are separately validated. Deadlines, cancellation, sleep/restart, credential expiration and lost external service must yield a stable `blocked` or `needs_review` state rather than spinning.

**Acceptance:** Demonstrate a task where a learned plan alters the sequence and produces a better verified outcome on unfamiliar data. If plans match deterministic results but consume more resources, report a negative cooperation result. No planning model receives authority to rewrite trusted host policy.

## 184. Operational learning and intervention: deciding what to improve

The future Training Director's first competence should be diagnosis: Is the task failing because of **insufficient data**, a **wrong native decision**, a **missing source**, an **unsupported parser or tool**, **bad permissions**, a **broken evaluator**, **excessive latency**, or **misleading retrieved knowledge**? A weak original model should not be retrained to compensate for a deterministic verifier that cannot understand the task, as observed when all arms failed unsupported source cases in earlier public benchmarks.

**Opportunity ranking:** Estimate expected task value, repeated failure frequency, data eligibility, cost to acquire labels, model-training feasibility, risk, test-suite power and rollback cost. Owner-visible proposals should say why a candidate experiment matters and what alternatives (tool repair, knowledge update, procedure, model fit) were considered. A model may propose a curriculum change, but cannot alter private held-out tests or change training permission. Where benefits are small or data scarce, deliberate non-action is valid.

**Experiment loop:** freeze a weakness group → obtain permission for eligible examples → independently verify new labels → ensure family-diverse split → choose simple baseline plus one original candidate → run approved training → evaluate → independent challenger → recommend retain/reject → archive lesson. The model-selection policy itself can be learned later from real historical experiment outcomes, with evaluator protection and comparison to simple heuristic policies.

**Acceptance:** The system should correctly choose 'fix a missing tool', 'collect positive examples', 'retrain model', 'refresh source' or 'do nothing' on independently constructed scenarios with known real downstream consequences. It should reject a proposed retrain using only duplicate negatives and identify when training cannot add value. Quantify cost per successful model improvement over many runs instead of claiming perpetual automatic learning.

## 185. Independent audit service and evidence-driven developer workflow

The current Sol/Argon manual relay is a temporary external-engineering practice. The permanent autonomous system requires a separate, independently controlled **review service** with restricted access, reproducible checks and a tamper-evident submission trail. The author freezes a product commit and preregistration before submitting; the independent reviewer checks a clean checkout, generates private unfamiliar cases outside the author's workspace and returns a signed or hashed review receipt with precise scope. If `REPAIRS_REQUIRED`, the author receives reproductions that do not disclose protected evaluation answers unnecessarily. If accepted, a trusted release manager advances only the authorized baseline and maintains revertibility.

**Five-update batched development:** Sol currently authors up to five substantial independently testable changes in one activation, with separate commits and author tests; Argon reviews per-update and combined integration in one subsequent activation. Author can repair an earlier rejected contribution and continue implementing provisionally after local validation, but later commits cannot be promoted over an unaccepted dependency. Sol must not claim that Argon independently reviewed Sol's own author-side tests; Argon must not self-review any code it authored. The owner relays short handoffs; no agent-to-agent screen automation, waiting or monitoring is permitted.

**Permanent artifact expectations:** `ATLAS_MASTER_PLAN.md` owner intent, `ATLAS_HANDOFF.md` current assignment, `ATLAS_OWNER_REPORT.md` independently evidence-based summary, `ATLAS_REVIEW_PROTOCOL.md` gates, ADR ledger, per-feature review receipts, immutable training/evaluation manifests and requirements crosswalk. All agents should read the relevant indexed sections rather than pasting a sixty-thousand-word book into each short prompt. AI navigation metadata must be deterministic and validated against actual headings and source hashes.

**Acceptance:** Deliberately inject wrong review scope, stale Git hash, modified private test, changed dataset after preregistration, missing reviewer independence and mismatched dependency receipts. The reviewer/release manager must reject promotion. Do not replace independent review with automated 'looks good' model judgments.

## 186. Agent-facing knowledge navigation, context budgets and documentation contracts

A very large master plan can be durable only if future agents reliably **find and apply the correct sections**. Preserve a full human-readable canonical file with numbered headings; generate a validated machine-readable chapter index that contains stable section ID, title, heading level, path, line range, anchor, text digest, owner authority class, required reread events, relevant capabilities and dependencies. Provide an AI quickstart containing the non-negotiable doctrine, current-truth warning, handoff link, safety restrictions and how to retrieve any section by ID. An abbreviated brief is a gateway, not a replacement for the full document.

**Context budget:** At activation check the full document's hash, date, all chapter identifiers and current handoff; read the doctrine, role/safety sections and current assignment completely; retrieve relevant chapters and referenced original archives as needed. For a major architectural change, reconciliation or security policy review, read the entire canonical document or use verified segmented navigation to cover every chapter. A model should never claim to have read all 60,000+ words if only a snippet was supplied. Preserve archived originals as historical sources with no executable authority.

**Document change protocol:** Owner intent changes result in a versioned change request and source-level diff, new effective date, review of conflicting earlier clauses, updated chapter index, requirements crosswalk and prompts, validated hashes and a separate document commit. Do not let an LLM silently overwrite the source spec in the same commit as a feature. Keep an explicit `SUPERSEDED_BY` trail and archived previous editions. A new canonical version may change priorities but cannot retroactively make an unaccepted implementation become accepted.

**Acceptance:** A test loads the chapter index, verifies every referenced line range and heading against the canonical file, checks exact digest, resolves both plain-language and ID-based queries, finds the security and training clauses, and fails if sections were renamed without updated links. Perform a scripted cold-start audit in which the next agent is asked to identify current accepted baseline, safety restrictions and NEXT-021–025 gates without inventing implementation status.

## 187. Reliable API and internal contract evolution

Every public/inter-agent interface and stored record needs a versioned schema, compatibility rules, default-deny handling for unknown operations, and migration tests. A new code path cannot silently reinterpret a prior field named `verified` to mean 'model predicted'. Use discriminated types for `evidence_verified`, `author_tested`, `independent_accepted`, `shadow_model`, `pretrained_reference` and `original_native`. Design exact request/response contracts for Project Inspector, Knowledge Broker, Training Director, Model Registry, Research Master, Engineering Master and owner permission service. Include size limits, required fields, timestamps, source digest linkage, explicit null semantics and reasoned error codes.

**Backward compatibility:** Preserve old task receipts as historical immutable inputs; migrate derived indexes rather than mutate evidence. If a schema cannot be upgraded without losing meaning, quarantine that record and record a disposition. Keep model checkpoint loader compatibility separate from dataset lineage. When transferring an artifact between ATLAS and future PLATZ Engine adapters, use stable namespaced IDs and exported canonical formats, never direct database access to the other product's internals.

**Acceptance:** Fuzz malformed schema versions, missing required IDs, unknown tool methods, conflicting/null grants, huge nested arrays, malicious serialization edge cases and stale dependency hashes. A version mismatch must be visible and safe. Provide test fixtures for accepted old versions, current versions and consciously rejected unknown future versions.

## 188. Packaging as a reproducible, inspectable software supply chain

The owner should be able to reinstall ATLAS on a private Windows workstation using a current verified release without depending on developer-chat history. A release bundle should contain app binaries, exact source revision and hash, platform/build requirements, model registry metadata, migrations, license notices, human/AI navigation documents, accepted feature matrix, error-recovery instructions, rollback guidance, independent-review receipt IDs and package checksum/signature information. Model files can be separate optional downloads with integrity checks, and must never be silently retrieved from unknown endpoints.

**Clean room test:** On a new Windows profile with no old store, the app starts offline and performs a supported deterministic task. With permitted old profile data, it previews migration, backs up files, restores and verifies citations. With no optional local LLM, native-only tasks remain functional. With invalid GPU driver or absent CUDA, tiny CPU-native inference and core verifier still work where supported. Under loss of disk space or interrupted extraction, installation fails recoverably. Privacy-sensitive artifacts never appear in general installation logs.

**Provenance:** Build recipes, dependency versions, compiler flags and CI environment must be reproducible. Protected review artifacts cannot be included in public packages. Packages should be signed when trusted key management exists; a checksum alone proves content identity only if the reference checksum is trusted independently. Output inventories must detect missing libraries, stale binaries, model checkpoint swap and hidden cloud dependencies.

**Acceptance:** Test source-built and shipped binary under both Windows PowerShell packaging paths while supported, including actual WPF UI automation and shutdown. Independently compare shipped source manifests to reviewed revisions and produce a clean install acceptance receipt. Labels such as 'production-ready' require a declared, independently tested scope.

## 189. PLATZ Technologies ecosystem and separate product boundaries

PLATZ Technologies is the umbrella vision. ATLAS AI remains the intelligence/research/training product; PLATZ Engine remains a distinct creative/game engine family with PLATZ Studio, Core, Runtime, Hub and the `PZ` identity. PLATZ Engine's independent master documents are the authoritative engine specifications, and its fresh-rebuild strategy may selectively learn from read-only FORGE historical code. There is no current permission to merge ATLAS and PLATZ repositories, import unverified old FORGE modules, or divert the bounded ATLAS Next native-intelligence milestone into GPU rendering-engine development.

**Future interoperable workflows:** ATLAS may inspect an explicitly shared PLATZ project snapshot, generate proposed versioned behavior/action graph changes, submit code or artwork artifacts, run eligible export validators and receive test receipts. The interface should include API version, project and asset digests, owner grant, dry-run/proposed changes, reversible write protocol, result status and independent host enforcement. Neither product may open the other's credential stores or private memory by direct database access.

**PLATZ creative architecture preservation:** Distinguish Variables → Events → Conditions → Actions visual logic and equivalent code behavior; canonical editable project data, non-destructive editing, numeric precision, real Vulkan-first rendering evidence with OpenGL/CPU fallbacks, pixel/voxel/2D/2.5D/3D content, accessibility, asset-scaled workflows and real build/export quality. These are PLATZ product goals and must not be falsely listed as currently implemented by ATLAS.

**Acceptance:** An eventual interoperability demo must work with versioned mock or real PLATZ APIs in a separately approved integration milestone, produce reversible artifacts and refuse unauthorized source modifications. No work on PLATZ Engine is part of NEXT-021–025 unless the owner separately changes the assigned milestone.

## 190. Post-NEXT-025 roadmap, gates and native-only graduation ladder

After the initial five native-intelligence milestones, plan **families of five substantial updates** around measured bottlenecks, not fixed numerical prestige. Suggested waves are research proposals, not automatic commitments:

- **Wave A — Native language utility:** stronger scratch-trained text understanding, grounded answer composition, multilingual evidence alignment, calibrated uncertainty and useful native-only chat over permitted knowledge.
- **Wave B — Practical coding:** project inspection beyond declarations, original code-analysis and patch-proposal policies, independent test selection, real compile checks in a qualified guest, and owner-reviewed reversible changes.
- **Wave C — Research and information upkeep:** deeper local autonomous study, contradiction graph, safe remote research sandbox, source-refresh schedules and verified knowledge-to-procedure reuse.
- **Wave D — Autonomous learning laboratory:** eligible data acquisition, multi-seed architecture study, model compatibility and continual-learning policies, efficient GPU scheduling and independently qualified improvement proposals.
- **Wave E — Creative workflows:** book continuity, sprite and texture pipelines, Blender and game-engine coding, document generation and eventual controlled PLATZ interoperability.
- **Wave F — Safe delegated computer tasks:** signed effect receipts, isolated guest actions, bounded scheduled work, human approvals, auditing and verified recovery. Never enable as a side effect of a model-quality result.

**Graduation gates:** (1) model is original by provenance; (2) task family and training rights are known; (3) architecture/checkpoint is reproducible; (4) native inference runs without reference calls; (5) unfamiliar-task utility is independently established over relevant baselines or meaningful efficiency at quality parity; (6) risk threshold and abstention are acceptable; (7) host/grant boundary and user experience are independently tested; (8) owner approves activating the specific capability. Graduating one task family does not qualify the model to control unrelated domains.

**Acceptance:** The roadmap should be reprioritized from actual evaluator findings. A defeated hypothesis is archived rather than disguised as a completed benefit. The Master plan remains the long-term contract; the handoff remains the immediate bounded assignment.

## 191. Representative owner acceptance demonstrations that prove useful AI

The following are future demonstration contracts, not claims of present ability. Each demonstration needs actual input artifacts, a real executable run, exact model lineage, source citations or test outcomes and an independent witness. A demo may be deliberately marked blocked until its prerequisites pass.

| ID | Owner request / task | Required visible outcome | What does **not** count |
|---|---|---|---|
| DEMO-01 | 'Explain how this unfamiliar module works' | Native or explicitly labeled reference explanation tied to actual symbols and call graph | Filename list with generic text |
| DEMO-02 | 'Find the cause of this failing test' | Reproduced failure, relevant source, specific supported diagnosis | Confident guess without test |
| DEMO-03 | 'Write a complete component for my project' | Full paste-ready file, dependencies, insertion points, verified APIs | Pseudocode presented as compiling code |
| DEMO-04 | 'Research these conflicting facts' | Claim-level source evidence, disagreement and scoped verdict | First search result paraphrase |
| DEMO-05 | 'Find and reuse the procedure that worked last time' | Proven procedure selected with applicability and verified novel outcome | Similar text search alone |
| DEMO-06 | 'Train a native specialist' | Actual checkpoint from random initialization, eligible data, independent comparison | Dataset summary and mock graph |
| DEMO-07 | 'Why did ATLAS reject an improvement?' | Baseline vs candidate cases, loss/utility/cost and negative record | Generic 'did not improve' |
| DEMO-08 | 'Run without other AI models' | Zero pretrained calls attested and measurable native model contributions | Deterministic host-only answer relabeled neural |
| DEMO-09 | 'Coordinate the three Masters on a new task' | Distinct learned role contributions with ablation benefit or honest tie | Three agent names in logs |
| DEMO-10 | 'Keep my private preferences separate' | Scoped opt-in memory, export/delete, cross-scope denial | Public plaintext file copy |
| DEMO-11 | 'Make a change and let me approve it' | Isolated preview and tests, external review, exact diff and host approval | Silent write to personal source |
| DEMO-12 | 'Stop training now' | Verified safe stop/pause and recoverable optimizer state | UI spinner stops while GPU continues |
| DEMO-13 | 'Recover after an interrupted update' | Restored app/profile/checkpoints with intact digests | Reinstall and lose history |
| DEMO-14 | 'Generate a consistent sprite sheet' | Editable assets, consistent frames and real export validation | Generated description only |
| DEMO-15 | 'Help with Unreal or Unity' | Version-correct engine-specific project changes or verified guide | Plain C++/C# with nonexistent API |
| DEMO-16 | 'Use public web research without my identity' | Verified isolated grant-bound fetch and report of privacy limitations | Host browser with owner account |
| DEMO-17 | 'Improve itself overnight' | Owner opt-in, safe resource lease, real completed validated experiments | Unapproved persistent background task |
| DEMO-18 | 'Compare my previous model with the new one' | Same tasks, versions, lineage, measured outcomes and fair cost | Comparing mismatched public scores |
| DEMO-19 | 'Restore a deleted/changed project revision' | Authorized versioned restore with original file checksum | Reconstruct from memory |
| DEMO-20 | 'Give me the truth about progress' | Source-backed accepted/pending/blocked state and useful owner-facing delta | Claims from development chat alone |

Each demo must have a stable test ID, required host/user scope, fixture source and revision, success oracle, owner acceptance evidence, independent review and known unsupported cases. A demo failure stays visible in the release plan until actually fixed; do not suppress it from the owner report.

## 192. Owner control, product quality bars and anti-forgetting release protocol

The owner owns the product vision and permission policies; neither original models nor external developer agents can downgrade those commitments. Every development activation must verify canonical master hash, relevant chapters, current handoff, actual Git status and independent verdict, then record what work it is authorized to perform. The owner should only have to forward a concise stable handoff trigger; Sol and Argon preserve detailed reciprocal assignments in files. The author cannot self-approve a batch, and neither agent can use Computer Use, waiting, background monitoring or browser automation to communicate with the other.

**Product quality bars:** A release feature should be (1) useful to a real owner task; (2) correctly implemented; (3) tested on actual source or data; (4) safe under hostile inputs; (5) recoverable and explainable; (6) accessible and reasonably performant; (7) independently accepted where required; (8) documented with limitations; (9) compliant with privacy/rights; and (10) clearly attributed as deterministic, original native or reference-assisted. Features failing the bar remain experimental or blocked. Do not create endless milestone counts as a substitute for capability growth.

**The permanent 'do not forget' protocol:** Keep the canonical full specification unchanged until an explicit versioned owner decision; ship a human reader guide and AI index; maintain an append-only amendment ledger; preserve prior versions and every original attachment in hashed archives; run a CI check ensuring requirements and chapter IDs map to actual sections; include the current specification digest in the agent handoff; require an explicit owner-visible diff for master revisions; and do not use a dynamically generated memory summary to override the canonical file. This is durable repository process, not a guarantee that a model retains all text internally.

**Scope of completeness:** The master plan and historical source archive together preserve all accessible supplied content, but no system can guarantee that unavailable historical ZIPs or unshared future decisions are present. The source-gap register must state what remains unavailable and how to add it later without silently changing binding intent. When conflict exists between a historical 'fully autonomous' aspiration and an active security boundary, preserve the aspiration as future work and enforce current restrictions.

**Acceptable owner-facing summary after a five-update batch:** 'Here are the five completed features, exactly what you can do with them, each frozen source revision, independent verdict, native trained models and benchmark comparisons, regression/security failures, actual model-origin percentages, remaining capabilities not implemented, and which questions you must approve before new autonomy is enabled.' Anything less is a research or developer log, not a complete product report.

# APPENDIX A — THE 12 CANONICAL TIER-5 VAULTS (PRESERVED REGISTRY KEYS, RECONCILED v3.2)
