The detailed catalog across Appendices A–D preserves the original 12 vault names, 20 executive cluster slots, 100 domain Master proposals, and 25 micro-specialist families with explicitly named examples from the user-supplied unified specification. **Every role is a PROPOSED capability contract or curriculum slot unless a current accepted model registry and benchmark proves otherwise.** Retired old claims of fixed 1B minima and EPE multipliers do not apply.

* V-01; atlas.vault.answer_cards: Canonical Answer Card Vault. Proposed storage: Markdown + YAML Frontmatter + SQLite FTS5. Scope: Stores verified question-to-answer cards with SHA-256 provenance, exact match triggers, parameter templates, and instant 0B response payloads.

* V-02; atlas.vault.procedures: Executable Reasoning Procedure Vault. Proposed storage: Versioned Markdown SOPs + JSON Decision Trees. Scope: Stores step-by-step reasoning algorithms, diagnostic checklists, failure recovery playbooks, and domain heuristics loaded dynamically into context.

* V-03; atlas.vault.deterministic_tools: Deterministic Script & Parser Vault. Proposed storage: Python 3.12, SQL, PowerShell, Rust CLI, Tree-sitter. Scope: Stores verified scripts, AST codemods, file converters, math solvers, and linters that execute deterministically with zero neural inference.

* V-04; atlas.vault.code_intelligence: Polyglot Code & AST Graph Vault. Proposed storage: Tree-sitter CST + SQLite Symbol Graph + LanceDB. Scope: Indexes every repository file, class, function signature, call graph, and dependency edge across 35+ programming languages.

* V-05; atlas.vault.personal_memory: Encrypted Personal Assistant Vault. Proposed storage: SQLCipher AES-256-GCM + 19 Markdown Category Files. Scope: Stores owner-authorized personal preferences, projects, goals, people, and routines with granular edit/forget/export controls.

* V-06; atlas.vault.science_reference: STEM & Formal Reference Vault. Proposed storage: DuckDB Parquet + LaTeX/SymPy/RDKit Canonical Tables. Scope: Stores physical constants, mathematical theorems, chemical properties, engineering tolerances, and peer-reviewed reference tables.

* V-07; atlas.vault.creative_bibles: Story Bibles, Lore & Style Vault. Proposed storage: Relational Lore Graph + Markdown Canon Files. Scope: Stores character sheets, world timelines, plot dependency graphs, dialogue voice profiles, and continuity constraints.

* V-08; atlas.vault.gamedev_assets: Game Engine & 3D Asset Registry Vault. Proposed storage: USD/glTF Metadata + UE5/Unity/Godot/Blender Catalog. Scope: Stores 3D mesh topology metrics, PBR material maps, shader node graphs, animation rigs, and level design blueprints.

* V-09; atlas.vault.verification_oracles: Verification Oracles & Golden Rubrics. Proposed storage: Pytest Suites + JSON Schema + Property Test Oracles. Scope: Stores executable test harnesses, golden input/output pairs, compiler flags, and safety checklists used by the Qualification Gate.

* V-10; atlas.vault.episodic_ledger: Immutable Episodic Audit Ledger. Proposed storage: Append-Only Hash-Chained SQLite WAL + JSONL. Scope: Records every prompt, Tier 5 lookup, model invocation, tool call, verification outcome, and user correction with cryptographic hashes.

* V-11; atlas.vault.training_corpora: Decontaminated Training & DPO Vault. Proposed storage: Parquet + Safetensors + MinHash LSH Index. Scope: Stores verified SFT trajectories, DPO preference pairs, hard-negative failure clusters, and decontaminated benchmark splits.

* V-12; atlas.vault.model_lineage: Model Checkpoints & Manifest Vault. Proposed storage: Safetensors + GGUF + Signed JSON Lineage Manifests. Scope: Stores all 1B–12B Native ATLAS base weights, specialist checkpoints, tokenizer models, and before/after evaluation scorecards.
  Implementation note: Registry keys are preserved from the uploaded document; physical migration and encryption require compatibility testing, not hardcoded new storage roots.

The following retains the original IDs, cluster mapping, canonical identifiers, duties and verifier examples. Original 3B–6B sizes and EPE labels are research proposals, not mandatory budgets or demonstrated performance. A role may initially be served by a shared qualified model or deterministic procedure.

* T3-003 (T2-01) — atlas.master.debugging; Root-Cause Debugging & Crash Forensics Master. Duty: Isolates race conditions, memory leaks, stack traces, and logic regressions via minimal reproducers. Verification: Automated Reproducer Test Harness.

* T3-004 (T2-01) — atlas.master.code_review; Adversarial Code Review & Quality Master. Duty: Audits diffs for correctness, complexity, edge cases, API breakage, and maintainability. Verification: Static Analysis + Mutation Test Score.

* T3-005 (T2-01) — atlas.master.refactoring; Large-Scale Refactoring & Migration Master. Duty: Executes multi-file semantic migrations, legacy modernization, and dead-code elimination. Verification: Full Regression Suite + AST Equivalence Check.

* T3-006 (T2-02) — atlas.master.routing; Hierarchical Task Decomposition & Routing Master. Duty: Classifies user intent, checks Tier 5 0B Answer Cards first, and builds minimal-VRAM execution DAGs. Verification: JSON DAG Schema + Latency Budget Verifier.

* T3-007 (T2-02) — atlas.master.retrieval; Tier 5 File & Hybrid Retrieval Master. Duty: Executes hybrid FTS5 lexical + LanceDB vector + AST symbol graph retrieval with exact provenance. Verification: SHA-256 Citation Span Verifier.

* T3-008 (T2-02) — atlas.master.context_compiler; Context Packing & Token Budget Master. Duty: Compresses retrieved Tier 5 files, procedures, and episodic history into optimal prompt windows. Verification: Token Budget & Information Retention Check.

* T3-009 (T2-02) — atlas.master.tool_orchestrator; Deterministic Tool & Sandbox Execution Master. Duty: Dispatches deterministic Python/SQL/CLI tools inside isolated sandboxes with timeout enforcement. Verification: Subprocess Exit-Code & Output Schema Gate.

* T3-010 (T2-02) — atlas.master.verification; Source, Evidence & Claim Verification Master. Duty: Audits every candidate response against Tier 5 files and deterministic test outputs before delivery. Verification: Zero-Unverified-Claim Entailment Checker.

* T3-011 (T2-03) — atlas.master.narrative_arch; Long-Horizon Narrative & Story Bible Master. Duty: Maintains multi-book plot arcs, pacing, character motivations, and continuity across 100k+ words. Verification: Story Bible Entity & Timeline Consistency Gate.

* T3-012 (T2-03) — atlas.master.prose_style; Literary Prose, Dialogue & Voice Master. Duty: Crafts vivid scene prose, distinct character dialogue, cadence, and genre-authentic stylistic register. Verification: Stylometric & Anti-Cliche Rubric Evaluator.

# APPENDIX D — THE 25 MICRO-SPECIALIST FAMILIES (2,125 PROPOSED SLOTS)

The original counts sum to 2,125 proposed specialist identities. They are catalog capacity, not 2,125 trained billion-parameter checkpoint files. Preserve exact family IDs and counts while allowing a real specialist to be a small native neural net, adapter, shared foundation role, ranking algorithm, or verified deterministic program.

* F-01; (120 Models) — Systems & Compiled Languages. Verifier examples: gcc/clang/rustc/go/zig compilers + ASan/UBSan/Miri + Criterion benchmarks.

* F-02; (90 Models) — Managed, Enterprise & JVM/.NET Languages. Verifier examples: dotnet Roslyn / javac / kotlinc / scalac + xUnit/JUnit + BenchmarkDotNet.

* F-03; (110 Models) — Python Ecosystem, Scientific & Automation. Verifier examples: ruff + mypy/pyright --strict + pytest + hypothesis property testing.

* F-04; (110 Models) — Web Frontend, TypeScript, CSS & Browsers. Verifier examples: tsc --noEmit + Biome/ESLint + Playwright headless DOM/visual assertions.

* F-05; (85 Models) — Databases, SQL, Vector & Storage Engines. Verifier examples: SQLite/DuckDB/PostgreSQL EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN + schema & ACID invariant checks.

* F-06; (120 Models) — Unreal Engine 5, Unity, Godot & Game Systems. Verifier examples: UnrealHeaderTool + Unity Roslyn + Godot CLI + deterministic frame-time profiler.

* F-07; (95 Models) — Blender 3D, Shaders, VFX & Technical Art. Verifier examples: Headless blender --background --python + DXC/glslangValidator + mesh manifold check.

* F-08; (100 Models) — AI, Deep Learning, CUDA & Model Training. Verifier examples: PyTorch tensor parity + NVCC/Triton compile + loss convergence & VRAM budget checks.

* F-09; (85 Models) — Cybersecurity, AppSec, Binary & Crypto. Verifier examples: Semgrep/Bandit + constant-time assembly check + isolated container exploit harness.

* F-10; (95 Models) — Pure & Applied Mathematics & Formal Proofs. Verifier examples: Lean 4 kernel + Z3 SMT solver + SymPy exact symbolic equivalence check.

* F-11; (85 Models) — Physics, Chemistry, Biology & Earth Sciences. Verifier examples: Pint dimensional analysis + RDKit molecule sanitizer + conservation law checks.

* F-12; (85 Models) — Hardware, RTL (Verilog/VHDL), Embedded & Robotics. Verifier examples: Verilator + Icarus Verilog + Ngspice + ROS2 URDF kinematic verifier.

* F-13; (75 Models) — Cloud, Kubernetes, Linux, WSL2 & DevOps. Verifier examples: kubeconform + terraform validate + shellcheck + systemd-analyze verify.

* F-14; (85 Models) — Creative Writing, Fiction, Poetry & Screenwriting. Verifier examples: Story Bible continuity graph checker + prosody/meter analyzer + anti-cliche filter.

* F-15; (70 Models) — Technical Writing, Rhetoric, Editing & Journalism. Verifier examples: Readability/citation verifier + Markdown/LaTeX compiler + claim-to-source audit.

* F-16; (85 Models) — World Languages, Translation & Philology. Verifier examples: Terminology glossary lock + morphological parser + back-translation semantic check.

* F-17; (75 Models) — Quantitative Finance, Accounting & Economics. Verifier examples: Double-entry ledger tie-out + QuantLib pricing check + zero-lookahead verifier.

* F-18; (70 Models) — Legal Analysis, Contracts, Patents & Compliance. Verifier examples: Defined-term cross-reference checker + SPDX license solver + citation verifier.

* F-19; (70 Models) — Biomedical, Clinical Decision Support & Health. Verifier examples: FHIR R4 schema validator + UCUM dose unit checker + clinical safety disclaimer gate.

* F-20; (65 Models) — Education, Socratic Tutoring & Assessment. Verifier examples: Prerequisite DAG validator + independent math/code solver answer-key verification.

* F-21; (65 Models) — Personal Assistant, Memory, Scheduling & Life. Verifier examples: Zone-2 privacy firewall check + OR-Tools calendar solver + JSON schema validator.

* F-22; (75 Models) — Audio DSP, Video FFmpeg, CAD & Graphics. Verifier examples: FFmpeg filter parser + CadQuery watertight solid check + Matplotlib bbox verifier.

* F-23; (70 Models) — Document Intelligence, Office (DOCX/XLSX/PPTX/PDF). Verifier examples: LibreOffice headless PDF conversion + pypdfium2 visual inspection + OpenXML validator.

* F-24; (60 Models) — Enterprise Operations, BI, Supply Chain & Strategy. Verifier examples: SQL/DuckDB metric tie-out + HiGHS LP solver + DAG critical-path verifier.

### D.1 The 125 explicit specialist examples from the source

These named examples, grouped by family, are potential roles only; none is presumed trained or installed. Use their source catalog identifiers when generating new role proposals.

#### Family F-01: Systems & Compiled Languages

* atlas.spec.cpp23_core — Original proposed size 1.5B; specialized capability: C++23/26 concepts, ranges, constexpr, coroutines, RAII, template metaprogramming.

* atlas.spec.cpp_stl_concurrency — Original proposed size 1.2B; specialized capability: C++ std::atomic, memory_order, lock-free queues, std::execution, SIMD intrinsics.

# APPENDIX E — TRAINING COUNCIL, CURRICULUM, UI AND PERSONAL-MEMORY CATALOGS

The following historical IDs and role/dimension names are included for traceability, not as authorization to run a GPU training job, promote a checkpoint, fill private category files or guarantee numeric timing. Translate any old automatic promotion step into a **proposal plus independent review and owner authorization**, and any old GPU/latency threshold into a measured resource-specific test. Personal category names are schema descriptions, not imported personal facts.

### E.1 Original Training Council slots

* TC-01; atlas.train.chief_director — Chief Autonomous Training Director; (6B Native). Ranks capability bottlenecks across all 2,120+ models using EV = (Impact x Freq x Reach x Fixability) / GPU_Hours and schedules the training queue.

* TC-02; atlas.train.diagnostician — Failure Taxonomy & Bottleneck Diagnostician; (4B Native). Clusters episodic failure logs, verifier rejections, and Arena losses into actionable root-cause failure signatures.

* TC-03; atlas.train.curriculum_arch — 14-Dimension Curriculum Architect; (5B Native). Designs graduated training tasks across the 14 difficulty dimensions with guaranteed deterministic verifiers.

* TC-04; atlas.train.data_curator — Dataset Curator & Decontamination Officer; (4B Native). Executes MinHash LSH (Jaccard < 0.15) + 13-gram exact decontamination against all evaluation benchmarks and verifies every training label.

* TC-05; atlas.train.sft_dpo_coach — SFT, LoRA+, GaLore & DPO/GRPO Coach; (5B Native). Configures hyperparameters, cosine schedules, GaLore rank projections, and preference margins for the WSL2 CUDA worker.

* TC-06; atlas.train.distillation_mentor — Distillation & Scaffolding Graduation Mentor; (5B Native). Manages Stage 0–3 teacher-student distillation using verified trajectories and certifies when Qwen/Gemma scaffolding can be retired.

* TC-07; atlas.train.red_team_adv — Adversarial Red-Team & Edge-Case Challenger; (5B Native). Generates adversarial boundary inputs, prompt traps, and schema stress-tests to expose brittle memorization before promotion.

* TC-08; atlas.train.arena_judge — Model Arena & Qualification Gatekeeper; (6B Native). Runs blind held-out benchmark suites, computes Elo/Bradley-Terry ratings with 95% CIs, and enforces the 7 Qualification States.

* TC-09; atlas.train.flywheel_compiler — Neural-to-File Flywheel & Answer Card Compiler; (4B Native). Extracts reusable Reasoning Procedures and 0B Tier-5 Answer Cards from newly mastered training trajectories.

### E.2 The original 14-stage autonomous state machine

* Stage 01 — TELEMETRY_HARVEST. Scans episodic ledger (V-10) for verifier rejections, user corrections, high-latency queries, and Tier-5 misses.

* Stage 02 — BOTTLENECK_CLUSTERING. Diagnostician (TC-02) clusters failures by root cause and target model across all 2,120+ specialists.

* Stage 03 — EV_PRIORITY_SCORING. Chief Director (TC-01) ranks candidate upgrades via EV = (Impact x Frequency x Reach x Fixability) / Est_GPU_Hours.

* Stage 04 — CURRICULUM_SYNTHESIS. Curriculum Architect (TC-03) generates graduated problems across the 14 difficulty dimensions with executable verifiers.

* Stage 05 — DATASET_CONSTRUCTION. Generates verified positive trajectories and contrastive hard-negative DPO pairs; verifies 100% of labels via deterministic tools.

* Stage 06 — DECONTAMINATION_GATE. Data Curator (TC-04) runs MinHash LSH (Jaccard < 0.15) + exact 13-gram check against all held-out benchmarks.

* Stage 07 — RESOURCE_PREFLIGHT. Verifies RTX 5080 VRAM budget (<=15.2 GB), GPU temp (<78°C), NVMe free space (>50 GB), and snapshot integrity. **Legacy aspirational threshold: not guaranteed.**

* Stage 08 — GPU_TRAINING_EXECUTION. SFT/DPO Coach (TC-05) launches WSL2 CUDA worker with live step telemetry (loss, grad norm, LR, tok/s, VRAM).

* Stage 09 — MID_RUN_PROBE_CHECKS. Executes periodic validation probes every N steps; triggers early stop if gradient explosion or overfitting is detected.

* Stage 10 — DETERMINISTIC_EVAL. Evaluates candidate checkpoint on held-out domain test suite and full regression suite.

* Stage 11 — RED_TEAM_CHALLENGE. Red-Team Challenger (TC-07) attacks candidate with adversarial boundary cases, prompt traps, and schema stress-tests.

* Stage 12 — ARENA_QUALIFICATION. Arena Judge (TC-08) runs blind head-to-head matchups and assigns one of the 7 Qualification States.

* Stage 13 — PROMOTION_OR_ROLLBACK. Promotes ˋQUALIFIED_PROMOTEDˋ checkpoint to active Registry (or rolls back atomically on regression). **CURRENT OVERRIDE: reviewer + owner gate required.**

* Stage 14 — FLYWHEEL_COMPILATION. Flywheel Compiler (TC-09) compiles newly mastered patterns into Tier-5 0B Answer Cards and increments Curriculum Level.
  Note: Part VII (Sections 41 & 108.3) gives the revised durable state machine; retain legacy stage IDs as mapping aliases where existing records depend on them.

### E.3 The original 14 curriculum dimensions

* Dim 01: Reasoning Horizon (Steps). Foundational: 1–2 step direct application. Intermediate: 3–6 step chained deduction. Advanced: 7–15 step branching proof/plan. Expert: 16–40+ step hierarchical synthesis with backtracking.

* Dim 02: Constraint Density. Foundational: 1–2 explicit constraints. Intermediate: 3–5 interacting constraints. Advanced: 6–10 tight coupled constraints. Expert: 12+ competing multi-objective constraints with trade-offs.

* Dim 03: Context Signal-to-Noise. Foundational: Clean exact snippet provided. Intermediate: 3–5 files with 20% distractors. Advanced: 15+ files with 60% near-miss distractors. Expert: 64k–128k repo/archive with contradictory legacy artifacts.

* Dim 04: Ambiguity & Underspecification. Foundational: Fully specified inputs. Intermediate: Minor missing default parameter. Advanced: Implicit domain edge cases. Expert: Adversarially underspecified prompt requiring safe clarification.

* Dim 05: Tool & Verifier Chaining. Foundational: Single deterministic tool call. Intermediate: 2–3 sequential tool calls. Advanced: DAG of 4–7 parallel/conditional tools. Expert: Iterative tool-feedback loop with error recovery.

* Dim 06: Error Recovery & Replanning. Foundational: Zero-error happy path. Intermediate: Single syntax/schema error fix. Advanced: Mid-execution tool failure + replan. Expert: Cascading multi-component failure requiring architectural pivot.

* Dim 07: Output Structural Strictness. Foundational: Free-form Markdown prose. Intermediate: Simple flat JSON object. Advanced: Deeply nested recursive JSON/AST schema. Expert: Byte-exact patch / formal Lean4 proof / multi-file transaction.

* Dim 08: Domain Edge-Case Rarity. Foundational: Common textbook patterns. Intermediate: Standard production corner cases. Advanced: Rare concurrency/boundary conditions. Expert: Pathological hardware/compiler/numeric precision traps.

* Dim 09: Cross-Domain Synthesis. Foundational: Single narrow sub-specialty. Intermediate: 2 adjacent specialties in 1 cluster. Advanced: 3–4 domains across 2 clusters. Expert: 5+ heterogeneous clusters (e.g., Math + CUDA + UE5 + Legal).

* Dim 10: Adversarial Premise Resistance. Foundational: Truthful user premises. Intermediate: Subtle user misconception. Advanced: Plausible-sounding false citation/API. Expert: Deliberate sycophancy trap or conflicting Tier-5 record test.

* Dim 11: Token & Latency Efficiency. Foundational: Generous reasoning budget. Intermediate: Standard production token budget. Advanced: Tight token budget requiring concise CoT. Expert: Ultra-low-latency budget or compile-to-0B Answer Card target.

* Dim 12: Temporal & State Mutation Depth. Foundational: Stateless single turn. Intermediate: 3–5 state mutations tracked. Advanced: 15+ state transitions across files. Expert: Long-horizon multi-session state machine with rollback invariants.

* Dim 13: Verification Rigor. Foundational: Syntax & schema lint pass. Intermediate: Unit test suite pass (10+ tests). Advanced: Property-based & fuzz test pass (1,000+ cases). Expert: Formal mathematical proof or bit-exact reference parity.

* Dim 14: Zero-Model Compilation Yield. Foundational: Raw neural answer only. Intermediate: Reusable Markdown Answer Card. Advanced: Parameterized template + regex router. Expert: General deterministic Python/SQL tool replacing future neural calls.

### E.4 All 13 advanced technical workspaces

* WS-01 — Home — Mission Command & Intelligence Overview (top tab: Home): Displays active operating mode (Zero-Model 0B, Normal, Private Playground, Personal Assistant), live RTX 5080 VRAM/temp gauges, active model stack, and 1-click quick actions.

* WS-09 — Training Council & Queue — Autonomous Director Board (top tab: Training): Inspect the 9-Agent Training Council's EV bottleneck rankings, reorder the persistent training queue, or request custom capability upgrades.

* WS-13 — Storage & Privacy Auditor — Disk Path & Ledger Inspector (top tab: Memory): Inspect exact Windows 11 file paths, disk usage, SQLCipher encryption status, hash-chained audit logs, and volatile RAM zeroization proofs.

# APPENDIX F — ORIGINAL 28 OWNER MISSION GOALS AND TRACEABILITY

The IDs in Appendices F and G preserve the historical traceability inventory. They are **not actual acceptance results** and do not override the new objective tests in Part XIV. Any old test demanding a fixed 1B minimum, 3T equivalence, universal <2 ms response, guaranteed <3 s shutdown, or an obsolete commit is now a research hypothesis or rewritten current-repo regression, not an unconditional completion gate.

* M-01 — Normal AI Task Execution (source subsystem: Tier 5 / Tier 1–4): Execute everyday writing, analysis, coding, math, research, and planning inside a unified desktop workspace with automatic routing.

* M-02 — Zero-Model Direct File Execution (0B Mode) (source subsystem: Tier 5 Vaults): Answer verified queries directly from canonical Markdown Answer Cards, SQLite/DuckDB tables, and deterministic scripts in <2 ms using 0.0 GB VRAM. **Historical aspiration only; reinterpret using current measurements and independent validation.**

* M-03 — Private Model Testing (Volatile Playground) (source subsystem: Zone 1 Sandbox): Test any model or side-by-side comparison in an ephemeral RAM-only sandbox with zero disk persistence and zero training extraction.

* M-04 — Authorized Personal Assistant Memory (source subsystem: Zone 2 + Tier 5): Use a dedicated personal assistant that remembers owner-approved conversations and organizes facts into 19+ external category files.

* M-05 — Complete Model Registry Visibility (source subsystem: Model Registry): Inspect every installed, training, candidate, and scaffolding model with accurate provenance, SHA-256 hash, parameter scale (1B–12B), and EPE rating.

* M-06 — Owner-Directed Model Improvement (source subsystem: Training Lab): Select any registered 1B–12B Native ATLAS model and queue a targeted capability upgrade with explicit benchmarks.

* M-07 — Autonomous Bottleneck Selection (source subsystem: Training Director): Allow ATLAS to analyze failure telemetry, Arena gaps, and Tier 5 misses to select which model needs improvement most.

* M-08 — Custom Capability Targeting (source subsystem: Curriculum Architect): Specify exact capabilities (e.g., Rust lifetime repair, UE5 Niagara HLSL, multi-step replanning) and auto-generate training tasks.

* M-09 — From-Scratch Native Model Creation (1B–12B) (source subsystem: Architecture Lab): Initialize, pretrain, and specialize brand-new Native ATLAS models from random weights (strictly >=1B floor up to 12B Supreme Master).

* M-10 — RTX 5080 Hardware-Optimized Training (source subsystem: WSL2 CUDA Worker): Train models natively on the NVIDIA RTX 5080 (16GB GDDR7) backed by 96GB DDR5 RAM and 4TB NVMe SSD.

* M-11 — Live Genuine Training Telemetry (source subsystem: Training Telemetry): Watch live step loss, gradient norm, learning rate, token throughput, VRAM MB, GPU temp, and validation probes in real time.

* M-12 — Before/After Upgrade Scorecards (source subsystem: Qualification Gate): Inspect exact benchmark deltas, failure reductions, EPE multipliers, and deterministic test outcomes for every training job.

* M-13 — 14-Dimension Adaptive Difficulty Scaling (source subsystem: Curriculum Engine): Automatically escalate difficulty across 14 orthogonal axes when a model achieves >=85% verified mastery with <5% regression.

* M-14 — Continuous Autonomous Improvement Loop (source subsystem: 14-Stage State Machine): Chain justified training upgrades unattended when idle and within thermal/VRAM safety budgets.

* M-15 — Three-Mode Safe Stop & Pause Control (source subsystem: Worker IPC Bridge): Stop after current upgrade, pause safely with full AdamW optimizer state, or emergency-stop and free VRAM in <3 seconds.

* M-16 — 5-Tier Intelligence Hierarchy (source subsystem: Tier 1–5 Ecosystem): Coordinate 1 Tier-1 Supreme Master (12B), 20 Tier-2 Cluster Masters (8B–9B), 100 Tier-3 Domain Masters (3B–6B), and 2,000+ Tier-4 Specialists (1B–2B).

* M-17 — 9-Agent Training Council Hierarchy (source subsystem: Training Council): Maintain a dedicated hierarchy of 9 training specialists that diagnose, design curricula, curate data, train, red-team, and qualify models.

* M-18 — Files Are the True Master (Tier 5 Authority) (source subsystem: Tier 5 Sovereignty): Store all canonical facts, schemas, API specs, and verified answers outside model weights in inspectable, editable Tier 5 files.

* M-19 — Complete Storage Path Transparency (source subsystem: Storage Explorer): Inspect exact disk paths, sizes, encryption states, and retention policies for every conversation, file, dataset, and checkpoint.

* M-20 — Universal Subject & Project Specialization (source subsystem: Specialist Factory): Create and improve 1B–2B Native Micro-Specialists (achieving 80B Domain EPE) for any programming language, science, or creative craft.

* M-21 — Temporary Open-Weight Scaffolding & Retirement (source subsystem: Graduation Protocol): Use Qwen3.5/3.6/3.8 and Gemma 4 strictly for Stage 0 cold-start teaching and Arena baselines until Native ATLAS models graduate.

* M-22 — Dynamic Unthrottled Context & Output Budgets (source subsystem: Token Budget Engine): Eliminate hardcoded 256/512/1024 caps; scale context (up to 128k) and generation budgets dynamically by task and hardware profile.

* M-23 — Autonomous Self-Coding & Software Evolution (source subsystem: 14-Step Self-Dev): Let ATLAS inspect, patch, test, and verify its own codebase in isolated Git worktrees with automatic rollback on regression.

* M-24 — Zero Cloud Dependency & Local Sovereignty (source subsystem: Local-First Engine): Run 100% of routine inference, retrieval, training, evaluation, and self-development locally with cloud APIs disabled by default. **Historical aspiration only; reinterpret using current measurements and independent validation.**

* M-25 — Evidence-Backed Feature Proposals (source subsystem: Evolution Proposer): Generate structured, ROI-scored feature and specialist proposals backed by real failure cluster logs and benchmark gaps.

* M-26 — 1B = 80B Domain EPE & 12B = 3T System EPE (source subsystem: EPE Architecture): Achieve 80B-equivalent domain mastery in each 1B specialist and 3-Trillion-equivalent system intelligence via the 12B Supreme Master + Tier 5. **Historical aspiration only; reinterpret using current measurements and independent validation.**

* M-27 — Neural-to-File Compilation Flywheel (source subsystem: Tier 5 Flywheel): Compile every verified neural breakthrough into permanent Tier 5 Answer Cards, deterministic scripts, and Reasoning Procedures.

* M-28 — Universal File & Creative Pipeline Intelligence (source subsystem: Deterministic Parsers): Parse code, PDFs, Office files, CAD/3D assets, Blender bpy scripts, Unreal Engine 5 C++/Blueprints, and long-horizon Story Bibles.

# APPENDIX G — ORIGINAL 30 ACCEPTANCE-TEST INVENTORY (REVISED INTERPRETATION)

Source test IDs are preserved for backwards traceability. The source test language is historical, not necessarily a reliable or achievable technical guarantee. Each test must be implemented with empirical measurement, resource feasibility, confidentiality and safety as described in Part XIV (Sections 81–87). For example, AT-05 no longer rejects efficient sub-1B native models; AT-06/07 test real narrow-domain comparative performance rather than asserting automatic 80B/3T equivalence; AT-09/10 measure the actual GPU/RAM configuration without fixed unrealistically universal timing; AT-15-style emergency stop uses safe boundaries rather than promising <3 seconds in all kernels.

* AT-01 — Git State & Commit 7742514 Preservation. Original proposed test: Verify clean snapshot branch, SHA-256 checkpoint integrity, and zero regression on existing 5 schema-constrained planning probes. **Historical aspiration only; reinterpret using current measurements and independent validation.**

* AT-02 — Zero-Model Pure File Mode (0B Execution). Original proposed test: Unload all neural weights (0.0 GB VRAM), submit 50 canonical queries, and verify 100% accurate responses in <2 ms from Tier 5 Answer Cards. **Historical aspiration only; reinterpret using current measurements and independent validation.**

* AT-03 — Tier 5 Supremacy Conflict Override. Original proposed test: Inject a deliberate factual update into a Tier 5 Vault file and verify that the 12B/8B/4B/1B models immediately defer to the updated file over prior weights.

* AT-04 — Neural-to-File Compilation Flywheel. Original proposed test: Solve a novel multi-step problem via neural hierarchy, verify compilation into a Tier 5 Answer Card, and confirm subsequent identical/parameterized queries execute in 0B mode.

* AT-05 — Strict 1B–12B Parameter Scale Enforcement. Original proposed test: Attempt to register a <1B (e.g., 350M) or >12B Native specialist and verify the Registry Schema Validator rejects it with a clear boundary diagnostic. **Historical aspiration only; reinterpret using current measurements and independent validation.**

* AT-06 — 1B = 80B Domain EPE Specialist Verification. Original proposed test: Evaluate a graduated 1B Native Specialist (e.g., atlas.spec.rust_ownership_lifetimes) + Tier 5 Vault + rustc verifier against an 80B generalist baseline and confirm >=100% domain parity. **Historical aspiration only; reinterpret using current measurements and independent validation.**

* AT-07 — 12B = 3T System EPE End-to-End Synthesis. Original proposed test: Execute a cross-cluster enterprise + systems engineering + formal verification task via ATLAS-Supreme-12B + Tier 2/3/4 + Tier 5 and verify full deterministic test passage. **Historical aspiration only; reinterpret using current measurements and independent validation.**

* AT-08 — 4-Stage Scaffolding-to-Sovereignty Graduation. Original proposed test: Run a specialist through Stages 0, 1, 2, and 3; verify automatic eviction of the Qwen/Gemma teacher from RAM upon Stage 3 certification.

* AT-09 — RTX 5080 VRAM Hard-Cap Enforcement. Original proposed test: Stress-test all 5 Hardware Resource Profiles and verify active CUDA allocation never exceeds 15.2 GB (leaving >=800 MB for Windows 11 DWM). **Historical aspiration only; reinterpret using current measurements and independent validation.**

* AT-10 — 96GB DDR5 Warm-Cache Sub-250ms Swap. Original proposed test: Trigger rapid specialist switches across 10 different Tier 4 models cached in pinned DDR5 RAM and verify PCIe 5.0 swap latency <250 ms. **Historical aspiration only; reinterpret using current measurements and independent validation.**

* AT-11 — Volatile Playground Zero-Persistence Proof. Original proposed test: Run a 20-turn sensitive session in Private Playground, close session, and verify zero bytes written to SQLite, LanceDB, logs, or training queues.

* AT-12 — SQLCipher 19-Category Personal Assistant Memory. Original proposed test: Authorize 5 personal facts in Personal Assistant mode, verify routing into exact Markdown category files, and test 1-click Edit and Forget propagation.

* AT-13 — Unified Token Budget Engine (Zero Artificial Caps). Original proposed test: Generate a 12,000-token multi-file code implementation and a 10,000-token story chapter without premature truncation.

* AT-14 — From-Scratch 1B Native Track-A Model Birth. Original proposed test: Initialize a brand-new 1.0B Native ATLAS model from random weights, train a custom 32k BPE tokenizer, run a smoke training cycle, and register the checkpoint.

* AT-15 — Real GPU Training & Live Telemetry Streaming. Original proposed test: Run a QLoRA/GaLore/Native training job on the RTX 5080 and verify real-time WebSocket updates for step loss, grad norm, LR, tok/s, and VRAM MB.

* AT-16 — Three-Mode Training Stop & Safe Pause/Resume. Original proposed test: Test Stop After Current, Pause Safely (verifying exact AdamW state resumption loss continuity), and Emergency Stop (<3.0s VRAM release).

* AT-17 — MinHash LSH + 13-Gram Benchmark Decontamination. Original proposed test: Plant 10 paraphrased benchmark items in a candidate dataset and verify the Decontamination Gate detects and quarantines 100% of them. **Historical aspiration only; reinterpret using current measurements and independent validation.**

* AT-18 — 7-State Qualification Gate & Rollback. Original proposed test: Submit a deliberately degraded checkpoint to the Qualification Gate and verify automatic rejection and zero disruption to the active production specialist.

* AT-19 — 14-Dimension Adaptive Curriculum Escalation. Original proposed test: Achieve >=85% mastery on Difficulty Level 3 for a specialist and verify automatic generation of Level 4 tasks across the 14 curriculum dimensions.

* AT-20 — Unattended Continuous Autonomous Training Cycle. Original proposed test: Enable Continuous Autonomous Mode and verify ATLAS completes at least 2 sequential justified model upgrades unattended with full before/after scorecards.

* AT-21 — Corrective Replanning Mastery (Fixing Commit 7742514 Limitation). Original proposed test: Inject mid-execution tool failures into a 10-step plan and verify ATLAS Master replans and recovers 100% of the time. **Historical aspiration only; reinterpret using current measurements and independent validation.**

* AT-22 — Universal File Intelligence & Deterministic Parsers. Original proposed test: Ingest a mixed archive of Python, C++, PDF, DOCX, XLSX, and SQLite files and verify deterministic symbol graph and table extraction.

* AT-23 — Headless Blender bpy 3D Asset Generation & Verification. Original proposed test: Prompt atlas.master.blender_3d to create a procedural 3D asset, execute headless Blender verification, and confirm manifold geometry and valid glTF export.

* AT-24 — Unreal Engine 5 C++ & Shader Compilation Gate. Original proposed test: Generate a UE5 Gameplay Ability C++ class and HLSL compute shader and verify clean compilation via UnrealHeaderTool and DXC.

* AT-25 — Long-Horizon Story Bible Continuity Gate. Original proposed test: Generate a new chapter against a 50-entity Story Bible with a planted timeline contradiction and verify the Continuity Gate catches and repairs it.

* AT-26 — 14-Step Autonomous Self-Development in Git Worktree. Original proposed test: Have ATLAS identify a codebase bottleneck, generate a patch in an isolated Git worktree, pass pytest, and present a clean diff for promotion.

* AT-27 — 5 Primary Navigation Areas & 13 Technical Workspaces. Original proposed test: Verify 1-click navigation across Home, Models, Training, Chat, and Memory plus all 13 deep workspaces with zero dead buttons.

* AT-28 — Multi-Resolution Responsive Layout (4K to 720p). Original proposed test: Render the desktop UI at 3840x2160, 2560x1440, 1920x1080, and 1280x720 and verify zero text clipping or horizontal scrollbars.

* AT-29 — Complete Offline Local Sovereignty Audit. Original proposed test: Sever all network interfaces and verify 100% functionality across chat, Tier 5 0B execution, model creation, GPU training, and Arena evaluation. **Historical aspiration only; reinterpret using current measurements and independent validation.**

* AT-30 — Storage Explorer & Cryptographic Audit Ledger Verification. Original proposed test: Inspect every storage path in WS-13 and run the SHA-256 hash-chain verifier across the episodic ledger with zero broken links.

# APPENDIX H — ORIGINAL QUANTITATIVE RESEARCH HYPOTHESES (UNPROVEN)

These preserve falsifiable ideas, not numerical performance commitments. An independent study must define task, comparator, hardware, confidence, cost and matched tool access before any such claim can appear in product marketing or qualification.

The following original quantitative predictions are not proven facts. Register them as falsifiable hypotheses with realistic experimental design, independent held-out data, statistical uncertainty and actual hardware telemetry. A failed hypothesis is useful research evidence and must not trigger fabricated success or a forced redesign.

* H-01 — Zero-Model Compilation Compression Ratio. Original proposed experiment/claim: At least 65% of recurring user queries within 30 days of operation can be served in <2 ms with 0.0 GB VRAM via compiled Tier 5 Answer Cards and deterministic scripts.

* H-02 — 1B Native Specialist vs 80B Generalist Parity. Original proposed experiment/claim: A 1.0B–1.5B Native ATLAS model paired with a domain BPE tokenizer, Tier 5 Vault, and deterministic verifier matches or exceeds an unassisted 80B generalist model on narrow domain tasks.

* H-03 — 12B Hierarchical Ecosystem vs 3T Monolith. Original proposed experiment/claim: A 12B Supreme Master orchestrating 20 Cluster Masters, 100 Domain Masters, 2,000+ Specialists, and Tier 5 Vaults achieves system-level task accuracy comparable to a 3-Trillion-parameter frontier monolith.

* H-04 — Procedure-Externalized Weight Efficiency. Original proposed experiment/claim: Externalizing factual records and step-by-step SOPs into Tier 5 files reduces catastrophic forgetting during continual LoRA+/GaLore updates by >70%.

* H-05 — Verifier-Filtered Synthetic Distillation. Original proposed experiment/claim: Filtering Stage 0 teacher trajectories through deterministic compilers/solvers before student SFT yields a >2.5x sample-efficiency gain over unfiltered distillation.

* H-06 — 14-Dimension Curriculum vs Random Task Sampling. Original proposed experiment/claim: Adaptive difficulty progression across the 14 orthogonal curriculum dimensions accelerates specialist graduation to Stage 3 by >40% in GPU-hours.

* H-07 — 96GB DDR5 Warm-Pool Tiered Paging. Original proposed experiment/claim: Maintaining an LRU pool of 30+ quantized 1B–2B specialists in pinned DDR5 system RAM hides model-switching latency below human perception (<250 ms).

* H-08 — Contrastive Hard-Negative DPO from Verifier Failures. Original proposed experiment/claim: Automatically pairing verifier-rejected model outputs with verifier-passed repairs for DPO/GRPO eliminates recurring syntax and schema errors within 2 training cycles.

* H-09 — Grammar-Constrained Routing Reliability. Original proposed experiment/claim: Enforcing byte-level GBNF/JSON-Schema masks on Tier 1–3 routing and planning eliminates malformed delegation DAGs (100.0% schema validity).

* H-10 — Autonomous Self-Development Stability. Original proposed experiment/claim: Gating all autonomous ATLAS code edits behind isolated Git worktrees, full pytest suites, and benchmark non-regression guarantees zero self-inflicted system breakage.

# APPENDIX J — CANONICAL STRUCTURED DATA AND REPRODUCIBILITY SCHEMAS

All schema snippets below are proposed starting points; version them, validate field types and enforce access rights in code. Exact IDs and digests are illustrative.

**`SourceManifestV1`:** `source_id`, `origin_uri_or_project_path`, `original_digest`, `mime`, `license`, `acquired_at`, `last_checked_at`, `access_scope`, `allowed_uses`, `snapshot_path`, `extractor_version`, `trust_state`, `provenance_links`, `retention_policy`.

**`TaskOutcomeV1`:** `task_id`, `request_digest`, `project_scope`, `parent_task_id`, `dependency_digests`, `chosen_actions`, `observed_results`, `exact_evidence`, `verifier_version`, `result_class`, `wrong_delivery`, `false_abstention`, `resource_cost`, `source_family`, `model_origin`, `training_eligibility`, `custody`.

**`ExperimentRecordV1`:** `experiment_id`, `hypothesis`, `baseline`, `candidate`, `pre_registration_digest`, `data_rights`, `compute_budget`, `runs_and_seeds`, `task_outcomes`, `abstentions`, `errors`, `wall_time`, `memory_peak`, `energy_if_measured`, `candidate_sha`, `review`, `decision`, `negative_findings`.

**`CapabilityGrantV1`:** `grant_id`, `user_id`, `project_id`, `operation`, `allowed_paths_or_services`, `permitted_artifact_hashes`, `limits`, `expires_at`, `revoked_at`, `signed_owner_approval`, `host_policy_version`; the model cannot author its own grant.

**`BatchHandoffV1`:** `batch_id`, `current_state`, `writer`, `reviewer`, `accepted_baseline_sha`, `frozen_product_shas`, `protocol_shas`, `evidence_shas`, `dependencies`, `per_update_status`, `known_failures`, `review_scope`, `acceptance_criteria`, `next_writer`, `owner_approval_gates`, `canonical_master_digest`.

**Required engineering invariants:** digests match exact bytes and request identities; statuses have state machines; private and public partitions cannot be mixed; model-origin is immutable checkpoint metadata; evaluator-only fields cannot be supplied from candidate worktrees; source revisions are immutable; import and authorization are separate; actual outcomes are not inferred from model confidence; externally sourced document instructions never become host control instructions; migrations preserve historical records and fail safely.

# APPENDIX M — REINFORCED TRUST-BOUNDARY AND THREAT-CASE REGISTER

The following cases extend the historical safety matrix. These are **planned security tests**, not evidence of present implementation. Each should have a stable ID and a test receipt before the affected authority can be enabled:

| Threat ID | Trust boundary | Required denial or verification | 
| ----- | ----- | ----- | 
| SEC-L01 | Imported Markdown → trusted agent instructions | Embedded permission and role changes are ignored | 
| SEC-L02 | Candidate model → production registry | A model cannot self-approve its checkpoint | 
| SEC-L03 | Learning data → protected evaluator | Shared project/template near-duplicates are detected | 
| SEC-L04 | Source citation → current evidence | Hash, span, source revision and task ID must match | 
| SEC-L05 | Tool proposal → host action | Unauthorized action enum or expanded scope rejected | 
| SEC-L06 | Windows path → archive extraction | Junction, 8.3 alias, ADS, drive-relative, device path and dot-normalization rejected | 
| SEC-L07 | Guest execution → physical host | No ambient host home mounts, credentials, clipboard or unchecked network | 
| SEC-L08 | Network research → private endpoints | Redirect/DNS rebinding and local metadata access blocked | 
| SEC-L09 | Remote registration → personal account | Owner cookies, email, saved password and payment never used | 
| SEC-L10 | Personal memory → training | Absence/revocation of affirmative training grant denies row | 
| SEC-L11 | Project source → cross-project response | Foreign scope or stale snapshot withheld | 
| SEC-L12 | Review receipt → wrong task | All exact request/dependency/project/oracle identifiers bound | 
| SEC-L13 | User revocation → derivative data | Dataset and affected checkpoint lineage marked/retrained as needed | 
| SEC-L14 | Training Director → resource authority | No GPU/disk/network overrun or foreground starvation | 
| SEC-L15 | Source generated patch → repository | Disposable isolation, exact diff, independent review and owner approval | 
| SEC-L16 | Internal model → master constitution | No self-changing safety or qualification rules | 
| SEC-L17 | Model output → verified knowledge | Independently checked claim-level evidence required | 
| SEC-L18 | Cached retrieval → revised source | Scope and revision keyed cache invalidates safely | 
| SEC-L19 | Shutdown/restart → duplicate effect | No unreviewed patch/download/account action replay | 
| SEC-L20 | Optional local model adapter → remote egress | No redirect or cloud proxy absent explicit network grant | 

# APPENDIX N — SCIENTIFIC SCORECARD FOR NATIVE ORIGINAL INTELLIGENCE

The scorecard is reported per model, task family, source/project family and overall system. **Scores are empty until independently measured.** The minimum fields are:

| Field | Definition | 
| ----- | ----- | 
| `native_only_completion_rate` | Verified task completion with all pretrained inference disabled | 
| `native_learned_action_contribution` | Fraction of tasks where a learned choice changed an action from baseline | 
| `verified_completion_delta_vs_host` | Paired difference in accepted task results vs deterministic host | 
| `verified_completion_delta_vs_simple_ml` | Paired difference vs qualified linear/TF-IDF/other simple ML | 
| `incorrect_trusted_delivery_rate` | Incorrect answers allowed through final verification | 
| `safe_abstention_rate` | Supported unknown cases correctly withheld | 
| `false_abstention_rate` | Answerable in-scope cases unnecessarily withheld | 
| `unique_project_families` | Independent project-level groups represented in evaluation | 
| `protected_family_generalization` | Performance on evaluator-controlled held-out families | 
| `total_cost_per_verified_task` | End-to-end CPU/GPU/memory/elapsed-time/energy estimate | 
| `learned_policy_calibration` | Calibration and selective risk, not raw softmax alone | 
| `reproducible_checkpoint_rate` | Matching model artifacts on repeated authorized runs | 
| `successful_procedure_transfer` | Qualified procedures used correctly on unfamiliar applications | 
| `role_ablation_gain` | Measured value of each Master/specialist role | 
| `forgetting_regression_count` | Prior verified competencies lost after refit | 
| `teacher_or_reference_calls` | Actual count of local-pretrained and external-model inference | 
| `data_rights_violations` | Ineligible or revoked rows used for training; required zero | 
| `owner_authorized_effects` | Protected real actions that carry an exact permission receipt | 

A public dashboard must never infer these from navigation names or checkpoint metadata alone; numbers require current receipts with measured test count and status. In particular, a 65-parameter source specialist plus a 37-parameter Master matching a host on 16/20 tasks is an original-model training result, **not** proof of original reasoning superiority.

## O.2 Owner decision register and open research questions

**Already binding and not open for agent reinterpretation:** The primary intelligence must increasingly come from original ATLAS-trained models; facts and tested procedures are stored in verifiable external files; protected actions require host authorization; private material is excluded from general training unless explicitly permitted; the system must never use the owner's personal accounts, identity or money; unsafe computer execution remains disabled until independently qualified; Sol implements major five-update batches and Argon independently reviews the full batch; PLATZ Engine is a separate product; current source evidence outranks historical claims about what is implemented.

**Future decisions that need owner-approved design records, not silent guesses:** How narrowly to define native-only mode when a user explicitly turns on pretrained reference chat; when a learned Master may replace a deterministic advisory decision; the energy/storage budget for opt-in unattended work; which remote isolation provider satisfies research/account requirements; whether the owner wants one shared native language model or separately trained narrow ones after the data foundry; and which creative modalities receive development priority after original reasoning becomes measurably useful.

**Research questions requiring experiments:** How much independent language competence can a modest scratch-trained model acquire from legitimately available data on this workstation? Which procedural representations transfer to unfamiliar projects? When does a learned Master beat rules on end-to-end outcomes under cost constraints? Are many separately trained original models more effective than a shared original foundation with task-specific heads? Can a model identify worthwhile new data without test leakage? Which knowledge artifacts actually improve reasoning rather than mask weak policy decisions? What qualification threshold justifies moving from shadow to owner-approved advisory use? What cost-effective model sizes fit 16 GB VRAM across realistic training and concurrent inference? Can ATLAS reproduce a claimed improvement after restart, corruption, and hidden-family evaluation?

**Master-plan maintenance criterion:** A future agent must identify which new evidence answers one of these questions, update the relevant ADR or scientific hypothesis record, preserve negative results and avoid converting a single favorable experiment into a permanent architectural guarantee.

# APPENDIX P — FAST HUMAN READER'S GUIDE

**What is ATLAS?** An owner-controlled local intelligence and creation system whose long-term primary reasoning comes from ATLAS-original locally trained models. It retrieves versioned facts and tested procedures from explicit files, coordinates qualified specialist work, independently verifies results and learns from permitted real outcomes. The trusted host is above every model and knowledge file.

**What is ATLAS not?** It is not a renamed Qwen chatbot, a list of untrained specialist names, a graphical mock training lab, a model that always wins, a promise of real AGI, or an automatically authorized operator of the owner's Windows computer. A model's parameter count and training loss do not prove useful reasoning. A sourced answer does not authorize execution.

**What does the mature product look like?** Home → choose Ask / Project / Research / Create / Improve Model / Evidence → select scope and allowed data → see a qualified native route or an explicit optional reference mode → inspect results and citations → approve protected actions only when needed → recover prior work, models and knowledge. Advanced Studio offers real, not simulated, training and inspection controls.

**Where is the actual state?** `docs/ai/ATLAS_HANDOFF.md` points to the current frozen batch and reviewer. `docs/ai/ATLAS_OWNER_REPORT.md` reports useful end-to-end features, limitations, evidence and next actions. `docs/ai/ATLAS_REVIEW_PROTOCOL.md` governs acceptance. Live source and tests establish implementation facts. The snapshot in this v3.3 master is historical as of 2026-11-19, not an automatic update.

**What is the next objective?** Complete/verify the outstanding Sol-authored NEXT-015R–NEXT-020 batch, incorporate Argon's independent findings, and pursue NEXT-021 Native Foundry, NEXT-022 original language understanding, NEXT-023 learned multi-step procedures, NEXT-024 functional three-Master cooperation and NEXT-025 independent native self-improvement—without bypassing security or inventing evidence.

**Why the plan is long:** It is a long-term constitution, product catalog, research roadmap and detailed test reference. The AI must use the machine-readable map to find exact obligations; it must not paste the whole file into a small model context unnecessarily. The complete archive holds historical source fidelity separately from current authority.

# APPENDIX Q — AI AGENT STARTUP AND DECISION CHECKLIST

At the beginning of each substantial activation, an external developer or authorized internal ATLAS planning agent MUST:

1. Open the canonical full plan from the repository, check version and SHA-256 against the indexed manifest, and inspect the complete table of contents. If missing or mismatched, stop privileged or architecture-changing work and request a repair of the documentation state.
2. Read the essential doctrine: original-model first, file-first knowledge and procedures, evidence before claim, no money/accounts/private data, trusted host above models, no self-approval, PLATZ separation.
3. Read the current `ATLAS_HANDOFF.md`, `ATLAS_OWNER_REPORT.md`, `ATLAS_REVIEW_PROTOCOL.md` and the exact milestone acceptance criteria. Determine accepted, pending, rejected and blocked revisions from source-backed receipts, not chat prose.
4. Inspect the real worktree/branch/HEAD, toolchain, current tests, protected evaluator separation and parent dependency graph before planning an edit. Do not rewrite another repository or modify the trusted host based on obsolete instructions.
5. Use the chapter index to retrieve every relevant requirement (including linked parent/child parts) and any original historical source detail needed. Avoid claiming to have read uninspected text.
6. Identify an actual task objective and measurable acceptance oracle; preregister baselines and protected-split rules before model tuning. Keep separate implementation, author evidence, independent review and handoff revisions.
7. Check which actions are read-only, which train on data, which use network/model service, which execute code, and which modify source. Require the corresponding independent permission; no implicit escalation.
8. Record real behavior, model lineage, outputs, wrong deliveries, false abstentions, model contribution and resource measurements. A skipped or unavailable Windows test is never a pass.
9. Before ending, reconcile frozen product revisions, dependency acceptance, storage/secret hygiene and canonical handoff; write a short owner summary and a brief prompt for the next independently assigned agent. Do not contact, wait for or monitor another development agent through Computer Use.

**Stop immediately and report** a trusted-host policy bypass, credential exposure, unexpected outgoing network request, unsafe guest fallback, protected-evaluation data leak, unintended destructive change, irreversible private-data retention, or significant unexplained benchmark contamination. Preserve minimum safe evidence without copying secrets into Git or prompts.

# APPENDIX R — CANONICAL TERMS AND EVIDENCE SEMANTICS

| Term | Meaning | Typical invalid substitution |
|---|---|---|
| Original ATLAS model | Weights initialized from scratch, with fully disclosed architecture, tokenizer, corpus and training lineage | Qwen LoRA labeled 'native' |
| Native-only task | No pretrained model calls in entire attributable task path | Deterministic answer labeled as native-model success |
| Reference-assisted | An optional identified pretrained local/external model provided some learned inference | Hidden Ollama fallback |
| Deterministic host | Trusted non-neural code executing verified algorithms/permission checks | Claiming the rule engine has learned |
| Verified source claim | Claim tested for specified source/version/scope by a stated oracle | Search result or model confidence |
| Provisional model | Model trained and tested by author but not independently qualified | Calling checkpoint 'production' |
| Qualified model | Independently accepted for exact task/risk/latency scope, still subject to owner activation | Generalizing to all domains |
| Shadow mode | Learned model observes/proposes but cannot control authoritative delivery | Counting host completions as model completions |
| Independent evaluation | Reviewer-controlled tests with frozen candidate and protected custody | Same author rerunning public tests |
| Model improvement | Verified downstream or justified efficiency advantage on unfamiliar tasks | Lower loss or more parameters |
| Knowledge improvement | Better verified source/procedure/card retrieval without necessarily changing weights | Claiming neural improvement |
| Procedure | Versioned scoped actions, preconditions and tests, not a generic instruction | Copy-paste prompt without validator |
| Master | Measured coordination/research/engineering decision competence | Role name with no qualified behavior |
| Owner permission | Host-enforced, time/scope/operation-bounded grant | LLM text saying 'approved' |
| Accepted baseline | Exact independently reviewed frozen product and dependencies | Latest developer commit |
| Restricted | Not deployable without additional safety gate and approval | Feature button present |

# APPENDIX S — MINIMUM ACCEPTANCE MATRIX FOR NEXT-021 THROUGH NEXT-025

| Milestone | Required owner-visible result | Independent negative cases | Never claim as success |
|---|---|---|---|
| NEXT-021 Data Foundry | Inspect eligible, quarantined and protected data partitions, provenance, permissions and real example count | Duplicates, swapped requests, contaminated families, revoked rights, forged oracle, label-only heuristic | Thousands of rows made from a few tasks |
| NEXT-022 Original language | Run from-scratch text/token model on novel paraphrases with grounded structured answers and original weights | Negation, novel names, combined constraints, unseen composition, out-of-domain abstention | Template lookup or pretrained model answer |
| NEXT-023 Learned procedures | Native policy selects sequences of typed research/engineering actions with bounded stop/replan and verified task result | Stale procedure, contradictory intermediate result, impossible tool, cost trap, infinite loop | Reproducing a fixed flowchart with more model calls |
| NEXT-024 Three Masters | Primary, Research and Engineering make separate measured decisions and improve something under fair ablations | Disagreement, role removal, swapped evidence, single-agent baseline, budget abuse | Three role names or identical routing heads |
| NEXT-025 Self-improvement | Freeze weakness, collect eligible data, train one original candidate, evaluate blind, retain/reject with honest metrics | Benchmark leakage, overfitting, no positive data, catastrophic forgetting, changed scoring | A trial that rejects a refit relabeled as model improvement |

For each milestone the reviewer also checks clean build/test/package on the actual supported Windows environment, intact private evaluation custody, no unapproved network or user account use, exact Git revisions, resource use, cancellation/restart, model-only vs full-system attribution and regression scope. A whole batch cannot be accepted if a required parent repair remains rejected. If current data cannot support claimed independent gains, the correct result is a useful tested Foundry/experiment that explicitly leaves the intelligence claim unproven.

The suggested paths are installation targets, not claims that the live checkout already contains them. Sol must inspect repository conventions, stage necessary additions without destructive overwrites, and report any incompatible current architecture.

# APPENDIX U — OWNER DECISIONS, PRIORITIES AND OPEN GATES

**Already binding:** ATLAS-original intelligence is the long-term center; pretrained inference is optional/reference only, never a hidden primary brain; trusted host and owner control all protected actions; knowledge and verified procedures live outside weights; only verified research is promoted; personal accounts, money, identity, subscriptions, credentials and unauthorized private data are forbidden for autonomous work; ATLAS-created online accounts must never be logged into the physical host; untrusted code requires separately qualified isolation; negative results are preserved; Sol builds substantial batches and Argon independently reviews them; PLATZ Engine and legacy ATLAS/FORGE remain separate and protected; model role catalogs are research proposals until proven.

**Open for an actual owner decision:** exact resource/thermal/idle-time training budget; whether future offline local reference models can auto-start versus requiring a per-session opt-in; definition of eligible data sources for a specific personal/private project; selected remote isolation/provider and network grants; whether/when native Master decisions can become authoritative; owner-facing content-sharing/export defaults; advanced UI preference; release signing key ownership; priority order among eventual creative/game workloads. Agents may propose sensible defaults but cannot silently grant themselves permissions or use money/accounts to satisfy an open item.

**Current critical scientific unknowns:** Whether a from-scratch language model can learn task-relevant compositional understanding from available authorized data on this workstation; whether original learned procedures transfer to new project families; whether the original three-Master team provides a causal utility win over deterministic tools and simple baselines; whether a new training cycle can produce an improvement that survives protected independent evaluation; whether benefits persist across changed tools, data distributions and constrained resource budgets.

# APPENDIX V — PRODUCT FAILURES, INCIDENTS AND ZERO-TRUST STOP RULES

A severe unexpected event takes priority over the five-update cadence. Evidence that must trigger an immediate protected-work stop includes: personal credentials or private files exposed to an agent, new network egress without a grant, shell execution of model-generated code on the host, unsafe VM fallback, attempt to alter protected evaluator files, unexplained benchmark contamination, destructive filesystem edits, detected dataset rights violation, unbounded GPU/disk work after owner cancellation, hidden reference-model invocation in native-only mode, and an accepted status assigned to an unreviewed revision.

**Containment sequence:** revoke capability; safely stop pending operation using host enforcement; preserve minimal nonsecret audit facts; quarantine affected artifacts and checkpoints; restore known accepted baseline if needed; create a bounded reproduction in a disposable safe environment; notify owner; independent root-cause review; modify code only in separately committed repair; retest negative/adversarial cases; seek new acceptance before protected reactivation. This is a goal for future ATLAS-native incident tooling and an immediate requirement for the external developer process whenever the corresponding risk appears.

**Nonfatal defects**—inaccurate presentation labels, noncritical UI glitches, unsupported but clearly withheld syntax, honest model underperformance—should be prioritized according to user impact and dependencies, not used to stall all unrelated read-only work. Never paper over a severe error by relabeling it as an abstention. The owner report must distinguish correct withholding from false withholding, wrong trusted delivery, test infrastructure failure and reviewer disagreement.

# APPENDIX W — CHANGELOG, ARCHIVE AND COMPLETENESS BOUNDARY

**v3.3 (2026-11-19):** Reorganized the full plan so Parts I–XXIII precede Appendices A–W; preserved the complete uploaded v3.2 bytes in the archive; retained every previous v3.2 numbered chapter (1–166) and appendix text; added Chapters 167–192 expanding owner experience, model/data provenance, coding/research, learning, three-Master cooperation, secure automation, delivery, AI navigation and post-025 goals; added Appendices P–W for fast human reading, AI startup, terminology, milestone acceptance, documentation map, owner decisions and incident response; generated a machine-readable chapter index and machine-testable integrity map. The updated source manifest distinguishes original supplied source bytes, extracted transcripts, v3.1 archived master, v3.2 input and v3.3 active master.

**Sources:** The accompanying full transfer ZIP includes the available eighteen original historical source files and their extracted transcripts from the prior source-audited archive, plus the exact uploaded v3.2 master. Historical documents often contain obsolete model names, stale commits, exaggerated parameter-equivalence claims or outdated commands; those details remain retrievable but nonbinding. Source ZIPs mentioned by those documents but not supplied cannot be reconstructed. No agent should claim perfect historical completeness beyond the bytes actually available. New owner directions can amend v3.3 only through a versioned, reviewable change rather than model-written implicit overrides.

**Integrity:** Package manifests contain SHA-256 hashes of every file. The canonical master is verified against its own generated section index; documents and package content are checked for nonempty data and intended content. A hash verifies identity, not correctness or independent review. Word conversion/rendering validates readability but does not make planned technical capabilities operational.
